=== NOTES 5:0 | Nature, Scope and Characteristics of Science and Technology
## SUB 0 | Branches of Science and the "-ologies"
[0:0:0] # Kinds of science
[0:0:1] TABLE head: Type | Fields
  [0:0:1.0] Natural | Physics, chemistry, biology, earth science, space science
  [0:0:1.1] Formal | Mathematics, logic, statistics
  [0:0:1.2] Applied | Engineering, medicine, agriculture
[0:0:2] - **Interdisciplinary:** **biotechnology** = biology + engineering; **geoinformatics** (geospatial) = geography + **information technology** (GIS, remote sensing, GPS); **data science** = statistics + computing; **cyber-physical systems** = computing + physical systems via sensors.
[0:0:3] - **Genetic engineering** (rDNA technology) is a branch of **biology** (biotechnology), not of electronics or civil/mechanical engineering (APRO 2008).
[0:0:4] - **ESSO** (Earth System Science Organisation, **2007**) works under the **MoES**: ocean, atmospheric and climate, geoscience, polar and cryosphere science.
[0:0:5] # Study of … (most-asked angle)
[0:0:6] TABLE head: Term | Study of
  [0:0:6.0] Ethology | Animal behaviour (Lorenz, Tinbergen, von Frisch – Nobel 1973)
  [0:0:6.1] Etymology | Origin and history of words (etymological dictionary = history and origin of words)
  [0:0:6.2] Entomology | Insects (UK UDA/LDA 2003 ×2)
  [0:0:6.3] Ichthyology / Malacology / Parasitology | Fish / molluscs / parasites
  [0:0:6.4] Rheology | Flow and deformation of matter – soft solids and flowing matter
  [0:0:6.5] Axiology | Nature of values (ethics, aesthetics)
  [0:0:6.6] Environmental psychology | Interaction between the physical world and human behaviour
  [0:0:6.7] Biometrics | Identity from physical / behavioural traits (see Technology Terms)
  [0:0:6.8] Euphenics | Improving the phenotype
  [0:0:6.9] Biocenosis | A community of interacting organisms
  [0:0:6.10] Astrobiology | Origin and possibility of life in the universe (not bacteria – that is bacteriology)
  [0:0:6.11] Anatomy | Identification and description of body structures
  [0:0:6.12] Biotechnology | Use of living cells to make or modify products (e.g. GM foods)
[0:0:7] - **Palaeogeography:** physical geography of past geological ages; it is part of **historical geography** (exam key: "historical geography"). **Palaeography** = ancient writing; biogeography = distribution of living things.
[0:0:8] - **Apograph:** a **copy or transcript of a manuscript** (the original = autograph).
[0:0:9] > ⚠ Trap: ethology (behaviour) vs etymology (words) vs entomology (insects); astrobiology is not "study of bacteria" (EO Gr-III 2021: only B and C correct).
[0:0:10] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:0 sub 3; OnlyIAS NCERT Human Geography p.8; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palaeogeography; general knowledge (rheology, axiology, apograph)
## SUB 1 | Psychology – Methods and Theories of Behaviour
[0:1:0] # Methods of studying behaviour
[0:1:1] - **Objective:** **observation**, experiment, tests.
[0:1:2] - **Subjective:** **introspection**, interview, dream analysis (depend on the person's own report).
[0:1:3] - **Simplest form of cognition: sensation** → perception → concept.
[0:1:4] # Theories
[0:1:5] TABLE head: Theory | Proponent
  [0:1:5.0] Anarchic (multifactor) theory of intelligence | Thorndike
  [0:1:5.1] Two-factor theory | Spearman
  [0:1:5.2] Achievement motivation | McClelland (with Atkinson)
  [0:1:5.3] Need hierarchy | Maslow – peak experience at self-actualisation
[0:1:6] > Statement angle: Monarchic / unitary theory = intelligence as one power – not Thorndike.
[0:1:7] ~~ Sources: general knowledge, not checked (HWO Gr-II and Welfare Officer papers)
[0:1:8] - **Freud – superego:** printed key / most teacher-education guides: develops in the **latency period**; strictly Freud has it form at the end of the **phallic stage** with the resolution of the Oedipus complex (Britannica).
[0:1:9] - **Rote memory:** material learnt **without understanding** and reproduced mechanically (vs logical / meaningful memory).
## SUB 2 | Pedagogy – Curriculum, Instruction, Teaching Aids and Measurement
[0:2:0] # Models and methods of instruction
[0:2:1] - **Carroll's Model of School Learning** (John B. Carroll, 1963): degree of learning = f(time actually spent ÷ time needed); six elements – aptitude, ability to understand instruction, quality of instruction, opportunity to learn, perseverance and the degree of learning; HWO key: **1 output, 1 input, 4 intermediate variables**.
[0:2:2] - **Personalized System of Instruction (PSI, "Keller Plan")**: **Fred S. Keller** (1968) – self-paced units, mastery before moving on, proctors; not Froebel (kindergarten) or Pestalozzi.
[0:2:3] - **Criticism of the subject-centred traditional curriculum:** **A.N. Whitehead** (The Aims of Education, 1929) attacked "inert ideas" and the fatal disconnection of subjects (HWO key); John Dewey (The Child and the Curriculum, 1902) is the other name books link with child-centred, activity curriculum.
[0:2:4] # Classroom interaction and teaching aids
[0:2:5] - **Flanders' Interaction Analysis Categories (FIAC):** **10 categories** – 7 teacher talk (4 indirect, 3 direct), 2 pupil talk, 1 silence/confusion.
[0:2:6] - **Epidiascope:** projects **both opaque and transparent** objects (episcope = opaque only; diascope / slide projector = transparencies only).
[0:2:7] # Measurement scales (S.S. Stevens, 1946)
[0:2:8] TABLE head: Scale | Feature | Example
  [0:2:8.0] Nominal | Names / categories only | Gender, roll number
  [0:2:8.1] Ordinal | Rank order | Class rank
  [0:2:8.2] Interval | Equal intervals, no true zero | Temperature °C, IQ
  [0:2:8.3] Ratio | Equal intervals and a true zero | Height, weight, age
[0:2:9] ~~ Sources: Carroll, "A Model of School Learning", Teachers College Record 64 (1963); Keller, "Good-bye, teacher…", JABA 1968; Flanders, Analyzing Teaching Behavior (1970); Whitehead, The Aims of Education (1929); Stevens, Science 103 (1946); HWO Gr-II papers (printed keys)
## SUB 3 | Observatories and Scientific Institutions
[0:3:0] TABLE head: Institution | Location / field
  [0:3:0.0] Radio Astronomy Centre (Ooty Radio Telescope) | Muthorai, Ooty, TN; NCRA-TIFR (1969)
  [0:3:0.1] GMRT | Khodad, ~80 km from Pune; world's largest metre-wave telescope (30 dishes)
  [0:3:0.2] BARC | Trombay, Mumbai
  [0:3:0.3] PRL | Ahmedabad; runs Udaipur Solar Observatory
  [0:3:0.4] IIA | Bengaluru; Kodaikanal, Kavalur, Gauribidanur
  [0:3:0.5] ISM | Dhanbad
  [0:3:0.6] Indian Astronomical Observatory | Hanle, Ladakh (~4,500 m); India's first dark sky reserve
[0:3:1] - **Hanle:** billed in 2002 as the **world's highest** ground-based telescope site (IAS 2002 key: **India**); ⚠ now the **University of Tokyo Atacama Observatory, Chile (5,640 m, May 2024)** is the highest.
[0:3:2] - **Jantar Mantar (Sawai Jai Singh II):** Jaipur, **Delhi, Ujjain, Mathura, Varanasi** – **not Allahabad**.
[0:3:3] - **Antarctic stations:** Dakshin Gangotri (1983) → Maitri (1989) → **Bharati (2012)** = third; Maitri and Bharati are operational.
[0:3:4] - **ISM Dhanbad:** opened **9 December 1926** as the **Indian School of Mines** (modelled on the Royal School of Mines, London) for **mining and applied geology**; became **IIT (ISM)** in **2016**.
[0:3:5] > ⚠ Trap: GMRT is near Pune, not Ooty.
[0:3:6] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:0 sub 7; PMF Art & Culture 6 p.70; PMF World Geography 4 p.50; Praveen CA360 Apr 2026 p.70; physicsworld.com (TAO)
[0:3:7] - **ARIES** (Aryabhatta Research Institute of Observational Sciences, Nainital; autonomous under **DST**) runs the **Devasthal Observatory**: 3.6 m DOT, 1.3 m DFOT and the **International Liquid Mirror Telescope (ILMT, 4 m, first light 2022 – India's first liquid-mirror telescope)** (PMF LENS Jan 2026 Part 2 p.16; aries.res.in).
## SUB 4 | Technology Terms
[0:4:0] TABLE head: Term | Meaning
  [0:4:0.0] Digital embossing | Raised touch printing; no plates, chemicals or solvents, no pollutants; braille maps; introduced in India by NATMO (attached office of DST), Jan 2022 (APPSC G1 2022: I, II and III)
  [0:4:0.1] Biometrics | Identification by unique physical (fingerprint, iris, face) or behavioural (voice, gait, signature) traits
  [0:4:0.2] Cyber (cyborg) insects | Live insects with micro-systems implanted in their bodies to steer them and carry sensors (DARPA HI-MEMS)
  [0:4:0.3] 3D printing | Additive manufacturing, layer by layer from a digital model; uses: confectionery, bionic ears, automotive, reconstructive surgery
  [0:4:0.4] RFID | Radio waves, no line of sight; FASTag = passive RFID (powered by toll reader)
  [0:4:0.5] NFC | 13.56 MHz, ~4 cm; metro smart card = passive NFC
  [0:4:0.6] ICOLC | International Coalition of Library Consortia
[0:4:1] > ⚠ Trap: cyber insects are not software viruses or "explosive insects" (AEE 2011 printed key wrong).
[0:4:2] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:0 sub 5; Tribune 10 Jan 2022 (NATMO); jyi.org (insect cyborg sentinels); NextGen S&T G2 Part 3 p.13 (biometrics)
## SUB 5 | Scientific Method and Research Methods
[0:5:0] # Steps of the scientific method
[0:5:1] - **Order:** **observation → question → hypothesis → experiment → analysis → conclusion**, then replication / peer review.
[0:5:2] - **Modern method of acquiring knowledge = the scientific method**; authority, expert opinion and personal experience are traditional sources (HWO).
[0:5:3] - **Theory** = well-tested explanation of **why**; **law** = what always happens, often mathematical (Newton's laws).
[0:5:4] - **⚠ Trap:** a theory does **not** "become" a law.
[0:5:5] # Research designs
[0:5:6] TABLE head: Design | Meaning
  [0:5:6.0] Pilot study | Small study preceding the main study to check its viability
  [0:5:6.1] Action research | Practitioners (teachers) study their own classroom problems to improve practice – Stephen Corey (1953)
  [0:5:6.2] Field experiment | Hypothesis tested in a natural setting
  [0:5:6.3] Clinical / case study | In-depth study of one individual
[0:5:7] > ⚠ Trap: "studying a classroom problem" = **action research**, not field experiment.
[0:5:8] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:0 sub 2; structural-learning.com (action research, Corey 1953); general knowledge (pilot study)
## SUB 6 | Scientific Temper, TRL and Indices
[0:6:0] TABLE head: Year | Event
  [0:6:0.0] 1962 | Kerala Sastra Sahitya Parishad
  [0:6:0.1] 1976 | Article 51A(h), 42nd Amendment: scientific temper, humanism, spirit of inquiry and reform
  [0:6:0.2] 1981 | Statement on Scientific Temper
  [0:6:0.3] 2001 / 2007 | DST Nano Science and Technology Initiative / Nano Mission
  [0:6:0.4] 2013 | STI Policy (first objective: spread scientific temper); Maharashtra Anti-Superstition Act
[0:6:1] - **Nehru** popularised "scientific temper" (The Discovery of India). **National Science Day: 28 Feb**.
[0:6:2] - **INSPIRE** (DST): all young students, no competitive exam. **Vigyan Jyoti**: **girls only**, Classes 9-12, STEM.
[0:6:3] - **Ranks:** nanoscience publications **5th → 3rd**; **GII 2025: 38th**; **NRI 2024: 49th** (79th in 2019).
[0:6:4] TABLE head: TRL (NASA, 1-9) | Stage
  [0:6:4.0] 1-3 | Basic principles to lab proof of concept
  [0:6:4.1] 4-6 | Validation, prototype
  [0:6:4.2] 7-9 | Operational demo, proven in actual use
[0:6:5] - **ANRF / RDI Scheme:** funds projects at **TRL 4 and above**; strategic technologies; **Deep-Tech Fund of Funds**.
[0:6:6] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:0 sub 4, 6
[0:6:7] - **UN International Years of science:** 2005 Physics, 2009 Astronomy, 2010 Biodiversity, **2011 Chemistry** (and Forests), 2015 Light, 2019 Periodic Table, 2025 Quantum Science and Technology (un.org).
## SUB 7 | Science vs Technology; Characteristics of Science
[0:7:0] # Science vs technology
[0:7:1] TABLE head: Basis | Science | Technology
  [0:7:1.0] Nature | Know-why | Know-how
  [0:7:1.1] Output | Laws, theories | Products, processes
[0:7:2] # Research and innovation
[0:7:3] TABLE head: Type | Meaning
  [0:7:3.0] Basic research | Curiosity-driven
  [0:7:3.1] Applied research | Solves a set problem
  [0:7:3.2] Translational | Takes lab results to use
  [0:7:3.3] Invention | First creation of a product or process
  [0:7:3.4] Innovation | Makes the invention useful in market or society
[0:7:4] - **Science:** **empirical, testable (falsifiable), reproducible, systematic, self-correcting**.
[0:7:5] - **Pseudoscience:** claims scientific status but **cannot be tested**.
[0:7:6] - **J.V. Narlikar:** **astrology** is not a science (UPPSC 2018); **astronomy** (celestial bodies), **cosmology** and **nanotechnology (1-100 nm)** are.
[0:7:7] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:0 sub 0-1

=== OWN MCQs of 5:0 (54)
[n0c5a291309b] sub0
  Which one of the following groups contains only branches classified as natural sciences?
  Agriculture, medicine, logic, statistics and geography | *Physics, chemistry, biology, earth science and space science | Mathematics, logic, statistics, engineering and medicine | Engineering, mathematics, computing, medicine and agriculture
  x: Natural sciences include physics, chemistry, biology, earth science and space science. Mathematics, logic and statistics are formal sciences, while engineering, medicine and agriculture are applied fields.
[nc34c5541eb6] sub0
  Which of the following fields is INCORRECTLY matched with the disciplines it combines?
  Biotechnology – Biology and engineering | *Data science – Biology and engineering | Cyber-physical systems – Computing and physical systems through sensors | Geoinformatics – Geography and information technology
  x: Data science combines statistics and computing; biology and engineering make up biotechnology.
[f6d9e24167a17] sub0
  Among which sciences is statistics classified?
  Natural sciences | Applied sciences | *Formal sciences | Earth sciences
  x: Formal sciences are mathematics, logic and statistics.
[fda513e613c96] sub0
  Geoinformatics combines geography with which field?
  *Information technology | Biology | Engineering | Medicine
  x: Geoinformatics is geography plus IT, using GIS, remote sensing and GPS.
[f0d731232e37b] sub0
  Under which ministry does the Earth System Science Organisation (ESSO), set up in 2007, work?
  DST | DBT | *MoES | DSIR
  x: ESSO (2007) works under the Ministry of Earth Sciences for ocean, atmospheric, geoscience and polar science.

  x: Entomology is the study of insects.

  x: Konrad Lorenz, Niko Tinbergen and Karl von Frisch shared the 1973 Nobel for ethology, the study of animal behaviour.

  1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | *1 and 3 only | 1, 2 and 3
  x: RFID uses radio waves and needs no line of sight; FASTag is a passive RFID tag powered by the toll reader.
[nc97337b2e99] sub5
  In the scientific method, which stage comes immediately after framing the question?
  *Hypothesis | Experiment | Observation | Analysis
  x: The sequence is observation, question, hypothesis, experiment, analysis and conclusion.
[nde2336d4263] sub5
  What is a well-tested scientific explanation of why something happens called?
  Hypothesis | Law | *Theory | Observation
  x: A theory explains why something happens, while a law describes what happens, often mathematically; a theory does not become a law.
[f82656d1c0ee3] sub5
  In the scientific method, which step comes immediately after experiment?
  Hypothesis | Observation | *Analysis | Conclusion
  x: The order is observation, question, hypothesis, experiment, analysis, conclusion, then replication and peer review.
[fec44989a6f8e] sub5
  Which of the following is NOT correct about the scientific method?
  It begins with observation | A hypothesis is framed before the experiment | *A well-tested theory finally becomes a law | Replication and peer review follow the conclusion
  x: A theory explains why and a law describes what happens; a theory does not become a law.
[n101cb775070] sub6
  Which stage of Technology Readiness Levels is correctly matched?
  *TRL 1–3 — basic principles to laboratory proof of concept | TRL 7–9 — only theoretical principles without prototypes | TRL 4–6 — proven in actual operational use only | TRL 1–3 — commercial market deployment
  x: TRL 1–3 cover basic principles through laboratory proof of concept. TRL 4–6 involve validation and prototypes, while TRL 7–9 concern operational demonstration and proven actual use.

  What is research that solves a set problem called?
  Basic research | *Applied research | Translational research | Curiosity research
  x: Applied research solves a set problem; basic research is curiosity-driven and translational research takes lab results to use.
[ff5b90f550da0] sub7
  Consider the following statements about science and technology:
1. The output of science is laws and theories.
2. The output of technology is products and processes.
3. Translational research is curiosity-driven.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  *1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | 1 and 3 only | 1, 2 and 3
  x: Science yields laws and theories and technology yields products and processes; curiosity-driven research is basic research, while translational research takes lab results to use.
[ncaac9e9d39f] sub7
  What is a claim that asserts scientific status without being testable called?
  A hypothesis | A theory | *Pseudoscience | A scientific law
  x: Pseudoscience claims scientific status while lacking testability; science is systematic, self-correcting and reproducible.
[n138b88cdca6] sub7
  According to astrophysicist J.V. Narlikar, which one of the following has NOT been considered a science?
  Astronomy | Cosmology | *Astrology | Nanotechnology
  x: Astrology claims scientific status but cannot be tested, so it is not considered a science. Astronomy (study of celestial bodies), cosmology (origin and evolution of the universe) and nanotechnology (1-100 nm scale) are all considered sciences.
[f968bb51c5b91] sub7
  In which size range does nanotechnology deal with structures?
  0.1-1 nm | *1-100 nm | 100-1,000 nm | 1-100 micrometres
  x: Nanotechnology works at 1-100 nanometres.
[g77784027f040] sub7
  Which of the following is NOT a characteristic of science?
  It is empirical | *It rests on claims that cannot be tested | It is reproducible | It is self-correcting
  x: Science is empirical, testable, reproducible and self-correcting; untestable claims are pseudoscience.
[g23a2ad0d9b69] sub7
  What is astronomy the study of?
  *Celestial bodies | Origin of words | Insects | Animal behaviour
  x: Astronomy studies celestial bodies and is a science, unlike astrology.

=== NOTES 5:1 | Relevance of Science and Technology to Day-to-day Life
## SUB 0 | Household Appliances, Instruments and Everyday Physics
[1:0:0] TABLE head: Appliance | Principle
  [1:0:0.0] Microwave oven | Magnetron; ~2.45 GHz rotates water molecules
  [1:0:0.1] Refrigerator | Vapour compression: refrigerant absorbs heat while evaporating inside, releases it at the condenser outside
  [1:0:0.2] Induction cooktop | Electromagnetic induction, eddy currents
  [1:0:0.3] RO purifier | Pressure on the concentrated side, against osmosis; desalination
  [1:0:0.4] Pressure cooker | Higher pressure raises boiling point (~120 °C)
  [1:0:0.5] Fuse | Heating effect; tin-lead; in series; low melting point, high resistivity
[1:0:1] - **Osmosis:** water moves through a semi-permeable membrane from low to high solute concentration (roots absorb water); **reverse osmosis** removes ions and bacteria.
[1:0:2] - **Electromagnetic induction:** generators, transformers (**AC only**), induction cooktops, **wireless charging**, **dynamic microphone**.
[1:0:3] # Instruments and units
[1:0:4] - **Invar tape** (nickel-steel, tiny expansion) – precise **linear measurement** in surveying; **lactometer** – milk; **hygrometer** – **humidity**; **caesium atomic clock** – most exact time.
[1:0:5] - **Units:** decibel = **sound level**; light year = **distance**; 1 horsepower = **746 W**; resistivity – **ohm-metre**; conductance – siemens, older **mho**; conductivity – **S/m**; e = **1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C**; conventional current flows **positive to negative**.
[1:0:6] # Optics, mechanics, heat
[1:0:7] - **Convex lens:** object **within F** → magnifying glass; real images in **camera, compound microscope, telescope**; **burning glass converges** sun rays. **Myopia:** image in front of the retina; **concave lens (negative power)**.
[1:0:8] - **Pascal's** principle – **hydraulic lift**; **momentum** conservation – **gun recoil, rocket propulsion**; **angular momentum** – ice-skater spins faster; **Doppler** – **red shift**; **Huygens'** – wavefronts, reflection, refraction; **equivalence principle** – **general relativity**, GPS corrections.
[1:0:9] - Mass measures **inertia**; doubling momentum makes KE ×4 (**+300%**); fan under an AC lamp seems to reverse (**stroboscopic** effect); bird on one live wire is safe (**no potential difference**); unmagnetised iron is attracted by **either pole**.
[1:0:10] - **Plasma** = 4th state of matter; inhaled air ~**21% O2**; **Cavendish** (1766) – hydrogen "inflammable air"; **silicon** – 2nd most abundant crustal element; oxidation = **loss of electrons**; **alkaloids** contain **nitrogen**.
[1:0:11] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Chemistry p.50; app notes 5:1 (sub 6, 10)
[1:0:12] - **Pitch** of sound depends on **frequency**.
[1:0:13] - **Resistivity** depends on **material and temperature**, not on length or area (resistance does); unit ohm-metre.
[1:0:14] - **X-ray tube:** only ~1% of the electrons' energy becomes X-rays; the rest is **heat at the target** (tungsten anode, cooled).
[1:0:15] - **Electrical energy** = **VIt**; **Joule's law of heating H = I²Rt**; Kirchhoff's first (junction) law: current in = current out; Fleming's left-hand rule – **middle finger = current**, forefinger = field, thumb = force.
[1:0:16] - **Human eye:** the lens (just behind the pupil) forms a **real, inverted** image on the **retina** (= the film of a camera); **ciliary muscles** of the ciliary body change the lens focal length (~**2.5 cm**) – accommodation; the **eustachian tube** (ear) equalises pressure on both sides of the eardrum.
[1:0:17] - **Magnetic meridian** = **vertical plane** through a freely suspended magnet's axis; a current-carrying wire makes a magnetic field (deflects a compass – Oersted).
[1:0:18] - **Speed of sound:** solids > liquids > gases; ~**330–343 m/s in air**, ~**1500 m/s in water**, ~5000–6400 m/s in metals (aluminium > glass); **rises with temperature** and humidity, independent of pressure.
[1:0:19] - **Speed of light v = c/n:** lower refractive index → faster (quartz 1.46 faster than sapphire 1.77).
[1:0:20] - **Centre of curvature** = centre of the hollow sphere of which the spherical mirror is a part (R = 2f); **pole** = midpoint of the mirror.
[1:0:21] - **Henry** = SI unit of **inductance** (farad – capacitance, ohm – resistance).
[1:0:22] - **Quality (timbre)** of a note depends on its **harmonics / waveform**; pitch ← frequency, loudness ← amplitude.
[1:0:23] - **Audible range = 20 Hz – 20,000 Hz**; below 20 Hz **infrasound** (earthquakes, elephants, whales), above 20 kHz **ultrasound** (bats, dolphins).
[1:0:24] - **Plane mirror:** radius of curvature and focal length = **infinity**; image virtual, same size, laterally inverted.
[1:0:25] - **Relative density** (density/density of water) is a ratio – **no unit**.
[1:0:26] - **Mass** is the **same everywhere** (amount of matter, measure of inertia); **weight** varies with place (zero at Earth's centre, 1/6 on the Moon).
[1:0:27] - **Dentist's mirror** = **concave** mirror (enlarged image of teeth).
[1:0:28] - **Stethoscope**, megaphone, ear trumpet, sound boards = **multiple reflection of sound**.
[1:0:29] - **EM induction** = current induced in a coil by **relative motion of magnet and coil** (changing flux) – Faraday.
[1:0:30] - **Eye defects:** **presbyopia** (age, cannot read near print) and **hypermetropia** – **convex** lens; myopia – concave; **glaucoma** – progressive optic-nerve damage; cataract – lens clouding; colour blindness – mostly **red–green** (red looks greenish).
[1:0:31] - **Motion:** free fall = **uniformly accelerated** (g); unequal distances in equal times = **non-uniform** motion; object at rest → distance–time graph **horizontal**; maximum range of a projectile at **45°**.
[1:0:32] - **Electron emission:** by light – **photoelectric** (Hertz 1887, Einstein); by heat – thermionic; by strong field – field emission.
[1:0:33] - **Boiling point of water:** 100 °C = **373 K** = 212 °F; **falls with altitude** (lower pressure); **rises** with impurities/dissolved solute and with pressure (cooker).
[1:0:34] - **Phonons** = quanta of vibration – **'sound particles'**.
[1:0:35] - **Heat transfer:** **conduction, convection, radiation** (evaporation is a phase change, not a mode).
[1:0:36] - **SI units:** force – **newton**; pressure – pascal; power – watt; energy – joule.
[1:0:37] - **Energy units:** **joule (SI)**, calorie (1 cal = 4.18 J), kWh, eV; pascal – pressure; horsepower – power.
[1:0:38] - **Slip-ring induction motor:** started with **full external rotor resistance**; adding rotor resistance **raises starting torque** and lowers starting current.
[1:0:39] - **Speed of sound:** air at **0 °C ≈ 331 m s⁻¹** (≈ 343 at 20 °C); faster in liquids and solids; rises with temperature.
[1:0:40] - **Photon energy E = hf:** X-rays > light > microwaves > radio waves.
[1:0:41] - **Lens power** P = 1/f (m), unit **dioptre**; convex +, concave −.
[1:0:42] - **Temperature:** K = °C + **273(.15)**; **absolute zero = 0 K = −273.15 °C** (≈ −273 °C); °F = 9/5 °C + 32 (water boils at **212 °F**).
[1:0:43] - **Candela** = unit of **luminous intensity** (light), not sound; sound terms: hertz, decibel, Mach.
[1:0:44] - **Thermos flask:** **vacuum** between double glass walls stops conduction and convection; **silvered walls** reduce radiation; cork supports are poor conductors.
[1:0:45] - **Sound** = **longitudinal** mechanical wave (compressions + rarefactions); needs a medium, **cannot travel in vacuum**, **cannot be polarised**.
[1:0:46] - **Second law F = ma:** for a given force, **acceleration ∝ 1/mass**.
[1:0:47] - **Water densest at 4 °C:** a freezing lake stays at **4 °C at the bottom** (anomalous expansion).
[1:0:48] - **Ultrasonic waves = frequency greater than 20 kHz**.
[1:0:49] - **Graphs of motion:** slope of a **distance–time** graph = **speed**; slope of a **velocity–time** graph = **acceleration**; area under a v–t graph = distance.
[1:0:50] - **Floating body taken to the Moon** floats **as before** – weight and buoyancy both scale with g.
[1:0:51] - **Car headlight** = **concave** reflector, bulb at the focus → parallel beam.
[1:0:52] - **Absolute zero = 0 K = −273.15 °C** – molecular motion would cease.
[1:0:53] - **Sitting near a fan:** moving air **evaporates sweat**, taking latent heat from the skin.
[1:0:54] - **Superconductors:** **zero resistance** below a critical temperature (BCS theory: Bardeen, Cooper, Schrieffer).
[1:0:55] - **Law of reflection:** **angle of incidence = angle of reflection**; incident ray, reflected ray and normal lie in one plane.
[1:0:56] - **Cutting a bar magnet** (across its length) → two complete magnets; **pole strength stays the same**, magnetic moment halves; poles cannot be isolated.
[1:0:57] - **Series resistors:** Rs = R1 + R2 + … – net resistance **increases**; the same current flows through all.
[1:0:58] - **Hydroelectricity** = potential → kinetic → electrical energy (**conservation of energy**).
[1:0:59] - **Microwaves:** ~**300 MHz–300 GHz** (1 mm–1 m) – radar, ovens (2.45 GHz), satellite links; radio below, infrared above (300 GHz–400 THz).
[1:0:60] - **Ice cools a drink** by taking **latent heat of fusion** (336 J/g) from it while melting.
[1:0:61] - **Conductivity = 1/resistivity**, so their product is **1 for every conductor**.
[1:0:62] - **Ice build-up** in a freezer insulates the coils (ice is a poor conductor) – hence defrosting.
[1:0:63] - **Visible spectrum:** **violet** shortest wavelength, highest energy; **red** longest wavelength, **lowest energy** (E = hc/λ).
[1:0:64] - **Covering part of a lens** (strips, half) still gives the **complete image**, only **less bright**.
[1:0:65] - **Metric horsepower = 735.5 W ≈ 736 W**; British (mechanical) hp = **746 W**; **1 calorie = 4.186 J**, so **1 J ≈ 0.24 cal**.
[1:0:66] - **Moment of inertia** depends on mass, its **distribution** and the **axis** – **not on angular velocity**.
[1:0:67] - **Sitar / stretched string:** plucking sets up **stationary transverse** waves.
[1:0:68] - **Concave mirror** = reflecting surface **curved inwards**; **convex mirror** = reflecting surface bulging outwards.
[1:0:69] - **Electrical → light:** LED, laser, TV, bulb; a **candle** turns **chemical energy** into light and heat.
[1:0:70] - **No atmosphere → black sky** (no scattering; Moon, space); blue sky = Rayleigh scattering.
[1:0:71] - **Rest mass:** electron < proton < neutron < alpha particle (He nucleus).
[1:0:72] - **Steel more elastic than rubber:** needs a **larger deforming force** for the same strain (higher Young's modulus).
[1:0:73] - **Soap-bubble / oil-film colours** = **thin-film interference** of reflected light.
[1:0:74] - **Atmospheric refraction:** Sun seen **~2 min before sunrise and after sunset**; also twinkling of stars, advanced sunrise.
[1:0:75] - **SI units:** pressure – **pascal**; temperature – **kelvin**; inductance – **henry**; weight (a force) – **newton**; 1 Å = 10⁻¹⁰ m (wavelength).
[1:0:76] - **X-rays:** penetrate soft tissue and light metals (Al); **not deflected** by electric/magnetic fields; travel at **c** in vacuum.
[1:0:77] - **Spherical mirror:** centre of the reflecting surface = **pole**; centre of the sphere = centre of curvature; f = R/2.
[1:0:78] - **Frequency order:** **cosmic/gamma > X-rays > UV > visible > infrared > microwaves > radio waves** (wavelength the reverse).
[1:0:79] - **Field-line traps:** they never begin at a point (no monopole) and **do exist inside a magnet** (S → N); lines near pole ends curve.
[1:0:80] - **Work and energy** have the same dimensions **ML²T⁻²** (both in joules); power ML²T⁻³.
[1:0:81] - **Principle of calorimetry:** heat lost = heat gained.
[1:0:82] - **Starting torque** of a slip-ring motor is raised by **adding resistance to the rotor**.
[1:0:83] - **Ammeter** measures **current** (in series); **voltmeter** – potential difference (parallel); **galvanometer** detects small current; **rheostat** varies resistance.
[1:0:84] - **LPG** = propane + butane; **CNG/PNG** = mainly **methane**.
[1:0:85] - **Water is densest at 4 °C** (1 g/cc); it expands on cooling from 4 °C to 0 °C (anomalous expansion).
[1:0:86] - **Not all liquids conduct:** electrolytes (salt solution), mercury conduct; **pure water, oils, kerosene** are insulators.
[1:0:87] - **Sound characteristics:** **pitch ↔ frequency** (one octave higher = **double frequency**, 500 → 1000 Hz); **loudness ↔ amplitude**; quality/timbre ↔ waveform; persistence of hearing ~**0.1 s**; denser compressions = higher **pressure**.
[1:0:88] - **Torque** = force × perpendicular distance – unit **N m** (same dimension as work, but not joule).
[1:0:89] - **Galvanometer → voltmeter:** high resistance in **series**; → ammeter: low resistance (shunt) in parallel.
[1:0:90] - **Transformer losses** (heating): copper (I²R), **hysteresis**, eddy currents, flux leakage.
[1:0:91] - **Headlight:** bulb at the **focus** of a concave mirror → reflected rays go out as a **parallel beam**.
[1:0:92] - **At Earth's centre g = 0**, so **weight = 0**; g is maximum at the poles, minimum at the equator.
[1:0:93] - **Units named after scientists:** **pascal – pressure**; joule – energy/work; watt – power; newton – force.
[1:0:94] - **Viscosity:** liquids – **decreases with temperature**; gases – increases.
[1:0:95] - **Heat/energy units:** joule, **calorie** (4.18 J), erg; **centigrade** is a temperature scale.
[1:0:96] - **Viscosity of liquids falls as temperature rises** (e.g. glycerin, honey); that of gases rises.
[1:0:97] - **Rocket** works on **Newton's third law** (action–reaction), i.e. conservation of momentum.
[1:0:98] - **All waves** (EM, sound, water) **reflect, carry energy and exert pressure**; only **EM waves cross vacuum**.
[1:0:99] - **Resolving power** = ability to show two closely placed objects as separate.
[1:0:100] - **Floating or sinking** depends on the **difference in densities** of object and liquid (floats if its density is lower).
[1:0:101] - **Transformer breather** – filled with **silica gel** (absorbs moisture; blue → pink when wet).
[1:0:102] - **Weight** is a force – unit **newton**, not pascal.
[1:0:103] - **Newton's third law** (action–reaction): **horse pulling a cart**, walking, swimming, **cannon/gun recoil**, rocket and jet propulsion.
[1:0:104] - **Photoelectric cells** use low-work-function alkali metals – **rubidium, caesium, potassium**.
[1:0:105] - **Fathometer** (echo sounder) – measures **depth of the sea**.
[1:0:106] - **Fleming's left-hand rule** = force on a **current in a magnetic field** (motor); right-hand rule = induced current (generator).
[1:0:107] - **Spring constant** depends on the spring's **material, wire thickness, coil diameter** (and length).
[1:0:108] - **Convection** only in **fluids (liquids and gases)**; conduction – mainly solids; radiation – also through vacuum.
[1:0:109] - **Lift cable breaks** (free fall) → apparent weight **zero**; lift **accelerating down** → weight less; accelerating up → weight more.
[1:0:110] - **Sound needs a medium** – it cannot cross the vacuum of space (no sound from the Moon).
[1:0:111] - **Hypermetropia** (long sight) → **convex** lens; **myopia** → concave; reading glasses = convex.
[1:0:112] - **Light** = **electromagnetic wave** (transverse, needs no medium, 3 × 10⁸ m/s).
[1:0:113] - **Stability:** a body does not topple while the **vertical through its centre of gravity falls within its base** (Leaning Tower of Pisa).
[1:0:114] - **Specific gravity (relative density)** = density of substance ÷ density of **water at 4 °C**; measured by hydrometer.
[1:0:115] - **Hydrogen atom:** ground-state energy **−13.6 eV**, so its **ionisation energy = 13.6 eV**.
[1:0:116] - **Dentist's mirror = concave** – teeth within focus give an erect, **magnified** image.
[1:0:117] - **Metal feels colder** than plastic at the same temperature – metals are **good conductors of heat** and draw heat from the hand faster.
[1:0:118] - **Sound is mechanical** (needs a medium); X-rays, microwaves, radio waves are electromagnetic.
[1:0:119] - **Lateral inversion** – a plane mirror swaps left and right (AMBULANCE written in reverse).
[1:0:120] - **Bragg spectrometer** measures **X-ray wavelength** (nλ = 2d sin θ); GM counter detects radiation; cyclotron accelerates particles.
[1:0:121] - **No external force** → velocity of the **centre of mass** stays **constant**.
[1:0:122] - **Bernoulli:** faster air between two passing cars → **lower pressure** → cars pushed together (side-swipe).
[1:0:123] - **Light/white clothes** reflect heat (poor absorbers) – worn in summer; **dark colours absorb** heat.
[1:0:124] - **Hypermetropia (far/long-sightedness):** near objects blurred, image formed **behind the retina**, corrected by a **convex lens**; near point beyond 25 cm (e.g. cannot read closer than 1 m). **Myopia:** near objects clear.
[1:0:125] - **Power = rate of doing work** (work ÷ time), SI unit **watt**; 1 horsepower = 746 W.
[1:0:126] - "Image at **infinity**" is true only when the object is **at F** of a concave mirror, not between P and F.
[1:0:127] - **Concave mirror:** solar cooker, car **headlight** (bulb at focus → parallel beam), shaving/dentist's mirror (magnified); **convex mirror:** vehicle **rear-view** (wide field, erect, diminished).
[1:0:128] - **Heat flow direction** depends only on **temperature** – from hotter to colder body.
[1:0:129] - **Light units:** **lux** = illuminance / luminous emittance (lm/m²); candela = intensity; lumen = flux.
[1:0:130] - **Concave mirror, object between C and F** → image **beyond C**, real, inverted, enlarged.
[1:0:131] - **Echo** = **reflection** of sound.
[1:0:132] - **Pressure** = force/area; SI unit **N/m² = pascal**; N·m = joule (work).
[1:0:133] - **Photon energy: UV > visible > infrared** (shorter wavelength → higher energy, E = hc/λ).
[1:0:134] - **Induced current:** **maximum** when the conductor moves **perpendicular** to the field; in a rotating coil it **reverses every half revolution** (AC).
[1:0:135] - **White reflects heat, black absorbs it:** white clothes in summer, dark in winter; umbrella **white on top, black underneath**.
[1:0:136] - **Ohm's law is not universal:** semiconductors, diodes and strong fields deviate – 'all homogeneous materials obey it' is false.
[1:0:137] - **Reverberation** = persistence of sound by **multiple reflections** from walls, roof and floor of large halls.
[1:0:138] - **Metric prefixes:** kilo 10³ (1 km = **1000 m**), centi 10⁻², milli 10⁻³, micro 10⁻⁶, **nano 10⁻⁹** (1 nm = 10⁻⁹ m = **10⁻⁷ cm**), pico 10⁻¹², **fermi 10⁻¹⁵ m** (nuclear sizes), angstrom 10⁻¹⁰ m.
[1:0:139] - **Capillarity:** lamp wick, blotting paper, water rise in plants; a **straw works by air pressure**, not capillarity.
[1:0:140] - **SHM acceleration** is **maximum at the amplitude** (extreme position).
[1:0:141] - **Sound** needs a medium – **no sound in vacuum**; **pitch** depends on **frequency**, loudness on amplitude.
[1:0:142] - **Ice in sawdust:** sawdust is a **poor conductor** of heat, so ice melts slowly.
[1:0:143] - **Kelvin** (absolute scale, 0 K = −273.15 °C) has **no negative values**; −40° is equal on Celsius and Fahrenheit.
[1:0:144] - **Air bubble in water / glass** = behaves as a **concave (diverging) lens** (rarer medium inside a convex shape).
[1:0:145] - **Equilibrium:** ball on top of a vertical rod = **unstable**; ball in a bowl = stable; ball on a flat floor = neutral.
[1:0:146] - **Ohmic conductors** (V ∝ I) = metals such as **silver**, copper; semiconductors (Ge, Si) and carbon are non-ohmic.
[1:0:147] - **Concave mirror uses:** **dentist's and shaving mirrors** (enlarged erect image), **torches, headlights, searchlights** (parallel beam), solar furnaces. **Convex mirror:** **rear-view mirror** of vehicles (erect, diminished, wide field – virtual and erect image for any object position).
[1:0:148] - **Insulators:** **ebonite**, rubber, glass, mica, dry wood – resistivity ~**10¹²–10¹⁷ Ω m**; a conductor with appreciable resistance = **resistor**.
[1:0:149] - **Accommodation:** ciliary muscles relax → lens **thinner → focal length increases** (distant vision); contract → thicker, shorter focal length (near).
[1:0:150] - **Frictionless ground:** a man can move only by throwing something / **sneezing** (momentum conservation); friction is a **non-conservative, non-central** force.
[1:0:151] - **Unequal opposite forces not in one line** → net force + torque → **rotation and translation**; equal ones = a couple (rotation only).
[1:0:152] - **Particle nature of light – photoelectric effect** (Einstein, photons); wave nature – interference, diffraction, polarisation.
[1:0:153] - **Pressure units:** SI **pascal (N/m²)**; also **kg/cm² (kgf/cm²)**, atm, bar, mmHg; kg/cm and kg/mm are not pressure units.
[1:0:154] - **Potential difference** = work done to move a **unit charge** between two points (1 V = 1 J/C); measured by a **voltmeter** in parallel.
[1:0:155] - **All photons travel at c (3 × 10⁸ m/s) in vacuum** whatever their energy; X-ray photon: shorter wavelength, higher frequency than visible.
[1:0:156] - **Second equation of motion** s = ut + ½at² links **position (displacement) and time**.
[1:0:157] - **Candle in space:** books say gravity is needed for a flame; in microgravity the flame is actually **spherical, dim, blue** (NASA).
[1:0:158] - **Hydrogen spectrum:** larger energy gap → shorter wavelength; **n=2 → n=1** (Lyman) gives the shortest among 2→1, 3→2, 4→3, 5→4.
[1:0:159] - **Knot** = speed at sea (1 nautical mile/h); **fathom** = depth (6 ft).
[1:0:160] - **Refractive index:** air **1.0003**, water 1.33, glass ~1.5, diamond 2.42 (highest common).
[1:0:161] - **Thin-film interference** gives colours of **soap bubbles** and oil films.
[1:0:162] - **Detergent lowers the surface tension** of water (better wetting and cleaning).
[1:0:163] - On refraction **frequency never changes**; speed and wavelength change.
[1:0:164] - **Aeroplane wing:** convex top – air flows **faster over the top → lower pressure** above → lift (Bernoulli).
[1:0:165] - **Plane mirror image = virtual, erect and laterally inverted** (same size; focal length infinite, not zero).
[1:0:166] - **g = 0 at the Earth's centre**; maximum at the **poles**, less at the equator.
[1:0:167] - **Bats** navigate by echoes of **ultrasonic waves** (also dolphins).
[1:0:168] - **Fan cooling:** a fan speeds up **evaporation of sweat**, which absorbs body heat; it does not cool the air.
[1:0:169] - **Rutherford (1933)** called atomic power **'moonshine'** – energy input exceeded output, not a doubt about E = mc².
[1:0:170] - Sound cannot travel in a **vacuum**.
[1:0:171] - **Momentum unit:** kg m s⁻¹ = **newton second (N s)**.
[1:0:172] - **Primary colours:** light – **red, green, blue**; pigments – red, yellow, blue; **black** is not a colour of light.
[1:0:173] - **Dry cell:** **metal cap = positive terminal**; zinc case = negative.
[1:0:174] - **Stress** = force/area, same unit as **pressure** (pascal).
[1:0:175] - **Infrared** – reading faded old documents, night vision, remote controls; **UV** – detecting forgery.
[1:0:176] - **Equations of motion:** v = u + at, s = ut + ½at², v² = u² + 2as – **u = initial velocity**, v = final velocity.
[1:0:177] - Convex + concave lens of **equal focal length** in contact = **plane glass** (zero power).
[1:0:178] - **Uniform circular motion:** speed and **magnitude** of acceleration constant; velocity and acceleration **directions change**.
[1:0:179] - **Choke** of a tube light = an **inductor**: starting voltage surge, then limits current.
[1:0:180] - **Maxwell (Mx)** = CGS unit of **magnetic flux** (1 Mx = 10⁻⁸ Wb); SI unit weber.
[1:0:181] - **Additive colours (light):** red + green + blue = **white**; green + blue = cyan, red + blue = magenta.
[1:0:182] - **Dispersion** = splitting of white light into **VIBGYOR** by a **prism** (violet bends most, red least); rainbow = dispersion in raindrops.
[1:0:183] - **s = ut + ½at²:** distance under constant acceleration **depends on initial velocity u**.
[1:0:184] - **Solids:** definite shape, high density, rigidity and **negligible compressibility**.
[1:0:185] - **BJT doping:** emitter **heavily** doped, **base lightly doped and thin**, collector moderately doped.
[1:0:186] - **Impulse** = F × t = **change in momentum**; work done by a centripetal force (circular motion) = **zero**.
[1:0:187] - A wave (longitudinal or transverse) transmits **energy only**, not matter – particles just oscillate.
[1:0:188] - **Thermal conductivity:** copper ~400 > zinc ~116 > lead ~35 > **mercury ~8 W/m·K (poorest)**.
[1:0:189] - **Buoyancy (upthrust)** exists only because of gravity; with no gravity there is **no upthrust**.
[1:0:190] - **Alcohol (spirit) thermometer** – for very low temperatures (alcohol freezes at ~−114 °C; mercury at −39 °C).
[1:0:191] - **Parallel resistors** share the **same potential difference** (current divides); **series** resistors carry the **same current**. Household wiring is parallel.
[1:0:192] - **Insulators** have electrons, but they are **tightly bound and do not flow easily**.
[1:0:193] - **Threshold frequency** = **minimum frequency** of light that ejects photoelectrons; minimum energy = **work function**.
[1:0:194] - **Manganin, constantan** – resistivity **almost unchanged with temperature** → standard resistors; **nichrome** – high resistivity, heater coils.
[1:0:195] - **Astronomical telescope:** final image **virtual, inverted and magnified**.
[1:0:196] - **Hertz** = SI unit of **frequency** (1 cycle per second).
[1:0:197] - **Pressure inside a soap bubble > atmospheric** (excess 4T/r).
[1:0:198] - **Churning milk:** cream separates by **centrifugal force** (lighter fat moves to the centre); centrifuges work the same way.
[1:0:199] - **Atomic force microscope** (and STM) – images **individual atoms**.
[1:0:200] - **Faraday's law:** faster magnet motion → faster flux change → **larger induced current**.
[1:0:201] - **Gravity:** only **attractive**, weakest fundamental force, long range; electric/magnetic forces attract **and** repel.
[1:0:202] - **Insulators are charged by static electricity** (friction/rubbing – comb, glass rod); current electricity flows only through conductors.
[1:0:203] - "**Objects in mirror are closer than they appear**" – printed on **convex** rear-view mirrors (diminished image looks farther).
[1:0:204] - **SI units in electricity:** charge – **coulomb**; current – ampere; potential difference – volt; resistance – **ohm**.
[1:0:205] - **Same M, R:** disc I = ½MR² > solid sphere I = 2/5 MR².
[1:0:206] - **Bose–Einstein condensate** = **5th state of matter** (near absolute zero; S.N. Bose & Einstein).
[1:0:207] - **Gas laws:** Boyle (PV const; V vs 1/p = **straight line**), Charles (V ∝ T), Avogadro, **Gay-Lussac's combining volumes** (simple volume ratios); **Joule's law** is about **heating by current**, not a gas law.
[1:0:208] - **Power of lens P = 1/f (m)**, unit **dioptre**; **+ power = convex**, – power = concave; **+2 D → f = 0.5 m**.
[1:0:209] - **Infrasonic < 20 Hz** < audible < 20 kHz < **ultrasonic**.
[1:0:210] - **Silver** = lowest resistivity (best conductor), then copper, aluminium; nichrome high.
[1:0:211] - Only a **concave mirror** can give an **enlarged** and an **inverted (real)** image; convex always diminished-erect, plane always same size.
[1:0:212] - **Force carriers:** **photon** – electromagnetic energy; gluon – strong force; W/Z – weak force; graviton (hypothetical) – gravity.
[1:0:213] - **Oscilloscope (CRO)** displays waveforms – used to **see sound waves** (via a microphone).
[1:0:214] - **Henry (H)** = SI unit of **inductance**; hertz = frequency.
[1:0:215] - **Washing machine** (spin drier) works on **centrifugation**; **cream separator** too.
[1:0:216] - **Eye defects:** myopia → **concave** lens; **hypermetropia (long sight)** → **convex** lens (image behind retina; near point far; can see far objects clearly); presbyopia → bifocal; **astigmatism → cylindrical** lens.
[1:0:217] - **Convex mirror** always forms a **virtual, erect, diminished** image (rear-view mirrors).
[1:0:218] - **Newton's third law:** bird flight, rocket propulsion, gun recoil, swimming.
[1:0:219] - **SI units:** joule – energy/work; **pascal (N/m²) – pressure**; watt – power; newton – force; **kelvin – temperature**; ampere – current; candela – luminous intensity.
[1:0:220] - **Hypermetropia (long sight)** – image behind retina – corrected by a **convex lens**.
[1:0:221] - **Magnetic field** = region around a magnet where its force can be detected.
[1:0:222] - **Ultrasonic waves** are produced by **piezoelectric** crystals (quartz) vibrating under AC voltage.
[1:0:223] - **Boiling point falls with pressure:** at **high altitude** (low air pressure) water boils **below 100 °C**, so cooking is slower; a pressure cooker does the reverse.
[1:0:224] - **Electromagnet core = soft iron** (magnetises and loses it easily); permanent magnets = steel/alnico.
[1:0:225] - **Pressure is scalar:** it is the **normal component of force per unit area** and has no direction of its own.
[1:0:226] - **Liquid pressure P = hρg:** depends on **height and density**, **not on base area**.
[1:0:227] - **Round trip:** displacement zero → **average velocity zero** (average speed = 100 km / 2 h = 50 km/h).
[1:0:228] - **EM waves:** not elastic (need no medium), travel in vacuum, **E ⟂ B**, speed **3 × 10⁸ m/s (3 lakh km/s)**.
[1:0:229] - **Second law (Clausius):** heat cannot flow by itself from a colder to a hotter body – a refrigerator needs external work.
[1:0:230] - **Kaleidoscope** – **multiple reflections** in inclined plane mirrors; periscope – plane mirrors; optical fibre – total internal reflection.
[1:0:231] - **Blue sky** = **Rayleigh scattering** of short-wavelength blue light; in space the sky looks **black** (no air to scatter).
[1:0:232] - **Latent heat** is fixed at a given temperature but **varies with temperature** (vaporisation heat → 0 at the critical point); independent of volume.
[1:0:233] - **Black surface** absorbs all colours and **reflects none** (blackboard).
[1:0:234] - **Constant velocity:** straight-line motion, constant speed, **zero acceleration**, displacement grows linearly.
[1:0:235] - **−40°** reads the same on Celsius and Fahrenheit (F = 9C/5 + 32).
[1:0:236] - **Concave mirror, object between P and F** → image **behind the mirror, virtual, erect, enlarged**; object at F → image at infinity.
[1:0:237] - **Electron:** in **cathode rays**, negative, mass ≈ 1/1836 of a proton, deflected by **electric and magnetic** fields.
[1:0:238] - Light is made of energy packets called **photons** (Planck, Einstein).
[1:0:239] - **Rainbow / dispersion:** a **shower of raindrops** splits sunlight into its colours (refraction + dispersion + internal reflection); a plane mirror or a single lens does not.
[1:0:240] - **Oil, soap or detergent** on water **lowers its surface tension** considerably.
[1:0:241] - **Uniform acceleration:** average velocity = (u + v)/2, so **v = 2 × average velocity − u**.
[1:0:242] - **Gravitation F ∝ 1/r²:** doubling the distance makes the force **one-fourth**.
[1:0:243] - **Gravitation:** universal, dominant between celestial bodies, negligible for atoms, but **not the same for all pairs** (F = Gm1m2/r²).
[1:0:244] - **Parallel resistors:** net R is **less than the smallest** (2, 4, 8 Ω → 8/7 Ω); house wiring is parallel.
[1:0:245] - **Ear:** response is roughly **logarithmic** (decibel scale); **sensitivity varies with frequency** (best ~1–4 kHz).
[1:0:246] - **Hydrostatic pressure:** **p = p₀ + ρgh** – fluid pressure rises with depth (dams are thicker at the base); treated in books under **Pascal's law** of fluid pressure.
[1:0:247] - **Angle of dip:** **90° at the magnetic poles**, **0° at the magnetic equator**.
[1:0:248] - **Regelation:** pressure **lowers the melting point of ice** – pressed ice blocks melt at contact and refreeze into one block (also ice skating).
[1:0:249] - **Archimedes' principle** uses: **purity of metals** (density via loss of weight in water – the king's crown), **lactometer**, hydrometer, ships and submarines.
[1:0:250] - **SI units:** acceleration **m s⁻²**; velocity m s⁻¹; force newton; energy **joule**; power watt; pressure pascal.
[1:0:251] - **During melting/boiling temperature stays constant** – heat goes as **latent heat**.
[1:0:252] - **Reflection:** angle of incidence = **angle of reflection**; refraction does not occur at a plane mirror.
[1:0:253] - **Dimensionless:** **strain** (ratio), refractive index, relative density; gas constant, viscosity, Planck's constant have dimensions.
[1:0:254] - **Integrated circuits (chips)** are made on **silicon** wafers.
[1:0:255] - **Conservation of energy:** energy can **neither be created nor destroyed**; photon energy **E = pc** (p = 10 MeV/c → E = 10 MeV).
[1:0:256] - **Torque = force × distance:** door handle **far from the hinges** needs least force.
[1:0:257] - **Conservation of mass-energy:** mass-energy can neither be created nor destroyed – 'something from nothing' is impossible (E = mc²).
[1:0:258] - **Anomalous expansion:** water **contracts** from 0 °C to **4 °C** (max density), then expands; rail gaps and mercury thermometers use normal expansion.
[1:0:259] - **g decreases** with **height** (and depth) and from poles to equator; g = GM/R², so more mass or smaller radius raises g.
[1:0:260] - **Energy units:** joule = N m = W s; kWh, eV; **kg m/s² = newton** (force).
[1:0:261] - **Floating boat:** removing the iron nails from the boat makes it rise, so it displaces less water and the **lake level falls**.
[1:0:262] - **Not true:** audible range 20 Hz–10 kHz (it is **20 Hz–20 kHz**); ultrasound >10 kHz (it is **>20 kHz**); earthquakes give **infrasound**, not ultrasound, before the main shock.
[1:0:263] - **Bubbles in boiling water** are mostly **water vapour** (steam), not air or H2/O2.
[1:0:264] - **EM waves are transverse** (E and B fields ⟂ each other and to travel), need no medium, carry energy and momentum, can be polarised.
[1:0:265] - **Viscosity** = internal friction of a fluid – a stirred liquid comes to rest because of it.

[1:0:275] - **In glass, violet is slowest** (highest refractive index, v = c/n); red is fastest and bends least.

[1:0:388] - **In vacuum all bodies fall together** (same g, no air resistance) – coin, feather and mango land at the same time.
[1:0:389] - **Levers:** class I – scissors, seesaw; **class II – wheelbarrow**, nutcracker (load in middle); class III – tongs, forearm.
[1:0:390] - **Bound orbits:** a body launched **below escape velocity** (11.2 km/s from Earth) stays bound – its **total energy (KE + PE) is negative**; at escape velocity it is zero.
[1:0:391] - A mirror that always shows you **smaller** than you are = **convex mirror**.
[1:0:392] - **Surface tension:** soap bubbles and raindrops are **spherical** (least surface); **detergents lower** it; capillarity is its result.
[1:0:393] - **Kelvin → Celsius:** **subtract 273** (C = K − 273); Celsius → Kelvin: add 273.
[1:0:394] - **Angstrom (Å)** = **10⁻¹⁰ m** (length; wavelengths, atomic sizes).
[1:0:395] - **Wire falling along East–West:** cuts Earth's horizontal field, so **current is induced West → East** (Fleming's right-hand rule).
[1:0:396] - **Resistance** – unit **ohm (Ω)**; volt = potential difference; ampere = current.
[1:0:397] - **Speed units** = distance/time (m/s, km/h, cm/s); **m²/h is not** a speed unit.
[1:0:398] - **Tone of a stringed instrument** is set by the **wave form** (mix of overtones).
[1:0:399] - A plane mirror **never forms a real image** – its image cannot be taken on a screen.
[1:0:400] - **Angstrom** (Å = 10⁻¹⁰ m) – unit of length for **wavelength**.
[1:0:401] - Convex mirror – used in vehicles as the **rear-view mirror**.
[1:0:402] - **Weight** = the **force with which the Earth attracts a body towards its centre** (W = mg, unit newton); it varies with place.
[1:0:403] - **Mach number** = speed ÷ speed of sound; **> 1 supersonic**, < 1 subsonic, > 5 hypersonic.
[1:0:404] - **Primary colours of light** (photography, screens) = **red, green, blue**; yellow, cyan, magenta are secondary (printing inks).
[1:0:405] - **Floating ice melting** in a glass of water: **level stays the same** (Archimedes – floating body displaces its own weight).
[1:0:406] - **Copper feels hotter than glass** at the same temperature because it **conducts heat faster** (not because it is denser).
[1:0:407] - **Pascal** gives his name to the unit of pressure (force ÷ area); **Pascal's law** = pressure applied to a confined fluid is transmitted equally in all directions – **hydraulic lift, brakes**.
[1:0:408] - **Mechanical waves** (need a medium) = **sound**, water, seismic waves; radio, light, X-rays = **electromagnetic** (travel in vacuum).
[1:0:409] - **Prefixes:** milli 10⁻³, micro 10⁻⁶, **nano 10⁻⁹**, pico 10⁻¹².
[1:0:410] - **Particles:** photon – zero mass, **spin 1** (boson); **neutrino** – (near-)zero mass; **quark** – **fractional charge**; electron/positron – spin ½.
[1:0:411] - **RADAR** detects aircraft with **radio waves** (microwave band).
[1:0:412] - **Lateral inversion** is a **reflection** effect (plane mirror), not refraction; twinkling and mirage are refraction.
[1:0:413] - **Rectifier (diode)** converts **AC to DC**; inverter – DC to AC; transformer changes AC voltage.
[1:0:414] - **Helium balloon rises** because helium is **less dense than air** (buoyant force > weight).
[1:0:415] - **Charge in a magnetic field:** force ⟂ velocity, no work – **speed constant**, velocity/momentum direction changes.
[1:0:416] - **Images in two mirrors:** n = 360°/θ − 1; **parallel mirrors → infinite** images.
[1:0:417] - **Air bubble in water** acts as a **concave (diverging) lens**; convex + concave of equal focal length in contact = **plane glass** (zero power).
[1:0:418] - **Semiconductors:** silicon, germanium, gallium arsenide; **quartz (SiO2)** is an insulator.
[1:0:419] - **Mercury thermometer:** works on **thermal expansion** of the liquid; it measures **temperature**, not heat.
[1:0:420] - **Photoelectric effect** is the **reverse of X-ray production** (photon → electron vs electron → photon).
[1:0:421] - **Weight on the Moon ≈ 1/6** of that on Earth (mass unchanged).
[1:0:422] - **Sound** = a form of (mechanical) **energy** producing hearing; a **mechanical wave** needing a medium (cannot travel in vacuum).
[1:0:423] - **Dimensions:** current [I], length [L], time [T], mass [M] – A·m/s² = **[I L T⁻²]**.
[1:0:424] - **Telescope:** larger **objective diameter** → higher **resolving power**.
[1:0:425] - **Through red glass** red and white objects both look **red**; others look black.
[1:0:426] - **Heating a plate with a hole:** the **hole expands** like the metal around it.
[1:0:427] - **Thermometer measures temperature**, calorimeter measures heat.
[1:0:428] - **Work–energy theorem:** positive net work **increases KE** (W = ΔKE).
[1:0:429] - **Luminous** objects emit their own light (Sun, lamp, candle); the **Moon** only reflects sunlight.
[1:0:430] - **Deep-sea diving:** **high water pressure** (P = hρg) can rupture the eardrum.
[1:0:431] - **Kirchhoff's laws:** junction rule = **conservation of charge**; loop rule (sum of emfs and p.d.s = 0) = **conservation of energy**.
[1:0:432] - **Simple pendulum:** T = 2π√(L/g) – depends on **length and g**, **not on mass or (small) amplitude**.
[1:0:433] - **Sound is a mechanical (not EM) wave**; light, radio, microwaves, X-rays are EM waves.
[1:0:434] - **Electromagnetism:** a changing magnetic field induces current (Faraday); current produces a magnetic field (Oersted).
[1:0:435] - **Compound microscope:** eyepiece **f larger** than objective f; final image **virtual**, magnified, inverted.
[1:0:436] - **Surface tension** makes drops **spherical** (least surface area for a volume).
[1:0:437] - **Concave mirror uses:** **vehicle headlights**, torches, search-lights, shaving/make-up mirrors, **dentist's mirror**, solar furnaces.
[1:0:438] - **g = GM/R²:** radius shrinks 1% with mass unchanged → g **rises ~2%**.
[1:0:439] - **Newton's laws:** 1st – inertia (rest/uniform motion unless a net force acts); **2nd – rate of change of momentum ∝ net force, in its direction**; 3rd – action = reaction.
[1:0:440] - **Pressure = force ÷ area**; SI unit **pascal: 1 Pa = 1 N m⁻²**.
[1:0:441] - **String snaps in circular motion:** stone flies off **along the tangent** (velocity is tangential).
[1:0:442] - **Evaporation** increases with **surface area**, temperature, wind and lower humidity (spread clothes dry faster); it is a surface, cooling process.
[1:0:443] - **Water:** **maximum density at 4 °C** (anomalous expansion); boils at **100 °C = 212 °F** at 1 atm.
[1:0:444] - **Resistance depends on length, area of cross-section, material (resistivity) and temperature – not on the current** through it.
[1:0:445] - **Periscope:** two plane mirrors **parallel to each other**, each at 45° to the tube.
[1:0:446] - **Ebonite** (hard vulcanised rubber) – a heat/electric **insulator**, used for cooker handles.
[1:0:447] - **RADAR** = reflection of radio waves; **SONAR**/fathometer = reflection of sound (sea depth); **echo** = reflected sound.
[1:0:448] - **Electromagnetic waves** (radio, light, X-rays) carry no charge – **not deflected** by magnetic or electric fields.
[1:0:449] - **Presbyopia** (age, weak ciliary muscles): often both near and far vision fail → **bifocal lens** (upper concave, lower convex).
[1:0:450] - **Planimeter** – area of plotted maps; **pyrometer** – very high temperatures; **echo sounder** – depth of the sea.
[1:0:451] - **Moon's surface gravity ≈ 1/6 of Earth's** – weight there is 1/6, mass unchanged.
[1:0:452] - **Convex mirror image:** always **virtual, erect, diminished**, between pole and focus behind the mirror.
[1:0:453] - **Wavelength order:** radio > microwave > **infrared** > visible > UV > X-ray > gamma.
[1:0:454] - **Thermal capacity** = mass × specific heat – depends on **mass** (and material), not shape or temperature.
[1:0:455] - **Dispersion** = splitting of white light into colours by a prism (rainbow = dispersion + total internal reflection).
[1:0:456] - **Thermopile** (many thermocouples) detects **radiant heat**; liquid and Six's thermometers measure temperature.
[1:0:457] - **Positron** = antiparticle of the **electron** (Carl Anderson, 1932).
[1:0:458] - **Spring balance in orbit:** extension method fails (weightlessness); **oscillation period T = 2π√(m/k)** still gives mass.
[1:0:459] - **Spring constant k = F/x** (Hooke's law) – unit **N/m**.
[1:0:460] - **SI units:** velocity **m/s**; acceleration m/s²; weight/force **newton**; pressure **pascal**.
[1:0:461] - **Inertia** = tendency of a body to stay at rest or in uniform motion unless a net force acts (**Newton's first law = law of inertia**).
[1:0:462] - **Persistence of vision** ~**1/16 s** (NCERT; often quoted 0.1 s) – why films run at **24 frames/s**.
[1:0:463] - **LASER** = Light Amplification by **Stimulated Emission** of Radiation.
[1:0:464] - **Rear-view (convex) mirror:** image **virtual, erect and smaller** – wider field of view, so all three reasons hold.
[1:0:465] - **Solenoid:** field **inside is uniform** (parallel lines) – used to make electromagnets.
[1:0:466] - **Fundamental (SI base) quantities:** length, **mass**, time, current, temperature, amount, luminous intensity; **weight** is a force (derived); mass = amount of matter, same everywhere.
[1:0:467] - **Melting point** indicates the **strength of intermolecular forces of attraction**.
[1:0:468] - **Magnetic keepers** (soft-iron pieces across the poles) protect magnets from **self-demagnetisation**.
[1:0:469] - **Galvanometer** = detects the **presence (and direction) of current**; refractometer – refractive index; viscometer – viscosity.
[1:0:470] - **Microscope magnification** ≈ (L/f_o)(D/f_e): **shorter focal lengths** (objective and eyepiece) give **more magnification**.
[1:0:471] - **Refraction effects:** bent pencil in water, early sunrise, twinkling, mirage; **blue sky = scattering**.
[1:0:472] - **Adiabatic** = no heat exchange (sudden **tyre burst**, rapid compression); isothermal = constant temperature.
[1:0:473] - **Crest to next trough = half a wavelength**; crest to crest = one wavelength.
[1:0:474] - **Bats** navigate by **ultrasonic** echoes (echolocation).
[1:0:475] - **Inertia:** body lurches forward when a bus brakes, backward when it starts; dust leaves a beaten carpet (inertia of rest).
[1:0:476] - **Changes of state:** evaporation – liquid → gas; sublimation – solid → gas; freezing – liquid → solid; melting – solid → liquid.
[1:0:477] - **Floating ice melts → water level unchanged** (ice displaces its own weight of water).
[1:0:478] - **Transistor** = semiconductor amplifier/switch – found in **hearing aids**, radios; a fuse is a heating wire, a fluorescent lamp a gas-discharge tube.
[1:0:479] - **Cosmic rays** = high-energy **charged particles** (mostly protons) from space, **not EM waves**.
[1:0:480] - **Voltmeter in parallel**; ammeter, fuse, galvanometer in series.
[1:0:481] - **Refraction:** denser → rarer medium – light **speeds up and bends away from the normal**; rarer → denser – slows, bends towards the normal.
[1:0:482] - **Newton's law of cooling:** rate of heat loss **∝ temperature difference** (doubles when the difference doubles).
[1:0:483] - **Magnetic field lines:** **closed curves**, never cross; outside N → S, inside S → N; none start at a point (no monopoles).
[1:0:484] - **Convex mirror:** image always **virtual, erect, diminished** (point-sized for a far object) – wide field of view.
[1:0:485] - **Units:** acceleration m/s²; **impulse = N·s** (change of momentum); work/energy – joule.
[1:0:486] - **Free fall (air resistance ignored):** constant **acceleration g**, speed keeps increasing.
[1:0:487] - **Velocity** = rate of change of **displacement**; acceleration = rate of change of velocity; retardation = negative acceleration.
[1:0:488] - **TV remote** = **infrared** waves; **bats** navigate by **ultrasonic** echoes; sound is **not** an electromagnetic wave; **RADAR = Radio Detection And Ranging**.
[1:0:489] - **Newton's third law:** swimmer pushes water back → water pushes swimmer forward; gun recoil; rocket (also momentum conservation).
[1:0:490] - **Refractive index** n = c/v – **always greater than 1** for ordinary media.
[1:0:491] - **Waves** (sound) carry **energy, not matter** – particles only oscillate about their mean positions.
[1:0:492] - **Hydrogen series:** **Lyman – ultraviolet** (to n=1); Balmer – visible (n=2); Paschen, Brackett, Pfund – infrared.
[1:0:493] - **Negative acceleration (retardation):** velocity **decreases** with time.
[1:0:494] - **Ohm's law:** **V = IR** – potential difference ∝ current at constant temperature.
[1:0:495] - **Kinetic theory:** average KE of ideal-gas molecules = (3/2)kT – **∝ absolute temperature**.
[1:0:496] - **Thermal conductivity:** stainless steel < aluminium < copper (< silver – best).
[1:0:497] - **Sound waves** are mechanical, **not electromagnetic** (IR, UV, radio are EM).
## SUB 1 | Human Body, Disorders and Health Technology
[1:1:0] # Genetic and blood disorders
[1:1:1] - **Thalassemia:** autosomal recessive; **reduced rate of synthesis of one of the globin chains** (α or β) of haemoglobin → anaemia (not X-linked, not a clotting defect, not phenylalanine build-up = PKU).
[1:1:2] - **Haemophilia:** **X-linked recessive** defect of a **clotting factor** – a cut keeps bleeding.
[1:1:3] - **Viruses** contain **nucleic acid** (DNA or RNA) in a protein coat; no enzymes for metabolism of their own.
[1:1:4] - **Scorpion venom** toxins are **proteins** (peptides).
[1:1:5] # Glands and digestion
[1:1:6] TABLE head: Gland | Hormone / fact
  [1:1:6.0] Pituitary (master) | At the base of the brain; GH, TSH, ACTH, ADH; oxytocin released here, made in hypothalamus
  [1:1:6.1] Pineal | In the brain (epithalamus); melatonin – biological clock; not on the kidney
  [1:1:6.2] Thyroid (largest endocrine) | Thyroxine (T4, T3) contains iodine; calcitonin; deficiency: cretinism, goitre
  [1:1:6.3] Thymus | Behind the sternum; trains T cells
  [1:1:6.4] Parathyroid | Back of thyroid; PTH – blood calcium
  [1:1:6.5] Adrenal (above kidneys) | Adrenaline, cortisol, aldosterone
  [1:1:6.6] Pancreas | Insulin (beta cells) lowers blood sugar; glucagon (alpha) raises it
  [1:1:6.7] Testes | Sperm and testosterone
  [1:1:6.8] Kidney | Erythropoietin (RBC production)
[1:1:7] - **Plant hormone** among options = **cytokinin** (cell division); thyroxine, oestrogen, testosterone are animal hormones.
[1:1:8] - Type 1 diabetes = **low insulin**; type 2 = **insulin resistance**.
[1:1:9] - **Stomach HCl:** from **parietal (oxyntic) cells**; pH ~1.5–3.5; activates **pepsinogen → pepsin**.
[1:1:10] - **Cattle:** **pepsin** is made in the **abomasum** (the "true stomach"); rumen, reticulum and omasum make no gastric juice.
[1:1:11] - **Blood:** **neutrophils** = most abundant granulocytes; Landsteiner – ABO groups (1901); **O negative = universal donor**, AB = universal recipient; natural pacemaker = **SA node** (right atrium).
[1:1:12] - **Liver:** largest gland; **urea is made in the liver** (not the kidney); **regrows** after partial removal → living-donor transplants.
[1:1:13] # Kidney and dialysis
[1:1:14] - Urine ~1.5 L/day, **pH ~6.0**; normal BP **120/80 mmHg**.
[1:1:15] - **Dialysis** removes wastes and excess water, but **cannot reabsorb nutrients** (glucose, amino acids) as the kidney tubules do; haemodialysis (machine) vs **peritoneal** dialysis (peritoneum as filter).
[1:1:16] # Imaging and health technology
[1:1:17] TABLE head: Method | Principle
  [1:1:17.0] X-ray (Röntgen) | Ionising radiation; security scanning
  [1:1:17.1] CT scan | X-rays + computer cross-sections; CT angiography – usual first test for aneurysm
  [1:1:17.2] MRI / MRA | Magnetic field + radio waves; not ionising; detects aneurysms (MR angiography)
  [1:1:17.3] Ultrasound | Sound > 20 kHz; safe for the foetus (no ionising radiation)
[1:1:18] - **Ultrasound uses:** sonography, cleaning jewellery, **industrial testing (cracks in metals)**, **SONAR** (underwater detection). **Light waves** – optical fibres.
[1:1:19] - **Telemedicine:** IT fibre backbone + **satellite**; **eSanjeevani** = national telemedicine service; wearables give doctors **real-time data**.
[1:1:20] - **Chitra heart valve** – **SCTIMST, Thiruvananthapuram**; **ARTSENS** – **IIT Madras**, arterial stiffness; **Garbhini-GA2** – first India-specific AI model for **fetal age** (**IIT Madras + THSTI Faridabad**).
[1:1:21] - **AIIMS Mangalagiri:** epidemiology unit maps outbreaks and **non-communicable diseases**.
[1:1:22] ~~ Sources: PMF General Science Part 4 p.20-24 (thalassemia, haemophilia); PMF General Science Part 2 p.42, p.52; NextGen Rocket Biology p.51, p.56; Barnes-Jewish Hospital "Brain Aneurysm Diagnosis" (CTA, MRA, angiogram); app notes 5:1
[1:1:23] - **Tissues:** connective = bone, cartilage, blood, tendon (not skeletal muscle, not skin); **muscle** has contractile actin–myosin; contraction = **Ca2+ released from the sarcoplasmic reticulum**, relaxation = Ca2+ pumped back; **smooth muscle** in stomach walls; cartilage in larynx, nose, ear – not in the urinary bladder; lactic acid causes cramps.
[1:1:24] - **Surgery terms:** **arthroplasty** – joint (hip/knee) replacement; **bariatric** – stomach bypass/weight-loss surgery; **lithotripsy** – breaking kidney stones by shock waves; **laparoscopy** – keyhole surgery with a camera, usually under general anaesthesia; **amniocentesis** – amniotic fluid test (genetic disorders, foetal sex); **Jarvik-7** – artificial heart; **BMD** test – osteoporosis.
[1:1:25] - **Warm-blooded** = birds and mammals (peacock, camel, ostrich); reptiles and fish (crocodile, tortoise, hagfish, dogfish) are cold-blooded.
[1:1:26] - **Pancreas** = endocrine (insulin from **beta cells**, glucagon) + exocrine (pancreatic juice); **insulin** promotes glucose uptake, fat storage and slows glycogen breakdown; **glucagon** raises sugar (glycogenolysis, gluconeogenesis); diabetes – high sugar, low insulin, extra glucose lost with water in urine; **gastrin** is a hormone, not a gland.
[1:1:27] - **Archaeopteryx** = oldest known **bird** (**Jurassic**), **reptile–bird connecting link** (teeth, bony tail, claws + feathers).
[1:1:28] - **Symmetry:** annelids (**Nereis**), arthropods = **bilateral**; coelenterates (sea anemone), echinoderms (Asterias, Echinus) = radial.
[1:1:29] - **King cobra** (*Ophiophagus hannah*, largest venomous snake) – **eats mainly other snakes** (ophiophagous).
[1:1:30] - **Blood cells:** **WBCs** defend the body; RBCs, neutrophils and lymphocytes form in the **bone marrow** (of long bones); **thrombin** turns fibrinogen into fibrin (clotting); blood pH ~**7.4** (slightly basic).
[1:1:31] - **Microtome** cuts thin tissue sections for histopathology; **endoscope** – thin optical-fibre tube (a narrow 'telescope') to see inside the body.
[1:1:32] - **Most digestion** happens in the **small intestine** (pancreatic juice + bile + intestinal juice); the pancreas only supplies enzymes.
[1:1:33] - **Salivary glands:** parotid (largest), submandibular, sublingual; the **pineal** is an endocrine gland in the brain.
[1:1:34] - **Circulation and breathing:** O2 path **lungs → blood → tissues**; nitrogen of air is unchanged by respiration; **coronary artery** feeds heart muscle; **left auricle** receives oxygenated blood from the lungs (pulmonary veins); **left ventricle** pumps it into the aorta; **hepatic portal vein** brings gut blood to the liver; narrower vessels → **higher BP**; tachycardia = fast heart rate.
[1:1:35] - **Antigen → antibody:** an antigen triggers **B lymphocytes (plasma cells)** to make specific **antibodies**; the thymus trains T cells; lymphocytes are the antibody-forming cells.
[1:1:36] - **Sexual cannibalism** (female eats male after mating) – **spiders** (black widow), also praying mantis.
[1:1:37] - **Magnification** = **observed size ÷ actual size**.
[1:1:38] - **Brain parts:** **cerebrum** – largest part (thinking, memory); **cerebellum** – balance and posture; **medulla oblongata** – heartbeat, breathing, **swallowing, vomiting**; **hypothalamus** – temperature, hunger/satiety, thirst (basal part of the diencephalon).
[1:1:39] - **Bariatric surgery** = **stomach bypass** (weight-loss) surgery.
[1:1:40] - **Mitochondria** = the '**powerhouses**' of the cell – aerobic respiration there makes ATP.
[1:1:41] - **Light entry is regulated by the iris** (it sets the pupil's size); NCERT words it as the pupil regulating light.
[1:1:42] - **Golden hour** = the first hour after a **heart attack** (or major trauma), when treatment saves most lives.
[1:1:43] - **Unicellular:** Amoeba, Paramecium, Euglena, bacteria, yeast; **Hydra** is **multicellular** (Cnidaria, reproduces by budding).
[1:1:44] - **Reproduction:** **binary fission** (Amoeba, bacteria), budding (Hydra, yeast), fragmentation = **asexual**; **Mendel's laws** rest on **sexual reproduction** (crossing of gametes, pea plants).
[1:1:45] - **Cyanide** blocks **cytochrome c oxidase** of the electron transport chain – no ATP, so death within minutes.
[1:1:46] - **Hypothalamus** = the body's **thermostat** – controls body temperature, hunger, thirst; links nervous and endocrine systems.
[1:1:47] - **Diabetes insipidus:** **deficiency** of vasopressin → much urine → **thirst**.
[1:1:48] - **Glycolysis** (cytoplasm): glucose → **2 pyruvic acid**; ethanol + CO2 form only later in yeast fermentation.
[1:1:49] - **Adrenal:** **adrenaline** = 'fight or flight' / **emergency hormone**; adrenal = **life-saving gland**; helps control **blood pressure**; cortisol is a steroid (no peptide chain) and rises with stress; female adrenals also secrete testosterone; GH rises with exercise.
[1:1:50] - **Kidney stones** are mostly **calcium oxalate**.
[1:1:51] - **Kidney:** organ of **excretion**; filters **urea** (nitrogenous waste) – kidney failure → urea builds up in blood (dialysis/dialyzer); **albumin in urine** and **Bright's disease** (nephritis) = kidney damage; urine yellow from **urochrome**; path **kidneys → ureters → bladder → urethra**; kidneys ≈ Malpighian tubules of insects.
[1:1:52] - **Down's syndrome** (trisomy 21): short stature, **furrowed tongue**, mental retardation – early ageing is not a listed feature; **Wilson's disease** = copper build-up (speech, movement, fluid build-up; not night blindness).
[1:1:53] - **Animal names:** *Physalia* – **Portuguese man-of-war**; *Taenia* – **tapeworm**; *Fasciola* – **liver fluke**; *Scypha* = **Sycon** (sponge).
[1:1:54] - **Lithotripsy** = breaking **kidney stones** by shock waves.
[1:1:55] - **Unicellular:** **Euglena, Amoeba, Paramecium** (protists), yeast, bacteria.
[1:1:56] - **Cytokinesis:** animal cells – **cleavage furrow**; plant cells – **cell plate** (the main plant–animal difference in cell division).
[1:1:57] - **Gizzard** grinds food in earthworms (red worms) and birds; crop stores it.
[1:1:58] - **Mammals:** hair, **four-chambered heart**, mammary glands; monotremes (platypus) lay eggs; whales and dolphins are aquatic.
[1:1:59] - **Cowper's (bulbourethral) glands** = male **reproductive** accessory glands (not intestine, not endocrine); epididymis also male; fallopian tube female; loop of Henle – kidney.
[1:1:60] - **Transfusion:** O gives to all; A → A, AB; B → B, AB; AB receives from all, so an A patient takes A or O; child of A × O parents can be A or O.
[1:1:61] - **Eye:** light enters through the **cornea** (thin transparent membrane, most of the focusing) → pupil → lens → **retina** (light turned into nerve impulses; image **real, inverted, diminished**).
[1:1:62] - **Cell organelles:** **mitochondria** – cellular respiration (ATP); ribosomes – protein synthesis; lysosomes – hydrolytic 'suicide bags'; **peroxisomes** – **oxidative enzymes** (catalase), remove toxic H2O2. **Robert Hooke** (1665) discovered the cell.
[1:1:63] - **Immunity:** active = own antibodies (infection, vaccine); passive = ready-made antibodies (**snake anti-venom**); **colostrum** = first milk, rich in antibodies; vestigial organs in man – **appendix**, **ear muscles**.
[1:1:64] - **Water** is the largest component of the cell (~70–90%).
[1:1:65] - **Hooves** – ungulates (deer, zebra, horse, cattle); **jackal** (dog family) has clawed paws.
[1:1:66] - **Blood cells:** RBC carry O2 (haemoglobin, most numerous ~5 million/mm³); **WBC** fight infection (the body's **'soldiers'**, ~7,000/mm³); platelets clot blood (~2.5 lakh/mm³) – so RBC > platelets > WBC.
[1:1:67] - **Blood** is a fluid **connective tissue**.
[1:1:68] - **Nervous system:** reflex action – **spinal cord**; **cerebrum** (forebrain) = largest part, thinking and memory; cerebellum – balance; medulla – heartbeat; pinna is the ear; neurons show **excitability and conductivity**; **sodium** ions carry nerve signals.
[1:1:69] - **Acid-base balance:** blood pH held at 7.35–7.45 by buffers – **bicarbonate**, **phosphate** (keys: phosphorus) and proteins – plus lungs and kidneys.
[1:1:70] - **Fingerprints** are unique – **not identical even in identical/Siamese twins**.
[1:1:71] - **Ascites** = fluid in the **peritoneal (abdominal) cavity**; hydrothorax – pleural cavity; hydroamnion – excess amniotic fluid; oedema – tissues.
[1:1:72] - **Lysosomes** = the '**suicidal bags**' – their enzymes can digest the cell itself when it is damaged.
[1:1:73] - **Liver functions:** secretes **bile**, **metabolises drugs** and toxins, regulates blood chemicals (glucose, proteins), stores **glycogen**, makes urea.
[1:1:74] - **Eye lens** forms a **real, inverted** image on the retina.
[1:1:75] - **Cellulose** is not digested by humans (no cellulase), though **diastase/amylase** is present.
[1:1:76] - **Blood** takes **oxygen** to all parts of the body.
[1:1:77] - **Sex hormones and reproduction:** female – estrogens (**estrone**), progesterone; male – testosterone from **Leydig cells**; **LH surge → ovulation**; **corpus luteum** (ovary) → progesterone; ovary = primary female sex organ; cervix is female; male accessory ducts/glands = **maturation and motility** of sperms; sperms fertile **1–2 days**; **tubectomy** (female) / vasectomy (male); male child = **Y from father + X from mother**.
[1:1:78] - **Brain controls both** the **heartbeat** (medulla) and **body balance** (cerebellum).
[1:1:79] - **Multiplication rate:** bacteria (~20 min), viruses, fungi multiply fast; **worms** reproduce **slowly**.
[1:1:80] - **Asexual reproduction** needs **one parent of either sex** (budding, fission, fragmentation) – no gametes.
[1:1:81] - **Skeleton:** adult **206 bones** (~200 at age 20); **7 cervical vertebrae**; ear ossicles malleus, incus, stapes (femur = thigh); bone and tooth enamel = **calcium phosphate**; **tendons** (collagen) join muscle to bone, ligaments bone to bone.
[1:1:82] - **Reflex arc path:** receptor → sensory (afferent) neuron → **spinal cord** → motor (efferent) neuron → effector.
[1:1:83] - **Vasopressin (ADH):** holds water in the kidney; **alcohol suppresses ADH** → more urine; **ADH deficiency** → **diabetes insipidus** (much dilute urine, great thirst).
[1:1:84] - **Satiety and thirst centres** are in the **hypothalamus**.
[1:1:85] - **Liver detoxification:** breaks down **alcohol** and drugs; makes bile (no enzymes – bile only **emulsifies fats**).
