[1:1:86] - **Viruses:** grow only in **living cells**, not synthetic media; spread by air, water, contact and vectors.
[1:1:87] - **Mollusca** (soft body, shell): **snails**, Pila, octopus, **Dentalium (tusk shell)**, Chiton. **Arthropoda** (jointed legs): **crabs**, insects, spiders. **Annelida** = **segmented** worms (earthworm, leech).
[1:1:88] - **Oviparous** (lay eggs): frogs, most reptiles, birds; rabbit, mouse, squirrel are viviparous mammals.
[1:1:89] - **Skeleton:** adult **206 bones**; skull **22** (28 with the 6 ear ossicles, 29 with hyoid); smallest bone **stapes** (middle ear); long bones are **hollow** (marrow cavity); bones joined by **ligaments**; humerus, carpals, talus (**astragalus**), atlas are all human bones.
[1:1:90] - **Gout:** high **uric acid** in blood → urate crystals in joints.
[1:1:91] - **Whales** (mammals) breathe with **lungs** through a blowhole, not gills.
[1:1:92] - **Digestion:** starts in the **buccal cavity** – salivary **amylase** (first enzyme) digests starch; oesophagus = food pipe; bile made in liver, **stored in gall bladder**; bile and pancreatic ducts open into the **duodenum**; water absorbed in **renal tubules and large intestine**; sweat = water + salt.
[1:1:93] - **Largest brain relative to body size** (options asked): **ant**; largest absolute brain – sperm whale.
[1:1:94] - **Crossing over** occurs at **pachytene** of prophase I (chiasmata seen at diplotene).
[1:1:95] - **Artificial insemination** = only **semen (sperm)** is placed in the female tract; fertilisation happens inside (IVF puts a fertilised egg).
[1:1:96] - **Bat:** a **mammal** (warm-blooded; wings **analogous** to bird wings as organs of flight), navigates by **echolocation (ultrasound)**; popularly called "blind" though most bats can see.
[1:1:97] - **Viagra** = **sildenafil citrate** (a PDE-5 inhibitor).
[1:1:98] - Sperm meets ovum in the **fallopian tube** (not cervix or uterus).
[1:1:99] - **Coelenterata (Cnidaria):** **Adamsia** (sea anemone), **Pennatula** (sea pen), Hydra, Aurelia.
[1:1:100] - **Nerves:** **31 pairs** of spinal nerves, 12 pairs of cranial nerves; **reflex arc** = receptor → sensory neuron → spinal cord → motor neuron → effector (an automatic response = **reflex**).
[1:1:101] - **Proteins are synthesised on ribosomes of the rough ER**, not in the cell membrane or Golgi (Golgi packages them).
[1:1:102] - **Snakes** have no external ear or eardrum – **'stone-deaf'** to airborne sound, sense ground vibrations.
[1:1:103] - **Not genetic:** **night blindness** and **xerophthalmia** (vitamin A deficiency); **genetic:** albinism, haemophilia, colour blindness, Down's syndrome.
[1:1:104] - **Brain scans:** EEG, MEG, MRI, CT – not ultrasound (bone blocks it). **CT** combines many X-ray images into 3-D cross-sections and is **painless**.
[1:1:105] - **Taxonomic hierarchy (higher → lower):** Kingdom → **Phylum → Class → Order → Family → Genus** → Species; order = group of related families. Aristotle first classified animals by **habitat**; 'meiosis' coined by **Farmer and Moore** (1905).
[1:1:106] - **Ultrasonic waves** also kill insects/bacteria, clean clothes, treat diseases and run automatic-door sensors.
[1:1:107] - **Smooth ER:** **lipid and steroid-hormone synthesis**, detoxification, transport; **protein synthesis** is on ribosomes of **rough ER**.
[1:1:108] - **Zoology** studies animals, **living and dead** (fossils); botany studies plants.
[1:1:109] - **Blood pressure** is **greater than atmospheric pressure**.
[1:1:110] - **Cross (plus) sign** = **first aid** symbol.
[1:1:111] - **Analogous organs:** different structure and origin, **same function** (bird and insect wings); homologous – same structure, different function.
[1:1:112] - **Audible range** 20 Hz–20 kHz; above = ultrasound, below = infrasound.
[1:1:113] - **Connective-tissue cells:** **adipocytes** – fat; chondrocytes – cartilage; osteocytes – bone; reticulocytes – young RBCs.
[1:1:114] - **Tooth decay** begins when mouth **pH < 5.5**; tears contain **lysozyme**, which kills bacteria.
[1:1:115] - **Insects breathe through tracheae opening at spiracles**; fish – gills; earthworm – skin.
[1:1:116] - **Circumvallation** = **ingestion** in Amoeba (pseudopodia encircle prey).
[1:1:117] - Mitochondria have **70S ribosomes** and circular DNA, so they make **some of their own proteins**.
[1:1:118] - **Muscles:** skeletal (striated, voluntary – **biceps**); smooth (involuntary – ureters, iris, bronchi, gut); cardiac (heart). Strongest muscle by force = **masseter (jaw)**.
[1:1:119] - **Body temperature** is regulated by the **hypothalamus** (the body's thermostat).
[1:1:120] - **Body numbers:** temperature **37 °C** (98.6 °F); heart rate **72/min**; ATP → ADP releases energy; **lacrimal gland** makes tears; **melatonin falls in bright light**; **pacemaker** device regulates the heartbeat; artificial muscle research targets the failing **heart**.
[1:1:121] - **Herbivores** exist among turtles (green turtle), fish (parrotfish) and marine mammals (dugong, manatee); some **snakes are viviparous** (boas, sea snakes).
[1:1:122] - **Fertilisation** in the **fallopian tube (oviduct)** – ampullary region; the embryo implants in the uterus; the embryo's heart forms and begins beating at about the **4th week** (~day 22).
[1:1:123] - Maximum absorption of nutrients into blood: **small intestine** (villi).
[1:1:124] - **Cell counts:** RBC (~5 million/mm³) > platelets (1.5–4 lakh/mm³) > WBC (4,000–11,000/mm³); the blood has more platelets than WBC.
[1:1:125] - **Big cats:** *Panthera pardus* – **leopard**; *P. tigris* – tiger; *P. leo* – lion; 'panther' is a general name (black panther = melanistic leopard).
[1:1:126] - **Genes** = segments of **DNA**, a **nucleic acid**.
[1:1:127] - **Evolution:** **natural selection** (Darwin) is the main mechanism; mutation supplies variation; acquired characters are not inherited.
[1:1:128] - **Evolutionary order of vertebrates:** fishes → amphibians → reptiles → birds → **mammals** (most advanced).
[1:1:129] - **Connective tissues:** areolar, adipose, bone, cartilage, blood; **cardiac muscle is muscular tissue**.
[1:1:130] - **Lungs** of an adult man weigh about **0.9–1.3 kg** together (keys: 0.91 kg).
[1:1:131] - **Lysosome** = storehouse of **digestive enzymes**; ribosome – protein synthesis; desmosome – cell junction; vacuole – sap storage.
[1:1:132] - Light is turned into **nerve impulses** at the **retina** (rods and cones).
[1:1:133] - **Honey bees** are trained to sniff out **landmines** (explosive vapours).
[1:1:134] - **Tissue** = group of similar cells doing one function; tissues → organ → organ system.
[1:1:135] - **Peacock = *Pavo cristatus*** (national bird).
[1:1:136] - **Growth hormone** (pituitary): excess in childhood → **gigantism**, in adults → acromegaly; deficiency → dwarfism.
[1:1:137] - **Largest part of the brain:** **cerebrum**.
[1:1:138] - **Male frogs croak** louder using **vocal sacs** (resonating sound box); females lack them.
[1:1:139] - **Bile** has **no digestive enzymes**; it only **emulsifies fats** – so the liver aids fat digestion without fat-digesting enzymes.
[1:1:140] - **Humerus, carpals, astragalus (= talus) and atlas** are all bones of the human body.
[1:1:141] - **Cell** = basic structural and functional unit of life; **ATP** = the cell's energy currency (→ ADP releases energy).
[1:1:142] - **'Thermostat' of the body = hypothalamus**; cerebellum – balance; hippocampus – memory; frontal lobe – reasoning.
[1:1:143] - **Ligaments** – elastic tissue joining **bone to bone**; tendons join muscle to bone.
[1:1:144] - **Spiders, scorpions, ticks = arachnids** (8 legs); insects have **6 legs**, 3 body parts.
[1:1:145] - **Lysozyme** in **tears** destroys bacteria (options may misspell it 'lysosome').
[1:1:146] - **Monogamous** animal among common options: **wolf** (pair-bonds for life); walrus, seal, deer are polygynous.
[1:1:147] - **True fish:** flying fish, seahorse, shark; **not fish:** silverfish (insect), jellyfish (cnidarian), cuttlefish (mollusc).
[1:1:148] - **Eye:** **cataract** = clouding of the **lens**; **eye donation** uses the **cornea**; myopia = eye defect (concave lens).
[1:1:149] - **Nucleolus** = site of **rRNA synthesis and ribosome formation** (inside the nucleus).
[1:1:150] - **Cockroach** – open circulation, **colourless ('white') haemolymph** (no haemoglobin), 13-chambered heart.
[1:1:151] - **Branches:** **ornithology** – birds; **genetics** – inheritance; **ecology** – environment; **palaeobiology/palaeontology** – fossils.
[1:1:152] - **DNA outside the nucleus:** only mitochondria and chloroplasts (not centrioles, Golgi or lysosomes).
[1:1:153] - **Amoeba** digests food in a **food vacuole** (pseudopodia only engulf it).
[1:1:154] - **Amnion** – fluid sac that protects the embryo from **desiccation** and shock (reptiles, birds, mammals = amniotes).
[1:1:155] - **BMD (bone mineral density) test** (DEXA) diagnoses **osteoporosis**.
[1:1:156] - **Long fasting:** most books key the **kidney** as the organ most affected.
[1:1:157] - **Pearl** = **calcium carbonate (nacre)** made by **molluscs** (oysters); **lac** = secretion of the lac insect.
[1:1:158] - **Metformin** = oral **anti-diabetic**; zolpidem – sleep aid, promethazine – antihistamine, hydralazine – blood pressure.
[1:1:159] - **Laparoscopy:** small cuts + camera inside the tummy/pelvis; usually under **general anaesthesia**.
[1:1:160] - **Aerobic respiration:** glucose + O2 → **CO2 + H2O + energy** (mitochondria); anaerobic → lactic acid or ethanol.
[1:1:161] - **King cobra** is the **only snake that builds a nest** (of leaves) for its eggs; the female guards it.
[1:1:162] - **Drug sources:** poppy – **morphine**; bacteria (*Streptomyces*) – **tetracycline**; cinchona bark – **quinine**; *Penicillium* fungus – **penicillin**.
[1:1:163] - **Invertebrates:** snail, octopus (molluscs), crab, prawn (arthropods), worms; vertebrates: fish, amphibians, reptiles, birds, mammals.
[1:1:164] - **Haemoglobin:** iron-containing red pigment of RBC that **binds oxygen** and gives blood its red colour; it gives no immunity.
[1:1:165] - **Bacteriophage** = **virus that infects bacteria**.
[1:1:166] - **ABO inheritance:** AB × O parents have only **A or B** children – an O child cannot be theirs.
[1:1:167] - **Mammals:** warm-blooded, hair, mammary glands – **bat, whale, man**; fishes are not; **shark** (Pisces), snakes and lizards are cold-blooded.
[1:1:168] - **Whale** = mammal (viviparous, suckles young) but almost **hairless** (only a few bristles).
[1:1:169] - **Pituitary:** pea-sized – the smallest of the common glands; single, **not paired**; master gland controlling other endocrine glands; the **parathyroid** works independent of it (PTH follows blood calcium).
[1:1:170] - Human body temperature stays ~**37 °C** in all seasons (warm-blooded) – it neither falls in winter nor rises in summer.
[1:1:171] - **Blood facts:** veins hold ~70% of blood; cells ~45% of volume; WBC made in bone marrow and lymph nodes; **platelets outnumber WBC**.
[1:1:172] - **Chondrichthyes** (sharks, rays): cartilaginous skeleton, **placoid scales**, **no air bladder**, **notochord persistent throughout life**.
[1:1:173] - An **automatic response** to a stimulus = **reflex**.
[1:1:174] - **Giant panda** = bear family (**Ursidae**).
[1:1:175] - **Analgesics** = **pain** relievers (aspirin, paracetamol non-narcotic; morphine narcotic).
[1:1:176] - **Alzheimer's disease:** plaques of **amyloid-beta**, cut from **amyloid precursor protein (APP)**, plus tau tangles.
[1:1:177] - **Skin** = the **largest organ** of the body; **liver** = largest **gland** (internal organ).
[1:1:178] - **Renal blood flow** ≈ **1,200 mL (cc) per minute**.
[1:1:179] - **Snake fangs** = modified **maxillary teeth** (upper jaw).
[1:1:180] - **Small intestine (longest to shortest):** **ileum** (~3.5 m) > jejunum (~2.5 m) > duodenum (~25 cm); complete digestion and most absorption happen here.
[1:1:181] - **Eating disorders:** **bulimia** – cannot stop eating (binges); **anorexia nervosa** – refuses food.
[1:1:182] - **Albuminuria** (albumin in urine) signals **kidney** malfunction (damaged glomeruli hold back albumin normally).
[1:1:183] - **Plant drugs:** coca leaves – cocaine; *Claviceps* (ergot) – LSD; *Cannabis* – bhang, hashish; **peyote cactus – mescaline**; tea leaves – caffeine.
[1:1:184] - **Amoeba, Paramecium, Euglena** = **Protista** (unicellular eukaryotes); Monera holds prokaryotes only.
[1:1:185] - **Semi-autonomous organelles:** **mitochondria and chloroplasts** (own DNA and ribosomes); ER is not.
[1:1:186] - **Oldest organisms:** **blue-green algae (cyanobacteria)**, ~3.5 billion years (stromatolites); first life = prokaryotes.
[1:1:187] - **Human ancestors (old → recent):** Java man, Peking man (*H. erectus*), Neanderthal, **Cro-Magnon** (early modern *H. sapiens*, latest).
[1:1:188] - **Pleiotropy** = one gene controls **several characters**; polygenic = many genes, one trait.
[1:1:189] - **Breathing control:** fall in blood O2 / rise in CO2 → **breathing rate increases** (medulla respiratory centre); mammals respire by **lungs**; combined lung weight ~0.9–1.3 kg.
[1:1:190] - **ECG** records the **heart's** electrical activity (EEG – brain, EMG – muscles).
[1:1:191] - **Hydrolytic enzymes** are packed in **lysosomes** (budded from the Golgi), which digest worn-out organelles.
[1:1:192] - **Blood pH** of a normal person = **7.35–7.45**.
[1:1:193] - **Synovial fluid** lubricates and cushions movable **joints**.
[1:1:194] - A normal human body cell has **46 chromosomes (23 pairs)**: 44 autosomes + 2 sex chromosomes.
[1:1:195] - **Semi-autonomous organelles:** **chloroplasts** and mitochondria have their own DNA and ribosomes and divide on their own – the 'cell within a cell'.
[1:1:196] - **Glycogen** = storage carbohydrate of liver and muscles; insulin (a **hormone**, beta cells) controls **carbohydrate** metabolism.
[1:1:197] - **Liver and skeletal muscle** store **glycogen** as the energy reserve (pepsinogen = inactive pepsin).
[1:1:198] - **Fungi:** eukaryotic, heterotrophic, unicellular (yeast) or multicellular; cell wall of **chitin**, not cellulose.
[1:1:199] - **Small intestine:** site of **complete digestion** of carbohydrates, proteins and fats and of most absorption of nutrients into blood (villi); the large intestine mainly absorbs water.
[1:1:200] - **Menopause:** ovulation stops → **no progesterone**; oestrogen falls sharply but is still made in small amounts.
[1:1:201] - **Rough ER** is rough because of **ribosomes** on its surface (protein synthesis).
[1:1:202] - The embryo's **heart starts beating** at about the **4th week** (~day 22).
[1:1:203] - **Amniocentesis** – amniotic fluid test that can show **foetal sex** (misuse banned, PCPNDT Act) and genetic disorders.
[1:1:204] - **Gout:** uric acid (end product of **purine** breakdown from **nucleic acids**) builds up in joints – patients limit purine-rich food (organ meat, some seafood).
[1:1:205] - **Kidney** is the most commonly **transplanted organ** (living or deceased donor).
[1:1:206] - **Hypotonic** outside → cell **swells**; hypertonic → shrinks (plasmolysis); isotonic → no change.
[1:1:207] - **Egg-laying mammals (monotremes):** **platypus and echidna**.
[1:1:208] - **RADAR** = **radio waves** (aircraft, weather); **SONAR** = **ultrasonic** sound waves under water (**navigators**, submarines, ships, depth); **LIDAR** = laser light.
[1:1:209] - **Apoptosis** = programmed cell death (genetic control); **necrosis** = accidental death by injury.
[1:1:210] - **Colour blindness:** X-linked recessive like haemophilia; colour-blind father × normal (non-carrier) mother → **no colour-blind child**, all daughters carriers.
[1:1:211] - **Antipyretics** (paracetamol) bring down **fever**; antibiotics kill bacteria; antiseptics are used on living tissue.
[1:1:212] - **Nucleoid** = membrane-less nuclear region of **prokaryotes**.
[1:1:213] - **Monera** = bacteria (and cyanobacteria); mushrooms and yeast are fungi, moss a bryophyte.
[1:1:214] - **Nephron** = the structural and functional (excretory) unit of the **kidney** (~1 million per kidney).
[1:1:215] - **Branches:** **cytology** – cells; histology – tissues; taxonomy – classification; physiology – functions.
[1:1:216] - **Dehydration:** a dehydrated person must not drink **sea water** – its salt draws still more water out of the cells; tea, coffee and alcohol are diuretics but still supply water.
[1:1:217] - **Levels of organisation:** cellular aggregate – sponges (**Spongilla**, Sycon); tissue – coelenterates (**Obelia**); organ – flatworms (**Fasciola**); organ system – annelids (**Pheretima**) onward.
[1:1:218] - **Retinal image:** **smaller than the object and inverted** (real).
[1:1:219] - **Bonds:** amino acids – **peptide bond**; sugars – glycosidic; nucleotides – phosphodiester; fats – ester.
[1:1:220] - **Darwin's natural selection:** **overproduction, struggle for existence, variation, survival of the fittest** – all are parts of it.
[1:1:221] - **Glycolysis** (cytoplasm): glucose → 2 **pyruvic acid**, **net gain 2 ATP** – the only ATP of anaerobic respiration.
[1:1:222] - **Hepatocytes** = the main cells of the **liver** (hepar = liver); neurons – brain; cardiomyocytes – heart.
[1:1:223] - **Cold-blooded** (poikilothermic): fish (**shark**), amphibians, reptiles; dolphins, whales and porpoises are warm-blooded mammals.
[1:1:224] - **Kidney flow:** blood in by the **renal artery**, cleaned blood out by the **renal vein**; filtrate from Bowman's capsule passes the tubules where **glucose** and much water are **reabsorbed**; renal blood flow ~**1,200 mL/min**; kidney stones are mostly **calcium oxalate**.
[1:1:225] - **Glycogen** = the storage carbohydrate of animals ('animal starch'); plants store **starch**.
[1:1:226] - **Haemoglobin:** red, iron-containing pigment of RBCs; carries **O2** and some **CO2** (carbamino-Hb); iron also in **myoglobin** (muscle), magnesium in chlorophyll.
[1:1:227] - **Strongest muscle:** the **masseter** of the **jaw**.
[1:1:228] - **Long bones** (leg bones) are **hollow** – marrow cavity inside.
[1:1:229] - **Sex of the child is fixed by the father's sperm** (X or Y); the mother gives only X.
[1:1:230] - **Mammals respire by lungs** (fish – gills, insects – trachea).
[1:1:231] - **Social insects** (ants, honey bees, termites) live in **colonies** with division of labour.
[1:1:232] - **Heart chambers:** fish **2** (Scoliodon); amphibians (frog, **salamander**) and most reptiles **3**; crocodile, birds, mammals **4**.
[1:1:233] - **Wall lizard (gecko)** climbs walls and ceilings with **adhesive pads (setae) on its digits**.
[1:1:234] - **Heart attack:** chest pain, heavy sweating, nausea, breathlessness (headache not typical); the first hour = **'golden hour'** for treatment; heart muscle is fed by the **coronary arteries**; **ECG** records the heart's electrical activity.
[1:1:235] - **Neutralising chyme:** NCERT – **bile juice** makes the acidic food from the stomach alkaline for pancreatic enzymes (pancreatic bicarbonate also helps).
[1:1:236] - **Jellyfish** – phylum **Cnidaria** (coelenterates; stinging cnidocytes); sponges – Porifera; comb jellies – Ctenophora.
[1:1:237] - **Hibernation** = winter dormancy with **reduced metabolism**; aestivation = summer dormancy.
[1:1:238] - **SONAR = Sound Navigation And Ranging** – ultrasound echoes locate objects under water.
[1:1:239] - **Frog (amphibian):** adults breathe by **lungs and skin**; **tadpoles have gills**.
[1:1:240] - **Wrong pair to spot:** Cowper's gland – intestine (it is a male reproductive gland).
[1:1:241] - **Cornea** = the thin transparent membrane at the front of the eye through which light enters.
[1:1:242] - **ECG** = graph of the **electrical activity of the heart**.
[1:1:243] - **Colour blindness** (X-linked recessive): affected mother × normal father → **all sons colour-blind**, **daughters normal carriers**.
[1:1:244] - **Haemoglobin:** carries O2, **contains iron**, gives **red colour**; it gives **no immunity** (that is WBC).
[1:1:245] - **Evolutionary order:** fish → amphibians (salamander) → reptiles (python) → mammals (kangaroo) / birds.
[1:1:246] - **Porifera (sponges):** **Sycon**, **Spongilla** (freshwater), **Euspongia** (bath sponge).
[1:1:247] - **Eye donation** – the donor's **cornea** is transplanted (keratoplasty).
[1:1:248] - **Plant vs animal cell:** plant – **cellulose wall**, plastids, **large central vacuole**; animal – no wall, small vacuoles; **both have a plasma membrane**.
[1:1:249] - **Plasma membrane** = lipid bilayer + proteins, **no nucleic acid**; nucleolus (rRNA), ribosome (rRNA) and chloroplast (own DNA) contain nucleic acids.
[1:1:250] - **Barr body** = inactive X chromosome; **absent in Turner syndrome (XO)** and normal males; present in Klinefelter (XXY), two in triple-X.
[1:1:251] - **DNA-containing organelles: nucleus, mitochondria, chloroplasts** (semi-autonomous); Golgi, ribosomes, plasma membrane have none.
[1:1:252] - **Plant vs animal cell:** only plant cells have a **cellulose cell wall** (all plant cells), plastids/chloroplasts (green cells only) and a large central vacuole.
[1:1:253] - **Fish internal ear** – chiefly an organ of **balance (equilibrium)**; the lateral line senses water movement.
[1:1:254] - **Prokaryotic cells** (bacteria) have **no membrane-bound organelles** – no mitochondria, nucleus or ER – but do have a cell wall, plasma membrane and 70S ribosomes.
[1:1:255] - **Mammoth** = extinct relative of the **elephant** (Proboscidea).
[1:1:256] - **Animals:** no cell wall, so **no cellulose**; heterotrophic, no chlorophyll, mostly mobile.
[1:1:257] - **Antibodies** are made by **lymphocytes** (B cells / plasma cells in lymph nodes and spleen); the thymus matures T lymphocytes.
[1:1:258] - **Angle of vision:** keys give **60°**; each eye's field reaches ~60° nasally/upward and ~90–100° to the side.
[1:1:259] - **Metastasis** = spread of **cancer cells** via blood or lymph to other organs.
[1:1:260] - **Hypothalamus** = **basal part of the diencephalon** (forebrain).
[1:1:261] - **O2 is used directly only in the electron transport chain** (final electron acceptor); glycolysis and fermentation are anaerobic.
[1:1:262] - **Adam's apple** = the projecting **thyroid cartilage of the larynx** (voice box), larger in males after puberty.
[1:1:263] - **Chromosome number is constant for a species** (man 46); meiosis halves it in gametes and fertilisation restores it.
[1:1:264] - **DNA:** hereditary material of living organisms; **double helix, antiparallel** strands; also in **mitochondria**; not all DNA codes for protein.
[1:1:265] - **Four-chambered heart:** mammals, birds and **crocodiles**; other reptiles 3, fish 2.
[1:1:266] - **Adult human skeleton = 206 bones.**
[1:1:267] - **Smallest bone:** **stapes** (middle ear).
[1:1:268] - **Grasshopper 'ears'** (tympanal organs) lie on the **first abdominal segment** – 'stomach' (abdomen) in exam options.
[1:1:269] - **Carbon** = backbone (principal structural element) of biomolecules; **oxygen** is the most abundant element by mass in the body.
[1:1:270] - **Body symmetry:** **sponges** mostly **asymmetrical**; coelenterates, ctenophores, adult echinoderms – **radial**.
[1:1:271] - **Tissue** = a group of **similar cells** doing a common function; **blood** is a fluid connective tissue (plasma + formed elements).
[1:1:272] - **Endoscope** = a **narrow telescope**-like optical-fibre tube to see inside the body.
[1:1:273] - **Jarvik-7** = **artificial heart** (Robert Jarvik, 1982).
[1:1:274] - **Acquired immunity:** pathogen-specific, has memory; **primary response low intensity**, secondary high; active vs passive; humoral (B cells) + cell-mediated (T cells). **Innate:** PMN leucocytes, NK cells, monocytes.
[1:1:275] - **Chromosomes:** normal human body cell = **46 (23 pairs)**; gametes 23.
[1:1:276] - **Blood:** ~**5–6 L** in an adult; pH **7.35–7.45**; a fluid **connective tissue**; carries O2 to all parts; BP is **greater than atmospheric pressure**.
[1:1:277] - **Smallest bird:** **bee hummingbird** (Cuba, ~5–6 cm); largest – ostrich.
[1:1:278] - **Respiration** is **exothermic** (releases energy); photosynthesis is endothermic.
[1:1:279] - **Coronary arteries** supply blood to the **heart** muscle.
[1:1:280] - **Kangaroo rat** (desert) never drinks water; excretes **almost solid, highly concentrated urine**.
[1:1:281] - **Mouth:** **salivary amylase (ptyalin/diastase)** starts **starch** digestion; humans have no cellulase, so **cellulose is not digested**.
[1:1:282] - **Paramecium** is a unicellular protozoan; Spirogyra (alga), Hydra and most fungi are multicellular.
[1:1:283] - **Iris vs pupil:** the **iris** (coloured muscular diaphragm) changes the pupil's size and so **controls the light entering**; the **pupil** is the opening (NCERT: 'the pupil regulates and controls the amount of light').
[1:1:284] - **Amniocentesis** (amniotic fluid cells, chromosome test) confirms **Down's syndrome** (trisomy 21); ultrasound only screens.
## SUB 2 | Everyday Chemistry – Colloids, Solutions, Polymers and Organic Reactions
[1:2:0] TABLE head: Colloid | Dispersed in | Example
  [1:2:0.0] Emulsion | Liquid in liquid | Milk, cream, mayonnaise
  [1:2:0.1] Gel | Liquid in solid | Cheese, butter, jelly
  [1:2:0.2] Sol | Solid in liquid | Paint, mud (suspension)
  [1:2:0.3] Foam / solid foam | Gas in liquid / solid | Whipped cream / pumice, foam rubber
[1:2:1] - **Solutions:** a solute **raises the boiling point** and **lowers the vapour pressure** of water and **lowers its water potential** (so "adding solute increases water potential" is false).
[1:2:2] - **Natural polymers:** wool, silk, leather (proteins), cellulose, rubber; **nylon** is synthetic (Carothers, 1935).
[1:2:3] - **Acetylene (ethyne):** **oxy-acetylene welding**; raw material for plastics (PVC via vinyl chloride); made from **calcium carbide + water** (not silicon carbide).
[1:2:4] - **Cumene process:** cumene (isopropylbenzene) → **cumene hydroperoxide**, then **dilute acid** → **phenol + acetone**.
[1:2:5] - **Ethanol + sodium → sodium ethoxide + hydrogen** (shows ethanol's weak acidity).
[1:2:6] - **Animal (bone) charcoal** decolourises raw sugar; **activated charcoal** – gas masks.
[1:2:7] # Polymers and materials
[1:2:8] - PVC – vinyl chloride; Teflon – tetrafluoroethene; neoprene – chloroprene; natural rubber – isoprene; **Bakelite** – phenol + formaldehyde.
[1:2:9] - **Nylon-2-Nylon-6** = **glycine + amino caproic acid** (biodegradable); **PHBV** = **3-hydroxybutanoic acid + 3-hydroxypentanoic acid** (biodegradable); nylon-6,6 = hexamethylenediamine + adipic acid; **Buna-S** = **1,3-butadiene + styrene**; Buna-N = 1,3-butadiene + acrylonitrile.
[1:2:10] - **Teflon** = **polytetrafluoroethylene (PTFE)**, non-stick coating. **PVC** – blood bags and dialysis tubes; **glass wool** – heat and sound insulation; **Pyrex** – heat-resistant labware; flint glass – lenses.
[1:2:11] # Carbon forms
[1:2:12] - Diamond – cutting tools; graphite – pencils, lubricant, electrodes; **coke** – fuel and **reducing agent in iron extraction**; **carbon black** – pigment in **paints**, inks, tyres; **activated charcoal** – **gas masks**; **carbon nanotubes** – **electronics, nanotechnology**; graphene – electronics, sensors (not a fuel or reducing agent).
[1:2:13] # Household chemicals
[1:2:14] TABLE head: Chemical | Fact
  [1:2:14.0] Baking soda | NaHCO3 – antacid, baking; with vinegar gives CO2
  [1:2:14.1] Washing soda | Na2CO3 – removes permanent hardness
  [1:2:14.2] Hypo | Na2S2O3 – photographic fixer
  [1:2:14.3] Bleaching powder | Calcium oxychloride – releases chlorine; water purification and bleaching; a disinfectant
  [1:2:14.4] Milk of magnesia | Mg(OH)2 antacid
[1:2:15] - **Hard water:** Ca/Mg **bicarbonates = temporary** (boiling, Clark's lime); chlorides/sulphates = **permanent** (washing soda, ion exchange, Calgon).
[1:2:16] - **Antiseptics** (boric acid, iodoform, H2O2) are safe on living tissue; **disinfectants** (phenol, bleaching powder) are **toxic to living tissue**, so used only on objects.
[1:2:17] - **Soap** is **alkaline** (harms silk and wool) and biodegradable; many **older detergents** had branched chains and were **non-biodegradable** (river foam).
[1:2:18] # Metals and alloys
[1:2:19] - Brass = Cu + Zn; **bronze = Cu + tin**; **German silver = Cu + Zn + Ni**; **fuse wire / solder = tin + lead**; **Dow metal = Mg + Al**; **nichrome** – heater coil; **tungsten** – bulb filament (highest melting point); **sodium kept in kerosene**.
[1:2:20] - **Gold:** 24 carat pure; 22 carat = **91.6%**; **18 carat = 75%**; aqua regia **3 : 1** (HCl : HNO3).
[1:2:21] - **Ruby** = Al2O3 + **chromium**; Statue of Liberty green = copper **patina**.
[1:2:22] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Chemistry p.7, p.13-14, p.27-30, p.49-50; OnlyIAS NCERT S&T Part 8 (ethanol + sodium); PMF General Science Part 7 p.54; app notes 5:1
[1:2:23] - **Salt (any non-volatile solute) in water:** boiling point **rises**, freezing point **falls** (colligative properties) – why salt is spread on icy roads.
[1:2:24] - **Shatter-proof (safety) glass** – early interlayer of **cellulose acetate/nitrate**; now PVB. Cellulose is a water-insoluble polysaccharide.
[1:2:25] - **Oil/petrol fires** cannot be put out with water (oil floats and spreads) – use **CO2**, foam or sand; electrical fires: no water either.
[1:2:26] - **Tungsten** = **very hard and ductile** (drawn into filament wire); carborundum and cast iron are hard but **brittle**.
[1:2:27] - **Lime:** **quicklime CaO** + water → **slaked lime Ca(OH)2** (lime water); limestone = CaCO3.
[1:2:28] - **Most ductile and malleable metal = gold**; best conductor of heat and electricity = silver.
[1:2:29] - **Methylpropane (isobutane), C₄H₁₀** = structural isomer of **n-butane**.
[1:2:30] - **Aspirin** = acetylsalicylic acid (Bayer, 1897), derived from **salicin of willow bark**; analgesic, antipyretic.
[1:2:31] - **Chemical effect of current:** current through a conducting liquid causes a **chemical reaction** (electrolysis, electroplating).
[1:2:32] - **Plaster of Paris** = CaSO4·½H2O = (CaSO4)2·H2O – **one water molecule per two formula units**; used by doctors (casts), artists and sculptors.
[1:2:33] - **Blood** is a natural **colloid** (sol); urea, cane sugar and NaCl form true solutions.
[1:2:34] - **Mixtures** are separated by **physical methods** (evaporation leaves salt from brine); pure substances cannot be.
[1:2:35] - **14 carat** = 14/24 ≈ **58%** gold.
[1:2:36] - **Esters** (e.g. **ethyl acetate**) have **fruity smells** – flavours and perfumes.
[1:2:37] - **Ice floats:** **hydrogen bonding** gives ice an open cage lattice → ice less dense than water (water densest at 4 °C).
[1:2:38] - **Sodium chromate (Na2CrO4)** – keys give **red** colour; the salt itself is yellow (dichromate is orange-red).
[1:2:39] - **Densest of common metals asked:** uranium (~19 g/cm³) > silver > copper > aluminium; densest element – osmium.
[1:2:40] - **Acidic salt:** **FeCl3** (strong acid + weak base) gives pH < 7; NaCl, KCl neutral.
[1:2:41] - **Centrifugation** separates **very fine insoluble particles** (cream from milk); decantation only for settling particles.
[1:2:42] - **Nylon** – first fully synthetic fibre (Carothers, 1930s), made from **coal, water and air**; strong, elastic, light.
[1:2:43] - **Metals** have **1–3 valence electrons** (Ca 2,8,8,2); 7 valence electrons = halogen (Cl 2,8,7); full shell = noble gas.
[1:2:44] - **Galvanising:** zinc is **more electropositive** than iron, so it corrodes first and protects iron (sacrificial).
[1:2:45] - **Baking powder** = baking soda + a dry acid (**tartaric acid / cream of tartar**); with moisture and heat it releases **CO2**, which leavens cakes.
[1:2:46] - **Chromatography** separates components that move at **different rates** through a medium (dyes in ink, pigments).
[1:2:47] - **Soda-acid fire extinguisher** = **sulphuric acid + sodium hydrogen carbonate** solution → CO2.
[1:2:48] - **Writing/etching on glass – hydrogen fluoride (HF)**.
[1:2:49] - **Osmium** = densest ("heaviest") metal, ~22.6 g/cm³.
[1:2:50] - **Saturated hydrocarbons** burn with a clean blue flame → **CO2 + H2O + heat**; unsaturated ones give a sooty flame.
[1:2:51] - **Melting points (options asked):** **boron** > iron (1,808 K) > silicon > aluminium.
[1:2:52] - **White phosphorus** glows in the dark by **slow oxidation** (chemiluminescence); stored under water.
[1:2:53] - **Rust** = brown hydrated iron oxide (Fe2O3·xH2O) – needs air and moisture.
[1:2:54] - **Atomic mass unit:** 1 u = **1/12 of a carbon-12 atom**.
[1:2:55] - **Baking soda on heating:** 2NaHCO3 → **Na2CO3 + H2O + CO2**; the CO2 makes cakes rise and powers the **soda-acid fire extinguisher**.
[1:2:56] - **Atomic models:** **Dalton** – atom indivisible (1808); **J.J. Thomson** – electron, **'plum pudding'** model; **Rutherford** – nucleus (gold foil); Bohr – orbits. Max electrons in a shell = **2n²** (K 2, L 8, **M 18, N 32**); **5th period has 18 elements**.
[1:2:57] - **Equivalent weight = atomic weight ÷ valency.**
[1:2:58] - **Pungent smell near urinals** = **ammonia** (bacterial breakdown of urea).
[1:2:59] - **Burning candle** = combustion – a **chemical, exothermic** change (wax melting alone is physical).
[1:2:60] - **Common salt (NaCl) is the raw material** for **NaOH, Cl2 (→ bleaching powder), baking soda and washing soda** (chlor-alkali and Solvay processes); slaked lime comes from **limestone**.
[1:2:61] - **Esters** – **sweet, fruity smell** (perfumes, flavours); made from acid + alcohol (esterification).
[1:2:62] - **Toothpaste is basic** – neutralises mouth acids (tooth decay starts below pH 5.5); enamel = calcium phosphate.
[1:2:63] - **Slaked lime Ca(OH)2** (not CaO = quicklime, CaCO3 = limestone): whitewash, and **Cl2 + slaked lime → bleaching powder**.
[1:2:64] - **Water of crystallisation:** blue vitriol CuSO4·**5H2O**, washing soda Na2CO3·**10H2O**, gypsum CaSO4·**2H2O**; **baking soda NaHCO3 has none**; plaster of Paris CaSO4·½H2O.
[1:2:65] - **Water + CO2 from air → carbonic acid**, so pH of exposed water falls slightly (rain pH ~5.6).
[1:2:66] - **Exothermic:** quicklime + water, diluting conc. H2SO4; evaporation and sublimation absorb heat (endothermic).
[1:2:67] - **Building materials:** **mortar** = cement + sand + water; concrete = cement + sand + gravel + water; **RCC** = concrete with steel bars.
[1:2:68] - **Chiral** (Greek *kheir* = **hand**) – molecule not superimposable on its mirror image.
[1:2:69] - **Quantum numbers:** n – size/energy; l – **shape**; m – **orientation**; s – **spin**.
[1:2:70] - **Nitric acid turns yellow** on standing: light decomposes it to brown **NO2**, which dissolves in it.
[1:2:71] - **Thermoplastics** (soften on heating): polythene, PVC, polystyrene, Teflon; **thermosets**: Bakelite, melamine; **elastomers**: natural rubber (isoprene), **neoprene (chloroprene)**; Dacron (terylene) – a polyester.
[1:2:72] - **Gas laws:** Boyle (PV const; V vs 1/p = **straight line**), Charles (V ∝ T), Avogadro, **Gay-Lussac's combining volumes** (simple volume ratios); **Joule's law** is about **heating by current**, not a gas law.
[1:2:73] - **Zinc + hot NaOH** → sodium zincate + **hydrogen** (combustible gas).
[1:2:74] - **Chemical change** = new substance, usually irreversible (**cooking food**, burning, rusting, curdling); dissolving, melting, dispersion of light = **physical**.
[1:2:75] - **Vulcanisation** (Charles **Goodyear**, 1839) = heating natural rubber with **sulphur** – harder, elastic, less sticky; John Dunlop – pneumatic tyre.
[1:2:76] - **Glass** = amorphous solid, called a **supercooled liquid**.
[1:2:77] - **Acetone** = nail-polish remover (volatile solvent).
[1:2:78] - **Law of constant (definite) proportions** (Proust): a compound always has the **same elements in the same mass ratio**, whatever its source.
[1:2:79] - **Kinds of glass:** **soft (soda-lime) glass** = sodium calcium silicate (windows, bottles); **hard glass** = potassium calcium silicate; **Pyrex/borosilicate** (contains **boron**) – harder and heat-resistant; **flint (lead) glass** – lenses, prisms, **electric bulbs**, optical instruments; glass has no sharp melting point (a supercooled liquid).
[1:2:80] - **Polycarbonate** – tough and transparent: **bullet-proof windows**, CDs, helmets; **polyester blends**: polycot (polyester + cotton), terrycot (terylene + cotton), polywool.
[1:2:81] - **How soap cleans:** soap lowers the **surface tension** of water; its molecules form **micelles** (hydrocarbon tail in the oily dirt, ionic head outside), an **emulsion** that is rinsed away; micelles are colloid-sized, so soap solution **scatters light** (looks cloudy).
[1:2:82] - **Galvanisation** = **zinc** coating on iron; tin plating = tin.
[1:2:83] - **Highest melting points:** **tantalum (hafnium) carbide** ~3,900 °C > **tungsten** ~3,420 °C (highest of the metals).
[1:2:84] - **Ores:** **bauxite – aluminium**; haematite, **magnetite – iron**; cinnabar – mercury; galena – lead; **amalgam** = an alloy of **mercury**.
[1:2:85] - **Bullet-proof vests** – woven **nylon**/aramid (Kevlar) fabric.
[1:2:86] - **Malleable** (beaten into sheets): gold (most), silver, aluminium; **potassium/sodium** are soft and too reactive.
[1:2:87] - **Magnetic tapes** are coated with **iron oxide** (ferric oxide / chromium dioxide).
[1:2:88] - **ns² np³** = group 15 (**nitrogen**, P); carbon 2s²2p², oxygen 2s²2p⁴, argon 3s²3p⁶.
[1:2:89] - **Glucose dissolves readily** because its –OH groups form **hydrogen bonds** with water.
[1:2:90] - Glass, not gelatine or ice cream, is the **supercooled liquid**.
[1:2:91] - **Pauli exclusion principle:** two electrons in one orbital differ only in **spin quantum number** (+½, −½).
[1:2:92] - **Isomers:** n-butane and isobutane (C4H10) = **chain isomers** (straight vs branched chain); position and functional isomers differ in group position / group.
[1:2:93] - **Tungsten** = hardest metal among common options (highest melting point; chromium is hardest pure metal by Mohs).
[1:2:94] - **Mercury** = the only metal **liquid at room temperature** (bromine the only liquid non-metal; gallium, caesium melt near 30 °C).
[1:2:95] - **Pure water pH = 7** (neutral, 25 °C).
[1:2:96] - **Ideal solution:** **ΔH mix = 0**, ΔV mix = 0, obeys Raoult's law (ΔS mix > 0).
[1:2:97] - **NaOH** in water → Na⁺ + OH⁻: **conducts and is basic**; HCl, acetic acid – acidic; methanol – does not ionise.
[1:2:98] - **Organic suffixes:** -**al** = aldehyde (propanal), -ol = alcohol, -**one** = ketone (propanone/acetone), -yne = alkyne.
[1:2:99] - **Electroplating:** passing **electric current** through a metal-salt solution deposits a thin film (gold on jewellery, chromium on taps, zinc/tin on iron).
[1:2:100] - **Water is amphoteric:** a **base** when it accepts H⁺ (HSO4⁻ + H2O → H3O⁺ + SO4²⁻), an acid when it donates.
[1:2:101] - **Benzene + chlorine:** with **Fe/FeCl3** → **chlorobenzene** (substitution); in **UV light** → benzene hexachloride (addition).
[1:2:102] - **CH4 + 2O2 → CO2 + 2H2O:** carbon oxidised (−4 → +4), hydrogen stays +1, oxygen reduced.
[1:2:103] - **Constantan** = **copper + nickel** (resistance wire); manganin = Cu + Mn + Ni.
[1:2:104] - **CO2** – fire extinguishers (non-combustible, heavier than air, cuts off oxygen).
[1:2:105] - **State functions:** U (= q + W), H, S, **G (= H − TS)**; **q and W alone are path functions**.
[1:2:106] - **Glycol** (ethylene glycol / its ethers) = **anti-freeze**; in aviation fuel it **prevents freezing** (icing).
[1:2:107] - **Basic salt:** **sodium acetate** (strong base + weak acid) gives pH > 7; NaCl neutral; CuSO4, FeCl3 acidic.
[1:2:108] - **Redox example:** 2Mg + O2 → 2MgO (Mg oxidised, O reduced); neutralisation and double displacement are not redox.
[1:2:109] - **Avogadro's hypothesis:** equal volumes of gases at the same T and P hold **equal numbers of molecules**.
[1:2:110] - **Rayon** (regenerated cellulose from wood pulp) = **artificial silk**; **acrylic** = artificial wool; **polycarbonate** – CDs; cotton – summer clothing (absorbs sweat).
[1:2:111] - **Compounds** = two or more elements combined in a fixed ratio – **pure, homogeneous** substances (not mixtures); **suspension** = heterogeneous mixture; solution = homogeneous; **glass** = an amorphous supercooled liquid, no definite melting point, not a true compound.
[1:2:112] - **Firedamp = methane** – forms an explosive mixture with air in coal mines.
[1:2:113] - **Rust** = hydrated iron(III) oxide (Fe2O3·xH2O), a **compound**; needs **iron + oxygen + moisture**; **increases weight**.
[1:2:114] - **Endothermic** = heat **absorbed** (e.g. CaCO3 → CaO + CO2 on heating, photosynthesis); exothermic = heat released.
[1:2:115] - **Periodic table:** across a period the **number of shells stays the same** (protons and electrons rise by 1); down a group shells increase.
[1:2:116] - **Sublime (solid → gas):** **dry ice**, **camphor**, iodine, naphthalene; ice melts, it does not sublime.
[1:2:117] - **Amalgam** = alloy of a metal with **mercury (Hg)** (iron forms none).
[1:2:118] - **Periodic table:** elements of a **group** have the same valence electrons → **similar chemical properties**; a period – consecutive atomic numbers.
[1:2:119] - **Carbonates + HCl → CO2** (limestone, chalk, marble = CaCO3); **quicklime CaO** gives no CO2.
[1:2:120] - **Formula from valency:** Na2X → X²⁻; with Al³⁺ → **Al2X3** (cross the valencies).
[1:2:121] - **Tungsten steel** (high-speed tool steel) – hard, abrasion-resistant at high temperature; chromium → stainless steel.
[1:2:122] - **Analgesics** – pain relief; **antibiotics** – treat infections; **antiseptics** – stop microbes growing on living tissue.
[1:2:123] - **Physical vs chemical change:** **evaporation**, melting, dissolving = physical; **curdling of milk, ripening, burning, rusting** = chemical.
[1:2:124] - **Caprolactam** is the **monomer** of nylon-6, not a polymer (nylon, Teflon, polystyrene are polymers).
[1:2:125] - **Tyndall effect** – colloids **scatter light** (beam visible); true solutions do not.
[1:2:126] - **Manganese dioxide** decolourises glass (removes the **green** tint from iron); SiO2 = the glass former.
[1:2:127] - **Electroplating:** object to be plated = **cathode**; plating metal (pure Ni, Cr, Ag) = **anode**; electrolyte = a salt of that metal (e.g. nickel sulphate).
[1:2:128] - **Soap** = **saponification** of vegetable oil/fat with NaOH (hard) or KOH (soft); mineral oils cannot be saponified.
[1:2:129] - **PMMA (perspex, acrylic glass)** from **methyl methacrylate** – light but strong, shatter-resistant plastic.
[1:2:130] - **Mixed oxides:** Pb3O4, Fe3O4, Mn3O4 hold the metal in two oxidation states; **Fe2O3 has only Fe(+3)**.
[1:2:131] - **Polyester** (terylene) fabric **resists wrinkling**; cotton, flax, silk crease.
[1:2:132] - **Mercury (Hg)** – the only **metal liquid at room temperature** (bromine – the liquid non-metal; gallium, caesium melt near it).
[1:2:133] - **Cement** hardens by **hydration** (calcium silicates and aluminates + water); **gypsum** is added to slow setting.
[1:2:134] - **Glycerol → nitroglycerine** (dynamite, Nobel); TNT from toluene.
[1:2:135] - **Element** (e.g. silver) cannot be split by chemical means; sugar, water = compounds; air = mixture.
[1:2:136] - **Fireworks:** **magnesium** – brilliant white; sodium – yellow; strontium – red; barium – green; copper – blue.
[1:2:137] - **Equivalent weight changes with valency** (e.g. Fe: 28 as Fe²⁺, 18.6 as Fe³⁺).
[1:2:138] - **Radon** (noble gas, m.p. ~ −71 °C) has a far lower melting point than sodium (98 °C), tin (232 °C) or radium (700 °C).
[1:2:139] - **Lime water test:** CO2 + Ca(OH)2 → **CaCO3** (milky); CO, NH3 and NO2 do not do this.
[1:2:140] - **Neutrons = mass number − atomic number** (Fe-59: 33 > Cu-61: 32 > Zn-61: 31 > Zn-60: 30).
[1:2:141] - **Styrene** (aromatic) – solvent/monomer for polyester resins and **reinforced plastics** (FRP), polystyrene.
[1:2:142] - **Iron melts at ~1538 °C** (so ~1500 °C among options).
[1:2:143] - **Monoatomic:** noble gases (**helium**, neon, argon); H2 diatomic, P4 and S8 polyatomic.
[1:2:144] - **Phenyl** (phenol derivative) = household **germicide** / floor disinfectant.
[1:2:145] - **Tyndall effect** = **scattering** of light by colloidal particles (beam visible in fog, dusty room).
[1:2:146] - **Redox:** **oxidation** = gain of O / **loss of H** / loss of electrons; **reduction** = loss of O / **gain of H** / gain of electrons.
[1:2:147] - **Laughing gas = nitrous oxide (N2O)** – mild anaesthetic.
[1:2:148] - **Permutit / zeolite** = **sodium aluminium silicate**; exchanges Ca²⁺/Mg²⁺ for Na⁺ to remove **permanent hardness**.
[1:2:149] - **Burning magnesium** → white **ash** of magnesium oxide (MgO), a basic oxide.
[1:2:150] - **CO2:** **acidic oxide** (carbonic acid), turns **lime water milky** (CaCO3), **not poisonous** (asphyxiant only at high levels; CO is the poison).
[1:2:151] - **Inert gases** are **chemically unreactive** because their outer shells are full.
[1:2:152] - **Carbon** is present in **all organic compounds** (O, N not always).
[1:2:153] - **Common salt = NaCl** (Na⁺ + Cl⁻, 1 : 1); rock salt is its mineral.
[1:2:154] - **Rusting increases the weight** of iron (oxygen and water are added).
[1:2:155] - **Plaster of Paris** (calcium sulphate hemihydrate) – casts (doctors), moulds and statues (artists, sculptors).
[1:2:156] - **Oxidation number of Ni in K4[Ni(CN)4] = 0** (4 × +1 + x + 4 × −1 = 0).
[1:2:157] - **Metalloids:** **germanium**, silicon, arsenic, antimony, boron; zinc, silver, gold are metals.
[1:2:158] - **Noble gases:** He, Ne, Ar, Kr, Xe, Rn – **chemically unreactive** (full shells); hydrogen is not one.
[1:2:159] - Cadmium, germanium and tin are **solids** at room temperature; among metals only **mercury** is liquid.
[1:2:160] - **Loss of oxygen = reduction** (CuO + H2 → Cu + H2O: CuO is reduced); corrosion is slow oxidation of a metal.
[1:2:161] - **Silver tarnishes** black: Ag + **H2S** → **Ag2S**.
[1:2:162] - **Sooty flame:** unsaturated/aromatic, carbon-rich compounds (benzene, naphthalene, anthracene) burn sooty; saturated alkanes (**hexane**) burn with a clean blue flame.
[1:2:163] - **Fluorine** = most reactive halogen and most electronegative element.
[1:2:164] - **Counting covalent bonds:** C3H7Cl (chloropropane) = 2 C–C + 7 C–H + 1 C–Cl = **10**.
[1:2:165] - **Copper in moist air** gets a green coat of **basic copper carbonate** [CuCO3·Cu(OH)2]; silver tarnishes black (Ag2S).
[1:2:166] - **Noble gases in air:** argon (~0.93%), neon, helium, krypton, xenon; **radon** is radioactive (from radium decay).
[1:2:167] - **Oxides:** non-metal oxides (CO2, SO2) are **acidic**; metal oxides are **basic** (some amphoteric – ZnO, Al2O3).
[1:2:168] - **Hydrofluoric acid (HF)** dissolves/etches **glass** (SiO₂ → SiF₄) – used for **writing on glass**; stored in wax/plastic bottles.
[1:2:169] - **Lewis acid** = electron-pair **acceptor** (BF3, AlCl3, FeCl3); **Lewis base** = donor (**NH3**, H2O).
[1:2:170] - **Gypsum** = CaSO4·2H2O (calcium) → plaster of Paris on heating; **china clay** = kaolin (Al silicate); **corundum** = Al2O3; **talc** = Mg silicate.
[1:2:171] - **Orbital nodes:** angular = **l**, radial = **n − l − 1** (4d: 2 angular, 1 radial).
[1:2:172] - **Transparent soap:** soap dissolved in **ethanol**, excess solvent evaporated; floating soap – air bubbles beaten in; shaving soap – glycerol + rosin.
[1:2:173] - **Oxides:** **metallic oxides are basic**, **non-metallic oxides are acidic**; all acids give H⁺ ions and share similar properties.
[1:2:174] - **Liquid elements at room temperature:** **bromine** (non-metal) and **mercury** (metal).
[1:2:175] - **Melting points:** **tin 232 °C** < lead 327 °C < zinc 420 °C < silver 962 °C < iron ~1538 °C.
[1:2:176] - **Storage battery = lead–acid** (lead plates in dilute H2SO4).
[1:2:177] - **Three needs for rusting:** iron, **oxygen and moisture** (water).
[1:2:178] - **Real gas ≈ ideal** at **low pressure and high temperature**.
[1:2:179] - **O2− ion** has **8 valence electrons** (octet; isoelectronic with neon).
[1:2:180] - **Water molecules** are held to one another by **hydrogen bonds** (the O–H bonds inside a molecule are covalent).
[1:2:181] - **Pure substance vs mixture:** **ice** (solid water) is a compound; ice-cream, air, honey, milk are **mixtures**.
[1:2:182] - **2HgO → 2Hg + O2** on heating: mercuric oxide loses oxygen – **reduction** (Hg +2 → 0); burning Mg or S in O2 is oxidation.
[1:2:183] - **Solution** = **homogeneous** mixture of two or more components; colloids (emulsions) and suspensions are heterogeneous.
[1:2:184] - **Aufbau principle** – order of filling orbitals (lowest energy first); **Hund's rule** – single occupancy first; **Pauli** – max 2 electrons, opposite spins.
[1:2:185] - **Acids:** sour, turn **blue litmus red**; **bases** bitter, soapy, turn **red litmus blue**.
[1:2:186] - **Diatomic:** H2, N2, O2, halogens (**I2**); **monoatomic:** noble gases (He) and metals (Cu, Ba).
[1:2:187] - **Green coating on copper** = **basic copper carbonate CuCO3·Cu(OH)2** (moist air + CO2).
[1:2:188] - **Henry's law:** solubility of a gas ∝ its pressure – **soda/soft drinks sealed under high CO₂ pressure**; divers' bends.
[1:2:189] - **White phosphorus** – very **low ignition temperature** (~30 °C), catches fire in air → **stored under water**.
[1:2:190] - **Cement:** main raw material **limestone** (~75%) with clay; gypsum is added to slow setting.
[1:2:191] - **Photochromic lenses** darken in **UV light** – embedded **silver chloride (halide)** microcrystals.
[1:2:192] - **Sodium chloride (salt)** = symbol of the freedom struggle – **Dandi March / Salt Satyagraha (1930)**.
[1:2:193] - **Bakelite** (phenol + formaldehyde, Baekeland 1907) = the **first fully synthetic plastic**, thermosetting, poor conductor of heat and electricity – switches, handles.
[1:2:194] - **Antifreeze:** **ethylene glycol** in car radiators lowers (depresses) the **freezing point** of water.
[1:2:195] - **Distilled water exposed to air** dissolves CO2 → pH **slightly below 7**.
[1:2:196] - **Periodic properties:** atomic size, valency, electronegativity, ionisation energy; **radioactivity is not periodic** (nuclear property).
[1:2:197] - **Monoatomic molecule** = a single atom (noble gases He, Ne, Ar) – molecule same as atom.
[1:2:198] - **SO2** (S in +4) acts as both **reducing and oxidising agent**; H2, H2S, HI are reducing agents only.
[1:2:199] - **Dry ice = solid CO2**; it sublimes straight into gas.
[1:2:200] - **Fishy smell** = **amines** (trimethylamine); rotten egg = H2S.
[1:2:201] - **Salts (ionic):** crystal **lattice**, brittle, **high melting and boiling points**, conduct when molten or dissolved.
[1:2:202] - **Isoelectronic** = same number of electrons: **Na+, O2−**, F−, Mg2+, Al3+, Ne (10 e⁻); K+, Ca2+, Cl−, Ar (18 e⁻).
[1:2:203] - **Alum stops bleeding:** its **Al³⁺** ions coagulate colloidal blood.
[1:2:204] - **Na2O dissolves in water → NaOH** (alkali); CuO, Al2O3, Fe2O3 are insoluble.
[1:2:205] - **Magnesium sulphate (Epsom salt)** – mordant (assist) in **dyeing** and used in **leather tanning**; also laxative.
[1:2:206] - **Hot water:** pH of pure water **falls** on heating (~6.1 at 100 °C) though it stays neutral (more ionisation).
[1:2:207] - **Litmus:** acids turn blue → red; **bases and neutral salt solution** leave blue litmus blue.
[1:2:208] - **Concentration of a solution** = amount of solute per unit volume or mass of solution/solvent.
[1:2:209] - **Saturated** = only single bonds (**butane**, alkanes); **unsaturated** = double/triple bonds (alkenes like pentene, acetylene, benzene).
[1:2:210] - **Superheater (boiler) tubes:** **chromium-molybdenum (Cr-Mo) steel**; **stainless steel** = iron + **chromium** (+ nickel) – rust-free.
[1:2:211] - **Type metal** (printing types) = **lead + antimony + tin**; **Rose metal** = bismuth + lead + tin (low-melting).
[1:2:212] - **Noble gases (group 18):** He, Ne, Ar, Kr, Xe, Rn – full outer shell, inert.
[1:2:213] - **Water is a good solvent** (universal) because of its **high dielectric constant** (~80) and polarity.
[1:2:214] - **Exothermic** (heat released): burning of fuels such as natural gas, **respiration**, **composting** of vegetable matter.
[1:2:215] - **Bullet-proof jacket** – layered **fibres** (fibrous glass, nylon/aramid such as Kevlar); laminated glass = bullet-resistant windows.
[1:2:216] - **Phenolphthalein:** colourless in acid and neutral, **pink in alkali**.
[1:2:217] - **Atomic weight = equivalent weight × valency**; equivalent weight changes with valency.
[1:2:218] - **Drying agents:** **quicklime (CaO)** (dries ammonia), anhydrous CaCl2, conc. H2SO4, silica gel; gypsum and slaked lime are not.
[1:2:219] - **C–C bond length:** single (ethane, **154 pm**) > benzene (139 pm) > double (ethene, 134 pm) > triple (ethyne, 120 pm).
[1:2:220] - **Crookes glass** (cerium oxide) **absorbs UV** – protective sunglasses; Pyrex/borosilicate – heat-resistant; soda glass – windows, bottles.
[1:2:221] - **Hydrogen peroxide (H2O2):** mild antiseptic and **oxidising agent** – in two-bottle **hair dyes** it **oxidises the dye** to develop the colour; also a bleach.
[1:2:222] - **Carbon compounds treated as inorganic:** **cyanides**, carbonates, oxides of carbon, carbides.
[1:2:223] - **Zone refining (zone melting)** gives ultra-pure metals (Si, Ge for semiconductors).
[1:2:224] - **Corrosion colours:** silver → **black Ag2S** (sulphide); copper → green basic carbonate; iron → brown rust.
[1:2:225] - **Soap vs detergent:** soap forms **scum (white curdy precipitate)** with hard water and does not work in acidic water; **detergents lather even in hard water** and clean more strongly. **Soft soap** = potassium (KOH) soap; hard soap = sodium soap.
[1:2:226] - **Sublimation** separates **iodine** (also camphor, NH4Cl, naphthalene) from non-subliming solids such as KCl or sand.
## SUB 3 | Plant and Animal Nutrition
[1:3:0] - **Essential plant micronutrients:** boron, zinc, copper, iron, manganese, molybdenum, chlorine, nickel – **sodium is not essential** for most plants.
[1:3:1] - **Boron deficiency** – internal cork of apple, heart rot; molybdenum – whiptail.
[1:3:2] - **Iodine:** **seaweeds** are the richest natural source; **iodised salt** is the best everyday dietary source; deficiency → **goitre**.
[1:3:3] # Livestock and poultry
[1:3:4] - **Kjeldahl method** estimates **nitrogen → crude protein** (N × 6.25) in feed.
[1:3:5] - **Cage layer fatigue** (laying hens) is prevented by enough **vitamin D, calcium and phosphorus – all three**.
[1:3:6] - **Rickets in chickens** = **vitamin D3** deficiency (birds use D3, not D2).
[1:3:7] - Livestock **macronutrients** = protein, carbohydrate, fat; vitamins are micronutrients.
[1:3:8] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Biology p.35, p.38, p.43; PMF General Science Part 2 p.5-7; general knowledge, not checked (Kjeldahl, cage layer fatigue)
[1:3:9] - **Stomata** = leaf pores for gas exchange and transpiration, each enclosed by two **kidney-shaped guard cells**.
[1:3:10] - **Dichogamy** = anther and stigma mature at **different times** (outbreeding device); herkogamy = physical barrier.
[1:3:11] - **Calcium in plants** – **cell wall** (calcium pectate of the middle lamella).
[1:3:12] - **Cryptogams** = no seeds/flowers (thallophytes, bryophytes, pteridophytes); **phanerogams** = seed plants; pteridophytes = **vascular cryptogams**.
[1:3:13] - **Desert plants** have long roots that grow towards deep water (**hydrotropism**).
[1:3:14] - **Pteridophytes** (ferns, Marsilea, horsetails, club mosses) = the first **vascular** plants, with no seeds; bryophytes have **no vascular tissue**.
[1:3:15] - **Carnivorous plants:** **Venus flytrap**, pitcher plant, sundew, bladderwort (get N from insects).
[1:3:16] - **Zinc:** printed key marks "zinc – activates immune cells" as the wrong pair (protein – antibodies; vitamin A – skin and linings; vitamin C – collagen, antibodies); **chicken** = printed richer zinc source (animal zinc absorbs well).
[1:3:17] - **Carotene** = hydrocarbon pigment (**no oxygen**); **xanthophyll** = oxygen-containing carotenoid.
[1:3:18] - **Milk minerals:** rich in **calcium and potassium** (and phosphorus) but very **poor in iron**.
[1:3:19] - **Minerals:** **zinc** – insulin synthesis and storage; **phosphorus** (with calcium) – bones and teeth; **fluoride** – tooth enamel, prevents caries; iron – haemoglobin.
[1:3:20] - **Xylem** carries **water and dissolved minerals** upward; phloem carries food.
[1:3:21] - **Seed dispersal agents:** wind, water, animals (and explosive fruits) – not fungi.
[1:3:22] - **Plant classification:** **Eichler (1883)** – first phylogenetic system, credited (Britannica) with one of the first widely used natural systems; **Bentham & Hooker** (*Genera Plantarum* 1862–83) – the classic natural system in NCERT; Engler & Prantl built on Eichler.
[1:3:23] - **Asexual reproduction:** budding (yeast, Hydra), fragmentation (Spirogyra), **gemmules** (sponges), spores; **syngamy** = gamete fusion (sexual).
[1:3:24] - **Root hairs** (zone of maturation) absorb the **most water**, not the growing point or zone of elongation.
[1:3:25] - **Calcium-rich foods:** **ragi**, amaranth, milk; **potassium:** almonds (~730 mg/100 g) > peanuts > cashews > walnuts.
[1:3:26] - **Chlorophyll** has **magnesium (Mg)** at the centre of its porphyrin ring (haem has iron).
[1:3:27] - **Dubai Camel Hospital** (2017) – billed as the world's **first camel hospital**.
[1:3:28] - **Meiosis in plants** is best studied in **anthers** (young flower buds); root/shoot tips show mitosis.
[1:3:29] - **Meristems:** **apical** – growth in **length**; **lateral** (cambium) – growth in **girth**; intercalary – at internodes. Meristems are **not** permanent tissue; complex tissue (xylem, phloem) has more than one cell type.
[1:3:30] - **Homologous** (same origin, different function – forelimbs); **analogous** (different origin, same function – wings of bird and insect); **vestigial** – appendix.
[1:3:31] - **Gymnosperms** (pine, cycas) bear **naked seeds without flowers**; cashew, coffee, groundnut are flowering angiosperms.
[1:3:32] - **Leaf:** photosynthesis, gas exchange, **transpiration**; the thick central vein = **midrib**; arrangement of leaves = **phyllotaxy**; conduction of food and water is the job of **xylem/phloem of the stem**.
[1:3:33] - **Vegetative propagation** gives **clones**, can be done most of the year, but **passes viruses on** (only meristem culture frees plants of virus).
[1:3:34] - **Smallest flowering plant = Wolffia** (duckweed); tallest – eucalyptus.
[1:3:35] - **N fertilisers:** urea, ammonium sulphate, ammonium nitrate, ammonium phosphate, CAN – **ammonium sulphide** is not a fertiliser.
[1:3:36] - **Flower** = a **modified shoot**: the shoot apical meristem becomes a floral meristem; its parts are modified leaves.
[1:3:37] - **Insect mouthparts:** butterfly – **siphoning** proboscis; mosquito – piercing-sucking; cockroach – chewing; housefly – sponging.
[1:3:38] - **Gymnosperms** (pine, cycas) = **naked seeds** (no ovary, no fruit); angiosperms bear seeds inside fruits.
[1:3:39] - **Transpiration pull** from the leaves is the main force lifting water up the xylem against gravity.
[1:3:40] - **Active transport** moves ions **against the concentration gradient** using **ATP** (from respiration); diffusion and osmosis are passive (no energy).
[1:3:41] - **Meristematic cells** are living, thin-walled and **actively dividing** – plant growth comes from their division (not dead, not only in bark).
[1:3:42] - **Chlorophyll** absorbs **light energy** for photosynthesis; O2 released comes from **splitting water** (photolysis).
[1:3:43] - **Chocolate is poisonous to dogs** (theobromine).
[1:3:44] - **Karonda** (*Carissa carandas*) is the fruit noted as a **rich iron source** (used against anaemia), also rich in vitamin C.
[1:3:45] - **Flower parts:** **stamen** (anther + filament) = **male**, **pollen** made in the **anther**; **pistil/carpel** (stigma, style, **ovary**) = female, **fertilisation in the ovary** (ovule); reproductive whorls = **stamens and carpels**.
[1:3:46] - **Nitrogen** – amino acids, **proteins**, chlorophyll, nucleic acids (NPK fertilisers); H, C, O come from air and water.
[1:3:47] - **Chloroplast** converts **solar energy into ATP** (photophosphorylation); mitochondria make ATP from food.
[1:3:48] - **Photosynthesis** is greatest in **blue and red light** (chlorophyll absorbs them; green is reflected).
[1:3:49] - **Photosynthesis** converts **light energy into chemical energy** (glucose).
[1:3:50] - **Binomial name:** first word = **genus** (a higher **taxon**), second = specific epithet.
[1:3:51] - **Idioblast** = parenchyma cell storing **ergastic substances** (crystals, tannin, oil).
[1:3:52] - **Calvin cycle:** CO2 + RuBP → **3-phosphoglyceric acid (PGA)**, the first stable product (C3 plants); glucose and starch come later.
[1:3:53] - **Measuring plant growth:** growth = increase in **protoplasm**, which is **hard to measure directly** – so several proxies (fresh/dry weight, length, area, volume, cell number) are used; no single one suffices.
[1:3:54] - **Xylem:** conducts **water and minerals** and gives **mechanical support**; phloem carries food.
[1:3:55] - **Corymb inflorescence** (lower pedicels longer, flowers at one level): **Delonix regia (gulmohar)**, mustard-family plants; mango and teak – panicle.
[1:3:56] - **O2 of photosynthesis comes from water** (photolysis), shown with O-18 tracer (Ruben and Kamen).
[1:3:57] - **Herbivores' urine is alkaline (pH ~7–8)**; carnivores' urine is acidic.
[1:3:58] - **Classification traps:** thallophytes have **no differentiated body**; **Funaria is a moss** (bryophyte); pteridophytes are cryptogams, not phanerogams.
[1:3:59] - **Active cell division:** apical meristems at **root and shoot tips** (onion **root tip** – mitosis slides).
[1:3:60] - **Seedless fruits** (banana, grape) come from parthenocarpy – natural or induced by **auxins/gibberellins**.
[1:3:61] - **True fruit** develops **only from the ovary**; false fruit also from thalamus etc. (apple).
[1:3:62] - **Scientific names:** mango = *Mangifera indica*; potato = *Solanum tuberosum*; lion = *Panthera leo*; peacock = *Pavo cristatus*.
[1:3:63] - **Gestation:** goat ~150 d < cow ~280 d < buffalo ~310 d < camel ~390 d.
[1:3:64] - **Abscisic acid (ABA)** = the **stress hormone** – closes stomata, aids **drought tolerance**, seed dormancy.
[1:3:65] - **Hypokalemia** = low blood **potassium** (kalium); low iodine – goitre, low iron – anaemia, low calcium – hypocalcaemia.
[1:3:66] - **Dead plant tissues:** sclerenchyma (fibres, sclereids), xylem vessels and tracheids; epidermis, parenchyma and collenchyma are living.
[1:3:67] - **Annual rings:** distinct in **temperate** trees; each ring = **spring (early) wood + autumn (late) wood**.
[1:3:68] - **Seed germination** needs **water, air (O2) and suitable temperature**; soil and sunlight are not essential.
[1:3:69] - **Angiosperm embryo sac** (female gametophyte) = **8-nucleate, 7-celled**.
[1:3:70] - **Dicot seeds:** **pulses**, gram, pea, bean (2 cotyledons); monocots: rice, wheat, maize.
[1:3:71] - **Modified plant parts:** **potato** = stem tuber (**'eyes' = nodes with buds**); onion scales = leaves; pea tendril = leaflet; carrot = root; **peanut** fruit develops **underground** (geocarpy).
[1:3:72] - **Guard cells** around a stoma are **kidney (bean) shaped**.
[1:3:73] - **Chloroplast** = site of photosynthesis (chlorophyll in thylakoids); **mitochondrion** = site of aerobic respiration.
[1:3:74] - **Fertilisers** supply N, P, K (Cl in KCl); **hydrogen** is not a fertiliser nutrient.
[1:3:75] - **Human mineral needs:** **phosphorus** (bones, ATP, DNA), **zinc** (enzymes, immunity), trace **cobalt** (in vitamin B12) – all three needed.
[1:3:76] - **Fern allies** = horsetails (*Equisetum*), club mosses (*Lycopodium*), *Selaginella* – grouped with ferns in **Pteridophyta**.
[1:3:77] - **Leaf:** **lamina (blade)** = broad green part; **petiole** = stalk; midrib/veins = support and conduction.
[1:3:78] - **Chlorophyll absorbs red and blue**, reflects green; **red light** gives the highest rate of photosynthesis.
[1:3:79] - **Evolution:** **Darwin** – natural selection (*On the Origin of Species*, 1859); Lamarck – acquired characters; de Vries – mutation.
[1:3:80] - **Energy for active transport** comes from **respiration** (ATP), not photosynthesis; movement down the gradient is diffusion.
[1:3:81] - **Guttation** = loss of excess water as **liquid droplets** from leaf tips/margins (transpiration = vapour).
[1:3:82] - **Insect-pollinated flowers:** pollen **rough and sticky**; wind-pollinated: light, smooth, dry pollen.
[1:3:83] - **Intercalary meristem:** at the **base of internodes** (and leaf bases), e.g. grasses regrow after grazing.
[1:3:84] - **Marsilea** = an aquatic **fern** (water clover), a pteridophyte with xylem and phloem.
[1:3:85] - **Plant tissues:** **meristematic** (cells keep dividing) vs **permanent** (lost the power to divide) – classified by **dividing capacity**.
[1:3:86] - **Double fertilisation** (sperm + egg → zygote; second sperm + polar nuclei → endosperm) is unique to **angiosperms**.
[1:3:87] - **Trees** = tall plants with a **hard, thick, brown woody stem** (trunk); shrubs bushy, herbs soft green stems.
[1:3:88] - **Diversity of organisms:** book answer – **adaptation** to varied environments; **mutation** is the source of variation.
[1:3:89] - **Photosynthesis gives out oxygen** (6CO2 + 6H2O → C6H12O6 + 6O2).
[1:3:90] - **Pollination:** autogamy – same flower; **geitonogamy – another flower of the same plant**; xenogamy – another plant.
[1:3:91] - **O2 is the by-product** of photosynthesis (from splitting water); sugar is the product, CO2 a raw material.
[1:3:92] - **Meristems:** **apical** (root and shoot tips) – increase in **length**; **lateral** (cambium) – girth (secondary growth); intercalary – at internodes (grasses).
[1:3:93] - **Banyan** (*Ficus benghalensis*, national tree) = **angiosperm** (prop roots).
[1:3:94] - **Stele:** the **protostele** (solid xylem core) is the most primitive, seen in early vascular plants (Rhynia); siphono-, dictyo- and atactostele are advanced.
[1:3:95] - **Sexual reproduction in plants (order):** pollination → fertilisation → zygote divides → embryo → seed → seedling.
[1:3:96] - **Conducting tissues:** xylem (tracheids, vessels, fibres, parenchyma), phloem (sieve tubes, companion cells, fibres); **pericycle is not**.
[1:3:97] - **Ruminants** (cow, goat) and the elephant digest fibre with gut microbes; the **pig** (monogastric) cannot.
[1:3:98] - **Iron sources:** green leafy vegetables (**spinach**), liver, jaggery, dates (carrot – vitamin A; pea – protein).
[1:3:99] - **Lamarck** – **use and disuse** of organs (limb loss in snakes, giraffe's neck) and inheritance of acquired characters.
[1:3:100] - **Photosynthesis** = how green plants **make their own food**: CO2 + water + light → glucose (+ O2).
[1:3:101] - **Bamboo** flowers **once in its life** after long, species-specific cycles (~30 years in books; Mizoram *Melocanna baccifera* ~48 years – 'mautam').
[1:3:102] - **Potassium deficiency:** muscle weakness, cramps, **paralysis**, irregular heartbeat; books also list **low blood pressure** (exam key), though low potassium more often raises BP.
[1:3:103] - **At night plants only respire**, giving out **CO2** – so sleeping under a tree is harmful.
[1:3:104] - **Chlorophyll** contains **magnesium** (haemoglobin – iron; vitamin B12 – cobalt).
[1:3:105] - **Roots** are the main absorbing organ of plants (water and minerals).
[1:3:106] - **'On the Origin of Species' (1859)** – written by **Charles Darwin**.
[1:3:107] - **Auxin** – cell elongation on the shaded side → **phototropism** (bending towards light).
[1:3:108] - **Xylem parenchyma** = the **only living** xylem cell; tracheids, vessels, fibres are dead.
[1:3:109] - **Plant tissues:** **collenchyma** – living, corners thickened with **cellulose, hemicellulose and pectin** (flexible support); **sclerenchyma** – **dead**, lignified walls; parenchyma – living, thin-walled.
[1:3:110] - **Bones and teeth** are mainly **calcium phosphate** (hydroxyapatite) – calcium and phosphorus are needed.
[1:3:111] - **Parthenocarpy** = fruit **without fertilisation** (seedless banana, grapes); parthenogenesis = embryo from unfertilised **egg**; apomixis = **seed** without fertilisation.
[1:3:112] - **Plant structures:** stomata – **transpiration**; xylem – water transport; root hairs – **absorption of water**; phloem – food transport.
## SUB 4 | Vitamins – Chemical Names, Deficiency Diseases and Sources
[1:4:0] # Vitamin table (most asked)
[1:4:1] TABLE head: Vitamin | Chemical name | Deficiency disease / sign | Soluble in
  [1:4:1.0] A | Retinol | Night blindness, xerophthalmia, vision loss, skin keratinisation | Fat
  [1:4:1.1] B1 | Thiamine | Beri-beri (polished rice) | Water
  [1:4:1.2] B2 | Riboflavin | Cheilosis, glossitis, stomatitis, dermatitis, digestive disorders | Water
  [1:4:1.3] B3 | Niacin | Pellagra (not B5) | Water
  [1:4:1.4] B5 | Pantothenic acid | Burning feet, paraesthesia | Water
  [1:4:1.5] B6 | Pyridoxine | Anaemia, dermatitis; needed for amino-acid metabolism and antibody production | Water
  [1:4:1.6] B7 | Biotin (vitamin H) | Skin disease, hair fall | Water
  [1:4:1.7] B9 | Folic acid (folacin) | Megaloblastic (macrocytic) anaemia | Water
  [1:4:1.8] B12 | Cobalamin / cyanocobalamin (contains cobalt) | Pernicious anaemia (anti-pernicious-anaemia factor), nerve damage | Water
  [1:4:1.9] C | Ascorbic acid | Scurvy (bleeding gums), slow wound healing, dry damaged skin, easy bruising | Water
  [1:4:1.10] D | Calciferol (D2 ergocalciferol, D3 cholecalciferol) | Rickets (children, also chickens – D3), osteomalacia (soft bones in adults) | Fat
  [1:4:1.11] E | Tocopherol | Sterility, muscle weakness | Fat
  [1:4:1.12] K | Naphthoquinone (phylloquinone) | Bleeding – increased clotting time | Fat
[1:4:2] - **Fat-soluble = A, D, E, K**; B-group and C are **water-soluble** ("A, D, E, K are water soluble" is the false statement).
[1:4:3] - **Wrong pairs to spot:** biotin = **B7** (not B12); B1 is not linked to pernicious anaemia (that is **B12**); vitamin E is not about clotting; vitamin K deficiency **prolongs** clotting, it does not shorten it.
[1:4:4] - **Cyanocobalamin** = the synthetic, cobalt-containing form of **B12**.
[1:4:5] # Functions asked
[1:4:6] - **Vitamin D** = the **"sunshine vitamin"** (made in skin under sunlight); needed for **bones and teeth** (calcium absorption).
[1:4:7] - **Vitamin C** = collagen formation (bones, teeth, skin, wound healing), iron absorption, antioxidant; **not stored**; the vitamin most **destroyed by heating**; lost by **oxidation** when cut fruit is exposed to air.
[1:4:8] - **Vitamin K** = blood clotting; made by gut bacteria (with **B12**). Vitamin A is stored in the liver.
[1:4:9] - **Heart disease risk:** high BP, raised glucose, raised lipids – **not** vitamin A deficiency.
[1:4:10] # Sources
[1:4:11] TABLE head: Vitamin | Richest / typical source
  [1:4:11.0] C | Amla (Indian gooseberry) – richest; chilli among vegetables; guava, citrus, papaya; milk is poor in vitamin C
  [1:4:11.1] A | Cod-liver oil (highest per gram), liver; carrot, mango (among fruits), pumpkin; Golden Rice (beta-carotene)
  [1:4:11.2] B1 | Rice bran / husk layer, whole grains, yeast (lost on polishing)
  [1:4:11.3] D | Sunlight, fish-liver oil, egg yolk, fortified milk and orange juice; plain seeds and fruits have almost none
  [1:4:11.4] E | Wheat-germ oil
  [1:4:11.5] K | Green leafy vegetables; also nuts, eggs, animal liver (APPSC 2004 key)
[1:4:12] # Discovery
[1:4:13] - **Casimir Funk** coined "**vitamine**" (1912); **Hopkins** – "accessory food factors"; **James Lind** – citrus cures scurvy; **McCollum and Davis** – vitamin A (1913); McCollum also identified **vitamin D** (1922, anti-rickets factor in cod-liver oil); **Umetaro Suzuki** – rice-bran extract (aberic acid, B1).
[1:4:14] - Vitamins are **organic compounds** needed in small amounts.
[1:4:15] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Biology p.10, p.41-43, p.46; PMF General Science Part 3 p.14-17; OnlyIAS NCERT S&T Part 11; app notes 5:1
[1:4:16] - **Vitamin B6 and dreams:** a 2018 Univ. of Adelaide study found B6 supplements improved **dream recall** (remembering dreams).
[1:4:17] - **Vitamin A and season:** standard RDAs (ICMR-NIN) give no season-specific need; night blindness peaks in the hot pre-harvest season (printed key: winter).
[1:4:18] - **Milk and vitamin A:** books treat milk as **poor in vitamin A** (and C, iron); carrot, mango, papaya are the carotene sources.
[1:4:19] - **Xerophthalmia** (vitamin A deficiency, leading cause of preventable child blindness) is India's priority deficiency – the National Prophylaxis Programme gives children vitamin A doses (UPSC 1996).
[1:4:20] - The body **cannot make most vitamins** (exceptions: D in skin, K and B12 by gut bacteria, niacin from tryptophan) – they must come from food; vitamins **do not kill bacteria**.
[1:4:21] - **Vitamin K** is the **antidote** to warfarin-type **anticoagulant** poisons (rat poisons) – it restores clotting-factor synthesis.
[1:4:22] - **Vitamin B12** is water-soluble yet **stored in the liver for 3–5 years** – the exception to 'water-soluble vitamins are not stored'.
[1:4:23] - **Match drill:** malaria – **mosquito bite** (*Plasmodium* via female *Anopheles*); goitre – **iodine** deficiency; rickets – vitamin D; night blindness – vitamin A.
[1:4:24] - **Milk** has several **B vitamins** (riboflavin B2, B12) and calcium but is poor in **vitamin C** and **iron**.
[1:4:25] - The body **cannot make vitamin A** itself (it comes from food retinol or carotene); proteins, enzymes and hormones are made in the body.
## SUB 5 | Everyday Chemistry – Atoms, Reactions, Acids, Metals and Organic Compounds
[1:5:0] # Chemical reactions
[1:5:1] - **Rusting** (hydrated iron oxide) is a **chemical change** – the nail's **weight increases**; **tarnishing of silver** (Ag2S) is **corrosion**; freezing, dissolving salt, a glowing filament are physical changes.
[1:5:2] - **Acid + metal → salt + hydrogen**; **acid + base → salt + water** (neutralisation); C + O2 → CO2 is **exothermic**.
[1:5:3] - **Signs of a chemical reaction:** gas evolved, colour change, heat change – **evaporation** is only a change of state (physical).
[1:5:4] - **Limestone:** CaCO3 → CaO + **CO2** on heating – CO2 **puts out a flame**; **quicklime + water → slaked lime Ca(OH)2 + much heat**; acid (HCl) + egg shell → CO2 – turns **lime water milky**.
[1:5:5] - **Propane combustion:** C3H8 + **5 O2** → 3 CO2 + 4 H2O; **Haber:** N2 + 3 H2 ⇌ 2 NH3 (X = 1, Y = 3, Z = 2).
[1:5:6] - **Combustion** needs fuel, oxygen and ignition temperature – **not water**; **magnesium** in crackers – bright light from **oxidation** to MgO.
[1:5:7] - **Displacement:** iron nail in copper sulphate gets a **brown copper** coat (solution pale green); iron + dilute HCl → **iron chloride + hydrogen**; **silver bromide** turns **grey** in sunlight (photolysis).
[1:5:8] - **Physical change:** dissolving salt in water; burning wax/wood and curdling milk are chemical.
[1:5:9] - **Ignition temperature:** a substance catches fire only once heated to it – at 100 °C, substances igniting at 95 °C and 98 °C burn, those at 105 °C and 108 °C do not.
[1:5:10] # Atoms and the periodic table
[1:5:11] - **Helium:** noble gas, Group 18, period 1 – **top right corner** of the periodic table; inert, so safe in balloons; **hydrogen** is lighter but **inflammable** (least safe).
[1:5:12] - **Atom:** electrons held round the nucleus by the **electrostatic (Coulomb) force**; nucleons held by the **strong nuclear force**; **protium (¹H)** has atomic number = mass number = 1.
[1:5:13] - **Periodic table:** metals on the **left**, metalloids on the zigzag, **non-metals on the right**; **Group IA = alkali metals** (Li, Na, K…); **potassium** is soft enough to **cut with a knife**.
[1:5:14] - **Spectral (emission) line:** electron **jumps from a higher to a lower orbit**, emitting a photon.
[1:5:15] - **Organic compound** must contain **carbon**; **ammonia** = N + H (NH3); **~94** elements occur naturally (118 known – "about 100"); **hydrogen** = most common element in the **Universe**.
[1:5:16] - **Transition (d-block) metals** form **coloured compounds** (d–d transitions).
[1:5:17] # Organic chemistry
[1:5:18] - **Alcohols as reducing agents:** they are themselves **oxidised to aldehydes or ketones**.
[1:5:19] - **Hydrogenation** of unsaturated hydrocarbons/oils uses a **nickel** catalyst (vanaspati); **Wurtz reaction** – alkyl halides + **sodium** in dry ether → higher alkanes; **vulcanisation** of rubber with **sulphur** (Goodyear, 1839).
[1:5:20] - **Perspex** (PMMA) – monomer methyl methacrylate made with **methyl alcohol**; **tear gas CN** = **α-chloroacetophenone**.
[1:5:21] - **Named reactions:** **Etard** – aromatic methyl → aldehyde (chromyl chloride); **Stephen** – nitrile → aldehyde (SnCl2/HCl); Gattermann–Koch = formylation (CO + HCl).
[1:5:22] - **Functional groups:** alcohols – **–OH (hydroxyl)**; **esters** – fruity smell; smallest **ketone** = acetone (**3 C**); **C2H6O** = ethanol; alcohol + conc. H2SO4 at 170 °C → **alkene** (dehydration); ethanol + alkaline KMnO4 → **acid**, acid + ethanol → **ester**.
[1:5:23] - **Alkaloids:** **nicotine** – tobacco; morphine – opium poppy; reserpine – Rauwolfia; **benzene** (crude oil, petrol) – carcinogen causing **leukaemia**.
[1:5:24] # Metals
[1:5:25] - **Gold** = most **malleable** and ductile metal (silver next); **aluminium** = most used metal in nearly pure form (foil, cans, conductors).
[1:5:26] - **Thermite:** **iron oxide + aluminium** – welds rails; **galvanisation** = **zinc** coating; **silver** = best electrical conductor; **lead** = toxic metal of car batteries.
[1:5:27] - **Electrolytic refining of copper:** impure anode dissolves; **pure copper deposits at the cathode**; excess **lithium in LiCl → pink** (F-centres; K in KCl violet, Na in NaCl yellow).
[1:5:28] - **Smelting** = extracting metal from ore by reduction at high temperature; **blast furnace:** coke → CO, limestone → CaO + CO2, CO reduces Fe2O3 (no FeSO4 decomposition); **seasoning** with oil stops **cast iron** rusting.
[1:5:29] # Soaps, water and household chemistry
[1:5:30] - **Saponification** = fat/oil + strong base (NaOH) → **soap + glycerol**; the soap's **hydrocarbon tail** grips oily dirt while the ionic head faces water (micelles).
[1:5:31] - **Alum** purifies water by **coagulation** of suspended mud (Al³⁺ neutralises the charge); **rain water** = purest natural water (natural distillation).
[1:5:32] - **Safety match:** **red phosphorus** on the **striking strip**; head = potassium chlorate + antimony trisulphide (most keys say "head = phosphorus" – old strike-anywhere matches).
[1:5:33] - **Melamine** – unbreakable crockery; **tincture of iodine** – antiseptic; enzymes = biocatalysts; **DNA fingerprinting** – identifying criminals; **borax** dissolves shellac for water-based varnish.
[1:5:34] # Salts and their uses
[1:5:35] - **Salts:** sodium carbonate – washing soda, glass; sodium benzoate – food preservative; calcium carbonate – flux in metal extraction; calcium phosphate – fertiliser.
[1:5:36] - **Sodium thiosulphate** – antidote for **cyanide**; **normal saline** = **0.9% NaCl** (isotonic); **gunpowder** = sulphur + charcoal + **potassium nitrate**.
