[1:5:37] - **Common names:** **alum** = potassium aluminium sulphate dodecahydrate; **aspirin** = acetylsalicylic acid; chalk = CaCO3; **barium nitrate** – oxidiser giving **green** flame in fireworks (strontium red, copper blue).
[1:5:38] # Acids and bases
[1:5:39] - **All acids contain hydrogen** (H⁺ in water); **pH = "potential of hydrogen"**; **NaOH + HCl → NaCl + H2O** = neutralisation, a **double displacement**.
[1:5:40] - **Acids** conduct electricity in water, taste sour, change indicators and **react with active metals** (H2); **litmus:** **purple** in neutral, **blue → red** in acid; **ammonia** turns red litmus blue (**weak base**).
[1:5:41] # Carbon and non-metals
[1:5:42] - **Graphite** = **inorganic** solid lubricant (lanolin, mineral oil, PTFE are organic).
[1:5:43] - **Sulphur** = common **fungicide**; **bromine** = only **non-metal liquid** at room temperature (mercury is a metal).
[1:5:44] - **Noble gases in air:** argon, neon, helium… – **radon** is the one usually said to be absent (strictly, traces near the ground); **dry ice** = **solid CO2** (sublimes at −78.5 °C) – vaccine transport.
[1:5:45] # Mixtures and colloids
[1:5:46] - **Aerosol** = tiny liquid droplets (or solid particles) dispersed in **air/gas** – fog, mist, sprays; **sea water** = a mixture (table salt, sugar, lime are compounds).
[1:5:47] - **Solubility in water (25 °C):** **glucose ~90 g/100 mL** > ammonium chloride ~38 > table salt ~36 > alum ~14.
[1:5:48] - **Butter** = water-in-fat **emulsion** (NCERT lists butter as a gel – liquid in solid); vinegar, sugar solution and honey are solutions.
[1:5:49] # Laboratory
[1:5:50] - **Titration** uses a **burette**; pipette transfers a fixed volume; **dry litmus + dry HCl gas → no change** (no ionisation without water); **turmeric** is a natural acid–base indicator (red-brown in base).
[1:5:51] # Industrial processes
[1:5:52] - **Haber process:** N2 + H2 → **ammonia** (iron catalyst, high T and P); **Contact process:** SO2 → SO3 over **V2O5** → **sulphuric acid**.
[1:5:53] ~~ Sources: NextGen Science and Technology Group 2 Part 3 p.12-16; NextGen Rocket Chemistry p.2-32 (elements, metals, acids, salts), p.43-47 (Haber, galvanisation), p.59 (saponification), p.64; PMF General Science Part 7 p.8-66 (chemistry in daily life); NCERT Class 10 Chemistry ch. 1-4
## SUB 6 | Human Physiology – Organs, Blood, Nerves, Senses and First Aid
[1:6:0] # Medicine and first aid
[1:6:1] - **Diuretics:** furosemide (loop), mannitol (osmotic), acetazolamide (carbonic-anhydrase inhibitor) – all increase urine.
[1:6:2] - **Catgut** (collagen from animal gut) = classic **absorbable suture**; silk and nylon are non-absorbable.
[1:6:3] - **Burns:** **1st degree** – epidermis only, redness and pain; 2nd – partial thickness, blisters; 3rd – full thickness.
[1:6:4] - **Heat stroke:** body cooling fails and core temperature **rises quickly** (> 40 °C); give **lemon water or buttermilk** (fluids and salts).
[1:6:5] - **Wounds:** **abscess** = cavity with **pus**; hematoma = collected blood; **contusion (bruise)** = classic **closed wound**; **root canal therapy** saves an abscessed tooth.
[1:6:6] - **Dosage forms:** syrup = liquid; tablets, capsules, boluses = solid; suppositories and enemas are rectal – **ecbolics** (oxytocics) are injected.
[1:6:7] - **Burn first aid:** cool the part in **cold running water**; do not pull off stuck cloth or apply butter.
[1:6:8] - **Capsule shells** = **gelatin**; **Dettol** = **chloroxylenol** + α-terpineol; **KOH mount** clears skin scrapings for fungi/mites; **sulphur ointment** controls **mange**.
[1:6:9] - **Antipyretics** (paracetamol) lower fever; analgesics relieve pain; **bronchoscopy** examines airways – not a nervous-system test (EEG, myelography are).
[1:6:10] - **Anorexia nervosa, bulimia** = **eating disorders**.
[1:6:11] # Digestion
[1:6:12] - **Digestion:** pancreas – digestive enzymes **and** insulin; **small intestine** – most absorption; **gall bladder stores bile** (made by the liver).
[1:6:13] - **Enzymes:** **lipase** – fat; **amylase** – starch; **trypsin** – protein; **lactase** – lactose; **oesophagus** links pharynx to stomach.
[1:6:14] - **Digestive system** = breaks food down and **absorbs nutrients**.
[1:6:15] - **Saliva** pH ~**6.7**, **salivary amylase (ptyalin)** digests starch; **lipase** digests fats; HCl from **gastric glands**.
[1:6:16] - **Fat digestion:** bile (made in the **liver**, stored in gall bladder) emulsifies, lipase completes it in the **small intestine**; digested fats go into **lacteals** – carried by **lymph**.
[1:6:17] - **Alimentary canal** = mouth → **anus**; **small intestine** = longest part (6–7 m), starts with the **duodenum**; **stomach** = cardiac, fundic, body, pyloric parts (serosa is a wall layer).
[1:6:18] # Heart and blood
[1:6:19] - **Resting heart rate** of an adult = **60–100 beats/min** (~72).
[1:6:20] - **Sphygmomanometer** measures **blood pressure**.
[1:6:21] - **WBCs** (with platelets) are **~1%** of blood volume (buffy coat).
[1:6:22] - **Blood pH 7.35–7.45** (slightly alkaline); **hypernatraemia** = excess **sodium**; **4 heart valves** (tricuspid, pulmonary, mitral, aortic).
[1:6:23] - **Vessels:** **capillaries** = narrowest (5–10 µm, one cell thick); **aorta** = largest artery; **arteries** carry blood from the heart to the body; **pulmonary artery** carries CO2-rich blood to the lungs; **renal** – kidney, **hepatic** – liver, **coronary** – heart.
[1:6:24] - **Blood cells:** **erythrocytes = RBC** (formed in **red bone marrow**), **leucocytes = WBC** (colourless, no haemoglobin), **thrombocytes = platelets**.
[1:6:25] # Brain and nerves
[1:6:26] - **Right cerebral hemisphere** – **spatial abilities**, face recognition; left – language.
[1:6:27] - **Adult brain** ≈ 1.3–1.4 kg (~**3 pounds**); **axon** = the long fibre carrying impulses away from the cell body (dendrites short, branched); **Nissl's granules** are in the neuron cell body (not excretory); **trigeminal (V)** = largest cranial nerve, sensory + motor.
[1:6:28] - **Synapse** = junction (gap) between two neurons.
[1:6:29] - **Neurons** send and receive information; **medulla oblongata** – involuntary actions (BP, salivation, vomiting, **breathing**).
[1:6:30] # Skeleton and muscles
[1:6:31] - **Axial skeleton** = skull, vertebral column, ribs, sternum; **femur** = longest and strongest bone (tibia = lower leg).
[1:6:32] - **Ossification** = deposition of calcium and minerals on cartilage → bone.
[1:6:33] - **Tooth enamel** = hardest substance in the body – **calcium phosphate** (hydroxyapatite); **calcium + phosphorus** give bones their strength; **milk teeth** fall from **6–8 years** (most keys; strictly ~6 to 12).
[1:6:34] - **Skeletal (striated) muscle** = **voluntary**; smooth and cardiac – involuntary.
[1:6:35] # Teeth, eye and senses
[1:6:36] - **Adult dentition (32):** **8 incisors, 4 canines, 8 premolars, 12 molars**.
[1:6:37] - **Eye:** visual acuity **20/200** = **legally blind**; **ciliary muscles** change the lens curvature (accommodation); one eye only → loss of depth – a **two-dimensional** world.
[1:6:38] - **Iris** gives the eye its colour – pigment made by **melanocyte cells**.
[1:6:39] # Kidney and excretion
[1:6:40] - **Excretory system:** nephron – **Bowman's capsule**, glomerulus, **Henle's loop**; ureters – **not** alveoli (lungs), colon or Schwann cells (myelin of nerves).
[1:6:41] - **Kidneys** filter waste and excess water from the blood.
[1:6:42] # Respiration
[1:6:43] - **Lungs:** gas exchange in the **alveoli**; total lung capacity ≈ **6 L** (vital capacity ~4.5–4.8 L).
[1:6:44] - **Alveoli** spread out ≈ a **tennis court** (~70 m²); **sneezing** = a reflex of the **respiratory system**.
[1:6:45] # Body composition
[1:6:46] - **Human body by mass:** **oxygen ~65%** > carbon > hydrogen > nitrogen > calcium.
[1:6:47] - **Anatomy** = structure (physical arrangement) of body parts; **physiology** = how they function.
[1:6:48] - **Trace elements:** official key treats **arsenic** as having a functional requirement (its essentiality in humans is unproven).
[1:6:49] # Immunity and disease
[1:6:50] - **Innate immunity** (first line): skin, mucus, **tears** (lysozyme) flushing dust from the eye.
[1:6:51] - **Pneumonia** bacteria: **Haemophilus influenzae**, Streptococcus pneumoniae (Lactobacillus, S. thermophilus – curd).
[1:6:52] - **Tears** contain **lysozyme** (antibacterial); ptyalin in saliva.
[1:6:53] # Reproduction
[1:6:54] - **Vas deferens** = **male** sperm duct – not female (female: ovary, fallopian tube with fimbriae, uterus, endometrium, cervix).
[1:6:55] # Glands and hormones
[1:6:56] - **Goitre** = **enlargement of the thyroid gland**, mostly from iodine deficiency.
[1:6:57] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Biology p.8-9, p.25-26, p.43-58 (digestion, blood, heart, brain, skeleton); PMF General Science Part 2 p.20-45 (respiration, blood, heart, digestion), Part 3 p.43-54 (nervous system, eye); NCERT Class 10-11 Biology; Medscape/AAP (burns first aid, milk teeth) for first-aid items
## SUB 7 | Acids and Chemicals in Food and Daily Life
[1:7:0] TABLE head: Acid | Found in / use
  [1:7:0.0] Formic (methanoic) acid | Ant bite, bee sting, nettle hairs; limited antibacterial preservative
  [1:7:0.1] Citric acid | Lemon (free form; highest citrate), citrus fruits; soft drinks
  [1:7:0.2] Acetic (ethanoic) acid | Vinegar
  [1:7:0.3] Tartaric acid | Tamarind, grapes; used in baking powder (with baking soda)
  [1:7:0.4] Lactic acid | Curd, sour milk, tired muscles
  [1:7:0.5] Malic acid | Apples
  [1:7:0.6] Oxalic acid | Tomato, spinach; removes ink stains
  [1:7:0.7] Ascorbic acid | Vitamin C (amla, guava, citrus)
  [1:7:0.8] Phosphoric acid | Cola soft drinks (with caffeine)
  [1:7:0.9] Carbonic acid | Soda water – CO2 in water, so soda water is acidic
  [1:7:0.10] HCl | Stomach (gastric juice)
  [1:7:0.11] Sulphuric acid | Main acid of acid rain (with nitric acid)
[1:7:1] - **Formic acid is stronger than acetic acid** (the CH3 group of acetic acid pushes electrons and weakens it); both are organic acids, but "being organic" does not explain the strength.
[1:7:2] - **Caffeine** (tea, coffee, colas) is a **diuretic**; citric acid is **not** a substitute for ascorbic acid (only ascorbic acid is a vitamin).
[1:7:3] # Chemicals in daily life
[1:7:4] TABLE head: Chemical | Fact
  [1:7:4.0] Potassium dichromate | Breath analyser – orange dichromate turns green when it oxidises alcohol
  [1:7:4.1] Prussic acid (hydrocyanic acid, HCN) | The deadly poison "cyanide"
  [1:7:4.2] Mustard gas (sulphur mustard) | Blister agent of chemical warfare, used in World War I; not related to mustard oil
  [1:7:4.3] Aqua regia (3 HCl : 1 HNO3) | Only common reagent that dissolves gold; no single acid, and no sulphuric–nitric mixture, does
  [1:7:4.4] Bisphenol A (BPA) | Chemical for polycarbonate plastics and food-can linings (food packaging)
  [1:7:4.5] Sodium hypochlorite (bleach) | Oxidising bleach – removes coffee and tea stains from white cloth
  [1:7:4.6] Merthiolate (thiomersal) | Preservative for serum and vaccines; heparin and oxalates are anticoagulants
[1:7:5] - **Chlorination:** chlorine + water → **hypochlorous acid (HOCl)** + HCl; **HOCl** is the disinfecting agent.
[1:7:6] - **Reactivity series:** **K > Na > Ca > Mg > Al > Zn > Fe** > Pb > H > Cu > Hg > Ag > Au – **potassium** is the most reactive metal.
[1:7:7] - **Food cans are tin-plated, not zinc-coated:** **zinc is more reactive** than tin and would react with food acids to form toxic salts.
[1:7:8] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Chemistry p.2, p.5, p.13, p.19, p.44, p.53, p.70; OnlyIAS NCERT S&T Part 7 (acids) and Part 8 (aqua regia); UPSC CSE 2006 key; app notes 5:1
[1:7:9] - **Tinning (kalai):** brass/copper vessels coated with **tin** to stop copper contamination.
[1:7:10] - **Tin (Sn) and lead lie just above hydrogen**; Cu, Hg, Ag, Au below H – electron-releasing tendency **Zn > Cu > Ag**.
[1:7:11] - **Sour milk:** bacteria make **lactic acid**, so pH falls **below 6** (fresh milk pH ~6.5).
[1:7:12] - **Metal + dilute acid → hydrogen:** Zn + 2HCl → ZnCl2 + **H2**; hydrogen burns with a **pop** when a lighted match is brought near.
[1:7:13] - **Lemon:** mainly **citric** with some **malic** acid (tartaric = tamarind, grapes); **avocado** fat = **oleic** acid; nettle sting (formic acid) – rub with a **dock leaf** (basic).
[1:7:14] - **Ripe banana:** main acid = **malic acid** (apples too); citric dominates in citrus.
[1:7:15] - **pH ladder:** gastric juice ~1.5 < lemon ~2 < vinegar ~3 < pure water 7 < **blood 7.4** < baking soda ~8.5 < **milk of magnesia ~10** < **NaOH ~14**.
[1:7:16] - **Oxalic acid** – also used in **photography** (ferrous oxalate developer).
[1:7:17] - **Oil of vitriol** = concentrated **sulphuric acid** (king of chemicals); blue vitriol = CuSO4.
[1:7:18] - **Ascorbic acid** has **no –COOH group** (its acidity is from an enol –OH in a lactone ring); citric, lactic and tartaric acids are carboxylic acids.
[1:7:19] - **Sulphuric acid** is usually called the **first mineral acid discovered** (some keys say nitric acid).
[1:7:20] - **More food acids:** **butyric** – rancid butter; **stearic** – fats; **oxalic** – wood sorrel; **malic** – apples.
[1:7:21] - **Onion tears:** cutting releases **alliinase** – amino-acid sulfoxides → **sulfenic acid** → syn-propanethial-S-oxide (tear gas).
[1:7:22] - **Litmus:** a **base** turns **red litmus blue**; an **acid** turns **blue litmus red**; neutral salt changes neither.
[1:7:23] - **Litmus test logic:** red litmus stays red → **not a base** (could be acid or neutral); blue stays blue → not an acid.
[1:7:24] - **Glacial acetic acid:** pure ethanoic acid melts at **290 K (16.6 °C)** – a fairly **high** melting point – so it **freezes in winter** in cold climates.
## SUB 8 | Plants – Structure, Transport, Photosynthesis, Pollination and Useful Plants
[1:8:0] # Medicinal and economic plants
[1:8:1] - **Ashwagandha** = ***Withania somnifera*** (Solanaceae); *W. coagulans* = paneer dodi; *Solanum nigrum* = black nightshade.
[1:8:2] - **Pseudocereals** (seeds used like grain, not grasses): **buckwheat** (kuttu, Polygonaceae), amaranth, quinoa.
[1:8:3] - **Botanical names:** fennel *Foeniculum vulgare*; ginger *Zingiber officinale*; garlic *Allium sativum*; touch-me-not *Mimosa pudica*.
[1:8:4] - **Medicinal uses:** **sarpagandha** – nervous disorders, hypertension (reserpine); **isabgol** – chronic constipation; **arjuna** – heart; **sandal** – diuretic, refrigerant.
[1:8:5] - **Garlic** – edible **bulb** (cloves); **potato** "eyes" = **axillary buds** (tuber is a stem); **apple** – edible part is the fleshy **thalamus**, not the mesocarp.
[1:8:6] - **Cissus quadrangularis** (hadjod, "bone-setter") – herb for **bone healing**.
[1:8:7] - **Rauvolfia serpentina** (sarpagandha) – traditional **snake-bite** antidote; **fenugreek** seeds help **diabetics**.
[1:8:8] - **Pitcher plant** – insectivorous (eats mosquitoes); **Rauwolfia** drug comes from the **root** of sarpagandha; **national tree** = banyan, ***Ficus benghalensis***.
[1:8:9] # Plant types and parts
[1:8:10] - **Annuals (seasonal plants)** complete their life cycle in one season; biennials two; perennials many years; **gymnosperms** = **naked-seeded** (pine, cycas).
[1:8:11] - **Agrostology** = study of **grasses**.
[1:8:12] - **Plant kingdom:** algae (**Thallophyta** – Spirogyra, Ulothrix, Cladophora, Chara – no roots, stem, leaves), bryophytes, pteridophytes, gymnosperms, angiosperms – **annelids are animals**; **angiosperms** bear flowers, seeds enclosed in ovary, usually have vessels.
[1:8:13] - **Endodermis** of dicot stem = **starch sheath**; **Sachs:** three tissue systems – **dermal, vascular, fundamental**.
[1:8:14] - **Banana** is a giant **herb** (pseudostem), not a tree.
[1:8:15] - **Edible parts:** **potato** – modified **stem** (tuber); **carrot** – modified **root**; **apple** – swollen **thalamus**; **cabbage** – **vegetative bud**.
[1:8:16] # Flowers, pollination and seeds
[1:8:17] - **Pollination:** bright, scented, nectar-rich flowers → **insects (entomophily)**; **anemophily = wind**; hydrophily = water; ornithophily = birds.
[1:8:18] - After fertilisation the **ovary → fruit** and the ovules → seeds.
[1:8:19] - **Sporopollenin** (pollen exine) = most resistant organic material: withstands high temperature, strong acids and alkalis; no enzyme degrades it – so pollen fossilises well.
[1:8:20] - Repeated **self-pollination** → inbreeding depression, **weak seeds**; cross-pollination gives vigour.
[1:8:21] - **Winged seeds** (wind dispersal): **drumstick, maple**; hooks – Xanthium, Urena; explosive – castor, balsam.
[1:8:22] - **Placentation:** **axile** – Hibiscus (Malvaceae); marginal – legumes; **phyllotaxy** = arrangement of leaves on the stem; **orchids** – "**dust seeds**"; **ornithophily** = bird pollination.
[1:8:23] - **Vallisneria:** the **whole male flower** floats to the female flower (water pollination).
[1:8:24] # Leaf, photosynthesis and respiration
[1:8:25] - **Leaf:** **stomata** – pores for gas exchange and transpiration; **guard cells** open and close them; **chloroplasts** hold chlorophyll; enzymes = biocatalysts.
[1:8:26] - **Photosynthesis:** green plants **make their own food** (glucose) from CO2 + water with sunlight and chlorophyll; light absorbed by chlorophyll, CO2 **reduced** (not oxidised); the O2 released comes from **water** (photolysis); **oxygen is a product, not a requirement**.
[1:8:27] - **Stomata** = pores for **gas exchange**, opened and closed by **guard cells** through **potassium ions** moving in and out.
[1:8:28] - **Inputs of photosynthesis:** **chlorophyll**, light, CO2, water (O2 and glucose are products).
[1:8:29] - **C4 types:** **Panicum** – PEP-CK; maize, sugarcane – NADP-ME; Amaranthus – NAD-ME.
[1:8:30] - **Chlorophyll a** = **blue-green**; chlorophyll b = yellow-green.
[1:8:31] # Water, roots and transport
[1:8:32] - **Root pressure** is **lowest in summer** (heavy transpiration) and highest in the rainy season.
[1:8:33] - **Xylem** carries **water and minerals upward** from roots to leaves; **phloem** carries food (**translocation**); a **vascular bundle** holds both; vessel **end walls and pit pores** stop cavitation spreading.
[1:8:34] - **Transpiration** = evaporation at the **leaf–air** surface; the tension **pulls water up** (not loss at the root surface).
[1:8:35] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Biology p.81, p.85-99, p.112-116; NextGen Rocket Chemistry p.52-55 (Rauwolfia, stomata); PMF General Science Part 1 p.55-101 (plant tissues, classification, morphology), Part 2 p.1-19 (transport, photosynthesis), Part 3 p.38 (pollination); NCERT Class 11 Biology ch. 3, 5
## SUB 9 | Proteins, Amino Acids and Malnutrition
[1:9:0] - **Proteins** = **body-building** nutrients (growth and repair; most important for growing children and athletes building muscle) – ~**4 kcal/g**; **amino acids** are their **building blocks** (20 common, ~9–10 essential; not stored).
[1:9:1] - **Essential amino acids** (from food): histidine, **isoleucine**, leucine, lysine, **methionine**, phenylalanine, **threonine**, **tryptophan**, valine. **Non-essential:** **tyrosine**, alanine, aspartic acid, cysteine, glycine, **proline** (an amino acid).
[1:9:2] - **Egg** = **highest biological value** – contains **all essential amino acids** in the right proportion.
[1:9:3] TABLE head: Protein | Found in
  [1:9:3.0] Casein (~80% of milk protein) | Milk (primary group); whey proteins the rest
  [1:9:3.1] Gluten | Wheat
  [1:9:3.2] Zein | Maize
  [1:9:3.3] Keratin | Hair, nails, horn
  [1:9:3.4] Myosin (with actin) | Muscle
  [1:9:3.5] Albumin | Egg white, blood
  [1:9:3.6] Collagen / fibroin | Connective tissue / silk
[1:9:4] - "**Only proteins**" pair: **keratin and myosin** (fructose, maltose and sucrose are sugars).
[1:9:5] - **Lactoderm** = the sticky **protein film (skin) on heated milk**.
[1:9:6] # Protein-energy malnutrition
[1:9:7] TABLE head: Disease | Cause | Sign
  [1:9:7.0] Kwashiorkor | Protein deficiency (calories adequate), children over 1 | Oedema, swollen belly, skin and hair changes
  [1:9:7.1] Marasmus | Severe lack of both protein and calories, infants | Extreme wasting, no oedema
  [1:9:7.2] Marasmic kwashiorkor | Deficiency of both proteins and calories with oedema | Wasting + oedema
[1:9:8] - Not PEM: scurvy (C), goitre (iodine), mumps (virus), achondroplasia (genetic dwarfism), rickets (D).
[1:9:9] - **Protein per 100 g (increasing):** egg white (~11 g) < fish (~20 g) < Bengal gram dal (~21 g) < groundnut (~25 g) < **skimmed milk powder (~35 g)**; soybean is the **highest-protein grain** (~40 g).
[1:9:10] - Nursing mother needs **65 g** protein a day.
[1:9:11] - **Pepsin** (stomach) breaks proteins into **peptides**; trypsin works later in the small intestine.
[1:9:12] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Biology p.12, p.42-46; PMF General Science Part 2 p.46, Part 3 p.2-4; protein-content order: general knowledge, not checked; app notes 5:1
[1:9:13] - **Alpha-keratin** – skin (epidermis), hair, nails, horns, wool; **beta-keratin** – feathers, scales.
[1:9:14] - **Protein needs with age:** older adults need **more** protein per kg (1.0–1.2 g/kg, PROT-AGE) than young adults (0.8 g/kg) to prevent muscle loss.
[1:9:15] - **DNA** contains the bases **adenine, guanine, cytosine, thymine** (+ sugar, phosphate); **tyrosine** is an amino acid, not part of DNA.
[1:9:16] - **Oxytocin** is a **peptide hormone** (9 amino acids, from the hypothalamus, released by the posterior pituitary), not counted as a protein; keratin, fibroin and collagen are structural proteins.
[1:9:17] - **Hair keratin** has **sulphur (disulphide) bonds**; hair setting/perming breaks and re-forms them; **henna** (lawsone) stains by binding to **proteins and amino acids** of skin and hair.
[1:9:18] - Enzymes are basically **proteins** – e.g. pepsin, trypsin, amylase (not fats, sugars or vitamins).
[1:9:19] - **Enzymes** are biological catalysts and are **mostly proteins** (a few RNA enzymes – ribozymes).
[1:9:20] - Proteins are **linear chains of amino acids** and **heteropolymers** (20 kinds); **nucleotides** build nucleic acids.
## SUB 10 | Carbohydrates, Sugars, Fats and Food Groups
[1:10:0] TABLE head: Nutrient | Role | Key facts
  [1:10:0.0] Carbohydrate | Main energy-giving food | Rice – highest carbohydrate share; rice's major nutrient = carbohydrate
  [1:10:0.1] Fat | Stored energy | ~9.3 kcal/g (most per gram); built from fatty acids + glycerol
  [1:10:0.2] Protein | Body-building | ~4 kcal/g
  [1:10:0.3] Vitamins, minerals | Regulatory, micronutrients | Protein, carbohydrate and fat = macronutrients (also for livestock)
[1:10:1] - **Starch** = a **polysaccharide** (glucose polymer); the **iodine test** turns starch **blue-black**.
[1:10:2] - **Fructose** = **fruit sugar**, the **sweetest natural sugar**; gives fruits their sweet taste.
[1:10:3] - **Honey:** mainly **fructose** (~38%) > glucose (~31%); sucrose and maltose minor; honey is not a colloid.
[1:10:4] - **Relative sweetness (descending): fructose > sucrose > glucose > maltose** (> lactose).
[1:10:5] - **Invert sugar** = glucose + fructose from **hydrolysis of sucrose**; **sweeter than sucrose** because of its free fructose (the hydrolysis is how it is made, not why it is sweet).
[1:10:6] - **Sucrose** = glucose + fructose (disaccharide); maltose, lactose = disaccharides; glucose, fructose, ribose = monosaccharides.
[1:10:7] - **Soybean** is rich in **all three** – protein (~40%), fat (~20%) and carbohydrate.
[1:10:8] - Plants make the essential foods **carbohydrates, fats and proteins**; **nucleic acids** are genetic material, not a "food".
[1:10:9] - **Oatmeal** = a **cereal-grain** product; sago and arrowroot are starches from stems/roots, soya flour is from a legume.
[1:10:10] # Artificial sweeteners
[1:10:11] TABLE head: Sweetener | Fact
  [1:10:11.0] Saccharin (ortho-sulphobenzimide) | First popular artificial sweetener (~550× sucrose)
  [1:10:11.1] Aspartame | Most widely used; NCERT: "about 100 times as sweet as cane sugar" (dipeptide)
  [1:10:11.2] Alitame | High-potency sweetener (~2000×)
  [1:10:11.3] Sucralose | Modified sugar – trichloro derivative of sucrose (~600×)
  [1:10:11.4] Stevia | Natural sweetener
[1:10:12] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Chemistry p.49-51; NextGen Rocket Biology p.11, p.42; PMF General Science Part 2 p.54-55; PMF General Science Part 7 p.68; OnlyIAS NCERT S&T Part 11 (food tests); app notes 5:1
[1:10:13] - **Linseed (flaxseed)** oil has the most **α-linolenic acid** (omega-3) among oilseeds.
[1:10:14] - **"No trans-fats"** = no **partially hydrogenated oils**, better for heart health (animal fats may still be used).
[1:10:15] - **Balanced (healthy) diet:** an **optimal** mix of carbohydrate, protein and fat with **adequate water and roughage (fibre)**, plus vitamins and minerals.
[1:10:16] - **Caffeine** by equal dry weight: **tea leaves** (~3–4%) > coffee beans (~1–2%).
[1:10:17] - **Carbohydrates** = **Cx(H2O)y** (H : O = 2 : 1, glucose C6H12O6); **monosaccharides** (ribose, glucose), **oligosaccharides** (maltose), **polysaccharides** (cellulose, starch).
[1:10:18] - **Food groups:** energy-giving (carbohydrates, fats), body-building (proteins), **protective / regulatory** (vitamins and minerals – yellow and orange fruits, vegetables).
[1:10:19] - **Winter** needs more **fat-rich** (energy-dense) food to keep the body warm.
[1:10:20] - **Taste of starch:** starch itself is almost tasteless; on chewing, **salivary amylase** turns it into **maltose**, so starchy food (and carbohydrates generally) taste **sweet**.
[1:10:21] - **Exercise fuel:** carbohydrate (muscle glycogen, blood glucose) is the main fuel of hard and endurance exercise; in **prolonged low-moderate** exercise **fat** supplies ~50% or more.
[1:10:22] - **ATP** (adenosine triphosphate) = the **energy currency** of the cell; glucose is the fuel, pyruvic acid the product of glycolysis.
[1:10:23] - **Roughage (dietary fibre)** gives no energy but adds bulk and helps expel undigested food (prevents constipation).
[1:10:24] - **Unsaturated fats** have **C=C double bonds**, so they are more reactive (oxidise, go rancid, can be hydrogenated); saturated fats have only single bonds.
[1:10:25] - **Sugarcane juice** is mainly **sucrose** (cane sugar).
[1:10:26] - **Cellulose** gives **no colour with iodine**; starch blue-black – both are plant glucose polymers.
[1:10:27] - **Formulae:** glucose/fructose **C6H12O6**; sucrose C12H22O11; starch (C6H10O5)n.
[1:10:28] - **Salted chips** make you thirsty – the extra **sodium** raises the salt concentration of body fluids.
## SUB 11 | Everyday Physics – Motion, Gravity, Fluids, Heat and States of Matter
[1:11:0] # Heat and states of matter
[1:11:1] - **Boiling points:** mercury ~357 °C > water 100 °C > benzene 80 °C > ethanol 78 °C (mercury boils last).
[1:11:2] - **Conduction:** iron from the fridge feels **colder** than wood at the same temperature – a good conductor draws heat from the hand faster.
[1:11:3] - **Changes of state:** **sublimation** = solid → gas; **deposition** = gas → solid.
[1:11:4] - **Convection** – air-conditioners and heaters circulate air (cool air sinks, warm air rises).
[1:11:5] - **Specific heat** = heat to raise **1 g by 1 °C**.
[1:11:6] - **Water expands ~9% on freezing** – a corked glass bottle full of water **breaks** in the freezer.
[1:11:7] - **Supercooling** = cooling a liquid **below its freezing point** without it solidifying.
[1:11:8] - **Water has a high specific heat** – a watermelon stays cool longer than an apple; kerosene ≈ **0.5 cal/g °C** (water 1); **1 calorie ≈ 4.2 J** (4.186).
[1:11:9] - **Dissolved salt raises the boiling point and lowers the freezing point** (colligative): salt in boiling water stops the boil until it reaches the new, higher boiling point; **rock salt melts ice** on roads.
[1:11:10] - **Pressure lowers the melting point of ice** (regelation) – ice skating; **melting ice stays at 0 °C** (latent heat of fusion); **triple point of water = 0.01 °C** (273.16 K, ~611.657 Pa).
[1:11:11] - **Mothballs (naphthalene)** work by **sublimation**.
[1:11:12] - **Anomalous expansion:** water contracts from 0 °C to **4 °C (maximum density)** and expands above it.
[1:11:13] - **Sound-proofing:** rockwool, wood, laminated glass – **not iron sheets**; **copper-bottomed pans** – copper conducts heat better than steel; a **small LPG burner** saves gas.
[1:11:14] # Motion, force and gravity
[1:11:15] - **Free fall:** without air resistance all bodies fall with the same g – brick and feather land **together** (Galileo; Apollo 15).
[1:11:16] - **Friction** acts in brakes, striking a match, walking – **not** in planets' revolution (gravity in near-vacuum).
[1:11:17] - A ball released from an orbiting satellite keeps its orbital speed and **revolves in the same orbit**.
[1:11:18] - **Motion** = change of position with time (strictly, change of position = displacement); **Newton's 1st law** (inertia) – rest or uniform motion unless an unbalanced force acts; 2nd F = ma; 3rd action–reaction.
[1:11:19] - **g decreases with depth** below the surface (and with height) – weight **decreases slightly** in a deep mine.
[1:11:20] - **Displacement** = shortest path between initial and final position (distance = actual path); at **maximum height** the velocity of a body thrown up is **0**.
[1:11:21] - **Friction** slows a moving object; **writing with a pencil** = friction (not gravity); gravity – planets, rolling rock, tides.
[1:11:22] - **Levers** are of **3 classes** (fulcrum, load or effort in the middle).
[1:11:23] - **Conservation of momentum:** no external force → momentum cannot change (a man on frictionless ice can move by whistling – printed key: both true, R not the explanation).
[1:11:24] # Pressure, fluids and gases
[1:11:25] - **Boyle's law:** V ∝ 1/P at constant T – an air bubble **grows** as it rises in a lake; **Charles's law:** V ∝ T at constant P.
[1:11:26] - **Bernoulli:** fast air blown between two sheets lowers the pressure there → the papers **come closer**.
[1:11:27] - **Surface tension** lets insects (water striders) walk on water.
[1:11:28] - **Floating:** stones in a boat displace their weight of water; thrown in, only their volume – the **lake level falls**.
[1:11:29] - **Density of water** ≈ **1 g/cm³** (1000 kg/m³), highest at 4 °C.
[1:11:30] - **Charles's law in daily life:** a balloon in **hot water swells** (air expands on heating).
[1:11:31] ~~ Sources: NextGen Science and Technology Group 2 Part 1 p.43-51; NextGen Rocket Physics p.30, p.86-94 (surface tension, specific heat, anomalous expansion); PMF General Science Part 1 p.16-19, Part 6 p.15-41 (heat, gas laws, states of matter); NCERT Class 9 Physics
## SUB 12 | Cell Biology, Biomolecules, Genetics and Respiration
[1:12:0] # Respiration
[1:12:1] - **Aerobic respiration** needs **oxygen** → CO2 + water + energy; anaerobic in muscle → lactic acid; in yeast → ethanol + CO2.
[1:12:2] - **Respiration** is **continuous** (day and night) and **exothermic**; **yeast fermentation** = **anaerobic respiration**; **sweating** (evaporation) is **endothermic**; composting is exothermic.
[1:12:3] - **Glycolysis** (1st stage of glucose oxidation) → **pyruvic acid**; ATP made via the electron transport chain = **oxidative phosphorylation** (photophosphorylation in photosynthesis).
[1:12:4] # Genetics and chromosomes
[1:12:5] - **Humans:** 46 chromosomes = **23 pairs** (22 autosome pairs + 1 sex pair).
[1:12:6] - **Genetic code:** **triplet codon**, nearly universal; **AUG** = start (methionine); **stop** = UAA, UAG, UGA.
[1:12:7] - **44 autosomes** + **XY sex chromosomes** (XX female, XY male; ZW in birds); genes pass through **both sperm and egg**.
[1:12:8] - **Variation** = differences among individuals of a species; the **limit of growth** is fixed by **internal (genetic, hormonal) factors**.
[1:12:9] - **DNA content** changes over the cell cycle: G1, telophase = 2C; G2, prophase = 4C; **somatic : gamete chromosomes = 2 : 1** (2n : n).
[1:12:10] # Cell, osmosis and diffusion
[1:12:11] - **Egg shell** = ~95% **calcium carbonate**; dissolved in acid, the egg's membrane is semi-permeable – the egg **shrinks** in salt water (hypertonic), swells in plain water.
[1:12:12] - **Cell organelles:** **nucleus** = control centre; **mitochondria** = **powerhouse** (ATP) – removing them **stops energy metabolism**; chloroplasts make food; nucleolus makes ribosomes.
[1:12:13] - **Plant cells only:** **cellulose cell wall** (both have vacuoles, membrane, Golgi); **ribosomes** – protein synthesis; **cell membrane** – regulates passage.
[1:12:14] # Biomolecules
[1:12:15] - **Cell composition (% of mass):** water 70–90; **proteins 10–15**; **nucleic acids 5–7**; **carbohydrates 3**; **lipids 2**.
[1:12:16] - **ATP → ADP** releases ~**30.5 kJ/mol** (7.3 kcal); **cellulose** = structural carbohydrate of plant cell walls; **chlorophyll's** central ion = **magnesium**.
[1:12:17] - **ATP = adenosine triphosphate** – the cell's **"energy molecule"** / energy currency.
[1:12:18] - **Cellulose** = **homopolymer** of β-glucose and the **most abundant organic compound** on Earth.
[1:12:19] # Reproduction
[1:12:20] - **Asexual reproduction:** **binary fission** – Amoeba; **budding** – yeast, Hydra; **regeneration** – Planaria; **spores** – Rhizopus.
[1:12:21] - **Amoeba** – binary fission, but **not all unicellular organisms are only asexual** (Paramecium conjugation, Chlamydomonas, yeast).
[1:12:22] # Evolution and origin of life
[1:12:23] - **Adaptive radiation** (one species → many forms for different niches) – printed key "adaptation"; **biogeny sequence:** coacervates → first cells (mycoplasma-like) → heterotrophs → aerobes.
[1:12:24] # Microbes and sterilisation
[1:12:25] - **Hot-air oven** sterilises glassware at **160 °C for 60 min** (170 °C/30 min, 180 °C/20 min).
[1:12:26] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Biology p.16, p.22, p.89; PMF General Science Part 1 p.40-46 (cell), Part 2 p.2, Part 4 p.15-38 (chromosomes, genetic code); NCERT Class 11 Biology ch. 8-9, 14 (cell, biomolecules, respiration)
## SUB 13 | Food Preservation, Ripening, Adulterants and Toxins
[1:13:0] # Preservatives and additives
[1:13:1] TABLE head: Preservative | Used in
  [1:13:1.0] Sodium benzoate / benzoic acid (E211) | Soft drinks, pickles, sauces (acidic foods)
  [1:13:1.1] Sorbic acid / potassium sorbate | Most common against moulds, yeasts, fungi and bacteria – cheese, pickles, cooked foods
  [1:13:1.2] Sodium metabisulphite | Jams, sauces
  [1:13:1.3] SO2 | Dried fruits, winemaking
  [1:13:1.4] Propionates | Stored grains, cheese
  [1:13:1.5] Vinegar (acetic acid) | Pickles; made by fermenting sugarcane juice
  [1:13:1.6] Boric acid | Old, no longer used (toxic)
[1:13:2] - **Food additives** = added **intentionally** to improve food (preservatives, colours, emulsifiers, sweeteners, flavour enhancers). **Adulteration** = adding inferior/harmful substances; causes poisoning, allergies, nutritional deficiency.
[1:13:3] - **MSG (Ajinomoto)** = flavour enhancer, **umami**. Aspartame and stevia are sweeteners; fructose is a natural sugar, not an artificial sweetener.
[1:13:4] - **Fortification:** iodised salt; rice with iron, folic acid, B12; oil/milk with A and D; FSSAI logo **"+F"**.
[1:13:5] - **Irradiation:** ionising (gamma) radiation kills organisms without much nutrient loss; potato/onion shelf life **8 months** without cold storage. **DAE: 25 irradiators in PPP mode**.
[1:13:6] - **PM Kisan Sampada Yojana** (MoFPI) Cold Chain scheme: 16 irradiation units approved, 9 operational (Aug 2025).
[1:13:7] - **Jul 2026:** food-safety reforms – Food Safety on Wheels, blockchain batch tracing, "One Nation, One Regulator" ends dual **BIS/AGMARK** certification.
[1:13:8] # Storage and freezing
[1:13:9] - **Cold storage** works because low temperature **lowers the rate of respiration** of fruits.
[1:13:10] - **IQF = Individually Quick Frozen** – each piece frozen separately and fast.
[1:13:11] - **Rancidity** = **oxidation** of fats and oils (so check the manufacture date of fried foods); nitrogen-flushed chip packets prevent it.
[1:13:12] - **Blackheart of potato** = **oxygen deficiency** in storage (an abiotic disorder).
[1:13:13] # Ripening
[1:13:14] - **Ethylene** = the gaseous plant hormone for **fruit ripening** (and the gas used for artificial ripening).
[1:13:15] - **Calcium carbide** releases **acetylene**, which acts like ethylene – the chemical (illegally) used to ripen mangoes.
[1:13:16] # Adulterants and harmful chemicals
[1:13:17] TABLE head: Food / chemical | Adulterant / found in
  [1:13:17.0] Mustard oil | Argemone oil/seeds – epidemic dropsy
  [1:13:17.1] Turmeric | Metanil yellow, lead chromate
  [1:13:17.2] Milk / chilli | Water, urea, starch / brick powder, Sudan dyes
  [1:13:17.3] Rhodamine B | Textile dye illegally used to colour cotton candy; carcinogenic; sale banned in Tamil Nadu (Feb 2024)
  [1:13:17.4] Aristolochic acid | Aristolochia plants in some herbal medicines
  [1:13:17.5] BPA | Polycarbonate plastics, food-can linings, thermal receipts
  [1:13:17.6] Triclosan | Antibacterial soaps, toothpaste, deodorants
  [1:13:17.7] Lead | Old paints, pipes, cosmetics, lead-chromate spices; not an MRI contrast agent
  [1:13:17.8] Phthalates | PVC plasticisers, toys, cosmetics
[1:13:18] - **Aflatoxins** = poisons made by **moulds** (*Aspergillus flavus*, *A. parasiticus*) on badly stored grain and oilseeds; not destroyed by cooking.
[1:13:19] - Mustard oil is **NOT a lubricant**: mineral oil = petroleum; vegetable fats = **phytosterol**; animal fats = **cholesterol**.
[1:13:20] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Chemistry p.51, p.68; NextGen Rocket Biology p.33, p.39; OnlyIAS NCERT S&T Part 7 (rancidity); app notes 5:1
[1:13:21] - **Artificial food colours** (tartrazine, sunset yellow) are mostly **coal-tar dyes**.
[1:13:22] - **Frozen storage:** meat such as pork at **−18 °C** or below; 3–5 °C is only chilling.
[1:13:23] - **Retrogradation** of starch (recrystallisation) = main cause of **staleness** of bread.
[1:13:24] - **Cut apple turns brown** – the enzyme **polyphenol oxidase** oxidises phenols in air (enzymatic browning).
[1:13:25] - **Endocrine disruptors:** man-made chemicals (BPA, phthalates, some pesticides) that mimic or block hormones.
[1:13:26] - **Food allergy signs:** flushing/hives, swelling of lips and throat (**difficulty swallowing, speaking**), **fall in blood pressure** (anaphylaxis); not atherosclerosis.
[1:13:27] - **Drying (sun drying, dehydration)** preserves food by removing the moisture microbes need.
[1:13:28] - **Silica gel** = the common **desiccant** (absorbs moisture in packets).
[1:13:29] - **Spoilage is prevented** by refrigeration, drying, salting, canning, preservatives – **exposure** (to air, moisture, microbes) causes it.
[1:13:30] - **Potassium bromate** (bread improver) removed from permitted additives by **FSSAI (2016)** – possible carcinogen.
## SUB 14 | Animal Kingdom – Classification, Anatomy and Veterinary Facts
[1:14:0] # Classification
[1:14:1] - **Aschelminthes (roundworms):** body **circular in cross-section** (hence the name); organ-system level; pseudocoelomate.
[1:14:2] - **Phylum features:** **Porifera** – ostia, osculum, choanocytes, **cellular level**; **Cnidaria** – **polyp and medusa**, **tissue level**; **Ctenophora** – comb plates, **bioluminescence**; **Platyhelminthes** – **organ level**; **Arthropoda** – jointed appendages; **Echinodermata** – **water vascular system**.
[1:14:3] - **Chordates vs non-chordates:** all chordates are bilateral, many non-chordates radial – so **bilateral symmetry** is the feature **not common** to both (printed key).
[1:14:4] - **Marsupial:** **koala** (pouch); **Clarias** = catfish (Pisces), not amphibian (Bufo, Rana, Hyla are); **emu** has **no crop**; **centipedes** – first legs → poison claws (**forcipules**).
[1:14:5] # Anatomy of animals
[1:14:6] - **Skull:** mammals and amphibians **dicondylic**; reptiles and birds **monocondylic**.
[1:14:7] - **Beaks:** insect-eaters **long and sharp**; seed-eaters short and strong.
[1:14:8] - **Grasshopper (insects)** breathe through **tracheae** (spiracles).
[1:14:9] - **Protozoa** (Amoeba) take food by **phagocytosis** and digest it with **enzymes in food vacuoles**.
[1:14:10] - **Birds** have **pneumatic (air-filled) bones**.
[1:14:11] - **Cockroach (insects):** **open circulation**, **tracheal respiration**; **cutaneous respiration** – **frog and earthworm**.
[1:14:12] # Animals in daily life
[1:14:13] - **Dog breeds:** **Chihuahua – Mexico** (not China).
[1:14:14] - **Snake venom:** **krait** (and cobra) – **neurotoxic**, neuroparalysis; Russell's and saw-scaled vipers – haemotoxic; pythons non-venomous.
[1:14:15] - **Wasp sting** (modified ovipositor) – to **defend** self/colony (most keys; solitary wasps paralyse prey); **chameleon** changes colour to **protect itself** (most keys; strictly signalling and temperature).
[1:14:16] - **Russell's viper** venom – **haemotoxic**, disrupts **blood clotting**; **poisonous** = harms if you bite/eat it, **venomous** = it bites/stings you.
[1:14:17] - **Worker bees** (sterile females) collect **nectar and pollen**, make honey and wax; queen lays eggs.
[1:14:18] # Veterinary science
[1:14:19] - **Veterinary:** **downer cow** – serum **creatine phosphokinase** rises; **left lung of cow – 3 lobes** (most keys); rumen **lactic acidosis** treated with alkalis – **not vinegar** (vinegar for urea poisoning); **dog pulse – femoral artery**; aborted **fetus** = post-mortem sample; **electuary** = drug paste with honey/jaggery.
[1:14:20] # Branches of study
[1:14:21] - **Saurology** = study of **lizards**.
[1:14:22] - **Branches:** **mycology** – fungi; **palynology** – pollen; **oncology** – tumours; **palaeontology** – fossils; entomology – insects; limnology – fresh waters.
[1:14:23] ~~ Sources: PMF General Science Part 1 p.88-95 (Porifera to Chordata), p.90 (tracheae), p.93 (snakes), Part 4 p.42 (marsupials); NCERT Class 11 Biology ch. 4; veterinary items: standard veterinary physiology texts, general knowledge, not checked (downer cow, cow lung lobes, electuary)
## SUB 15 | Everyday Physics – Light, Sound, Electricity and Magnetism
[1:15:0] # Light and colour
[1:15:1] - **Visible spectrum (VIBGYOR):** wavelength rises from violet to **red** (orange > yellow > indigo > violet); an opaque object shows the colour it **reflects**.
[1:15:2] - **TV remote** = **infrared** LED.
[1:15:3] - **Refraction** = change of direction (bending) of light passing into another medium: tank looks **shallower**; **prism dispersion** (each colour refracted differently); **twinkling of stars** and **Sun seen ~2 min before sunrise/after sunset** = **atmospheric refraction**.
[1:15:4] - **Scattering:** **blue sky**, **red Sun at sunrise/sunset**; the **rainbow** is **dispersion**, not scattering; a **pinhole camera's** smallest aperture is limited by **diffraction**.
[1:15:5] - **Grass looks green** – it **reflects green**; **red** has the longest visible wavelength (~620–750 nm).
[1:15:6] - **Dispersion** = splitting of white light into seven colours by a prism.
[1:15:7] - **Primary colours of light:** red, green, blue – **yellow is not** one.
[1:15:8] # Electricity
[1:15:9] - **Rectifier:** AC → DC (inverter: DC → AC).
[1:15:10] - **Indian supply: 230 V, 50 Hz** – a **110 V heater** draws ~4× its power and **burns out** within seconds/minutes.
[1:15:11] - **Resistivity** depends on the material and temperature, **not on shape** (resistance does); insulators – practically **infinite** resistance; a **semiconductor's resistance decreases** on heating (metal's increases).
[1:15:12] - **Short circuit:** resistance ≈ 0 → **current increases heavily**; a **fuse** melts on heavy current.
[1:15:13] - **Galvanometer:** detects current and its **direction**; null detector in bridges; more sensitive, not more accurate, than an ammeter.
[1:15:14] - **Transformer** works on **mutual induction**.
[1:15:15] # Sound
[1:15:16] - **Sound** is a **longitudinal** mechanical wave: **speed rises with temperature** (~0.6 m/s per °C in air); **solids** carry both longitudinal and transverse waves; **cannot travel in vacuum** (ultrasound too).
[1:15:17] - **Loudness** depends on **amplitude**; **pitch** on frequency.
[1:15:18] - **Echo:** reflected sound must arrive ≥ 0.1 s later → reflector at least **17.2 m** away (344 m/s); **women's voices** have **higher pitch** (shorter, thinner cords).
[1:15:19] - **Frequency** depends only on the **source** (same in every medium); speed and wavelength change with the medium.
[1:15:20] # Magnetism
[1:15:21] - **Magnetic shielding:** put the instrument inside **soft iron of high permeability**.
[1:15:22] ~~ Sources: NextGen Science and Technology Group 2 Part 2 p.52-55, Part 5 p.15; NextGen Rocket Physics p.18-35 (refraction, scattering, transformer, rectifier), p.53 (short circuit), p.57-85 (infrared, echo); PMF General Science Part 6 p.47-81 (light and sound); NCERT Class 10 Physics ch. 10-12
## SUB 16 | Food Plants, Milk and Cooking
[1:16:0] TABLE head: Product | Plant part / fact
  [1:16:0.0] Turmeric | Rhizome (underground stem) – not fruit, root or seed
  [1:16:0.1] Chicory (mixed with coffee) | Roasted root
  [1:16:0.2] Hing (asafoetida) | Dried gum-resin from the roots/rhizome of *Ferula*
  [1:16:0.3] Ginger | Rhizome
  [1:16:0.4] Rice | Parboiling (soak + steam) raises nutritive value – B vitamins move from the bran into the grain; polished raw rice → beri-beri
[1:16:1] # Milk
[1:16:2] - **Milk** = an **emulsion** (fat in water); **casein** is its main protein; **lactoderm** = protein skin on boiled milk.
[1:16:3] - **Cow's milk is slightly yellow** because of **carotene** (beta-carotene) in the milk fat; riboflavin only gives **whey** a greenish-yellow tint.
[1:16:4] - **Double toned milk = 1.5% fat** (9% SNF); toned 3%, standardised 4.5%.
[1:16:5] - **Lactometer** tests milk purity (specific gravity).
[1:16:6] # Cooking terms
[1:16:7] - **Poaching:** gentle cooking in a little hot liquid at **160–180 °F** (eggs, delicate foods); blanching = brief boiling; broiling = direct heat.
[1:16:8] - Fruits such as **papaya, mango, peaches, apricots** are rich in **antioxidants, carbohydrates and soluble fibre** (not proteins or fats).
[1:16:9] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Biology p.75; PMF General Science Part 3 p.4, p.31; Vedantu "Cow's milk is slightly yellowish" (carotene); double toned milk 1.5% fat: general knowledge, not checked; app notes 5:1
[1:16:10] - **Potato** = underground **stem (tuber)** (has eyes/buds); carrot, turnip = roots; groundnut = fruit.
[1:16:11] - **Sweet potato** = tuberous **root**; potato, ginger and sugarcane store food in **stems**.
[1:16:12] - **Caramelisation** of sucrose needs about **170 °C**.
[1:16:13] - **Boiling** – the most widely used cooking method.
[1:16:14] - **Plant pigments:** **lycopene** – red of tomato; **carotene** – orange (carrot); **lutein** – **yellow** xanthophyll (marigold, egg yolk, eye macula); anthocyanin – red/purple/blue; capsaicin – pungency of chilli (not a colour).
[1:16:15] - **Flower colours:** red, pink, blue, purple = **anthocyanins** (in cell sap); yellow/orange = carotenoids.
[1:16:16] - **Tomato red = lycopene** (a carotenoid); capsaicin gives chilli its heat.
[1:16:17] - **Clove** = dried unopened **flower bud** (*Syzygium aromaticum*).
[1:16:18] - **Sensory evaluation:** in a **ranking test** the judge arranges samples in increasing or decreasing order of one characteristic.
[1:16:19] - **Banana** is propagated vegetatively by **suckers** (cultivated bananas are seedless).
[1:16:20] - **Chilgoza** = edible seed of the **pine** *Pinus gerardiana*.
[1:16:21] - **Maillard reaction** = sugars + amino acids browning on heating – **bread → toast**.
[1:16:22] - **Rice** = *Oryza sativa*; **tomato** is in family **Solanaceae** (with potato, brinjal, chilli).
[1:16:23] - **Lycopene** = red carotenoid of **tomato** (also watermelon).
[1:16:24] - **Curd** has **lower pH** (~4–4.5) than milk (~6.5–6.7) – **lactic acid** from lactose.
[1:16:25] - **Sweet potato** = modified (tuberous) **root**; potato (tuber), arbi and jimikand (corms) = **stems**.
[1:16:26] - **Curd** should **not be kept in brass** (or copper) vessels – its acid forms toxic salts.
## SUB 17 | Instruments, Units, Scientists and Inventions
[1:17:0] # Scientists and discoveries
[1:17:1] - **Physicists:** **Röntgen** – X-rays; **V.F. Hess** – cosmic rays; **E.O. Lawrence** – cyclotron; **C.H. Townes** – maser.
[1:17:2] - **Biologists:** **Micheli** – mycology; **Cuvier** – palaeontology; **Virchow** – pathology; **Stephen Hales** – plant physiology.
[1:17:3] - **Millikan's oil-drop experiment** – charge of the electron (1.59 × 10⁻¹⁹ C in 1913; now 1.602 × 10⁻¹⁹ C).
[1:17:4] - **William Gilbert, De Magnete (1600)** – Earth as a giant magnet explains the compass; **John Dalton** – atomic theory (**1808**); **Wöhler** – **urea (1828)**, first synthetic organic compound.
[1:17:5] - **Higgs boson** (CERN, 2012) explains **why particles have mass**.
[1:17:6] - **Inventors:** **Cockerell** – hovercraft; **David Bushnell** – submarine (Turtle, 1775); **J.C. Périer** – steamship; microscope – **Leeuwenhoek** (official key; compound microscope – Janssen c. 1590).
[1:17:7] # Instruments
[1:17:8] - **Metric chain (surveying):** 20 m chain = **100 links** of 20 cm.
[1:17:9] - **Instruments:** **hygrometer** – humidity; **ammeter** – current; **odometer** – distance; **anemometer** – wind speed; **tachometer** – rotational speed (rpm); **pyrometer** – very high temperature; **hydrometer** – relative density of liquids; **epidiascope** – projects opaque objects and transparencies.
[1:17:10] - **Odometer** = distance travelled by a vehicle (speedometer = speed).
[1:17:11] - **Ammeter** (in series) measures **current**; voltmeter – potential difference.
[1:17:12] - **Mercury thermometer:** range **−35 to 673 °F** (printed; mercury freezes −38.8 °C, boils 356.7 °C).
[1:17:13] # Units and prefixes
[1:17:14] - **Viscosity:** **poise** (CGS); SI unit **pascal-second** (1 Pa·s = 10 P).
[1:17:15] - **SI base units:** current – **ampere**; temperature – **kelvin**; luminous intensity – **candela**; mass – kg; length – m; time – s; amount – mole.
[1:17:16] - **Prefixes:** **deka = 10**, hecto = 10², kilo = 10³.
[1:17:17] - **Micro = 10⁻⁶** (1 µs = 10⁻⁶ s); **least count** = smallest change an instrument can resolve (metre scale 1 mm).
[1:17:18] - **Steradian** = SI unit of **solid angle** (radian – plane angle); **watt** = power = energy per unit time (11 W bulb uses 11 J/s).
[1:17:19] - **1 acre = 4,046.86 m²** (~0.4047 ha); 1 hectare = 10,000 m².
[1:17:20] ~~ Sources: NextGen Rocket Physics p.2, p.6-12 (discoveries, SI units), p.19 (Millikan); NextGen Rocket Chemistry p.7-9 (Wöhler, Dalton); PMF General Science Part 5 p.41 (SI units); instruments and surveying units: NCERT Class 11 Physics ch. 2, general knowledge
## SUB 18 | Transport, Smart Materials, Public-Health Tech and Impacts
[1:18:0] TABLE head: Technology | Fact
  [1:18:0.0] FASTag | Passive RFID
  [1:18:0.1] Metro card | Passive NFC, 13.56 MHz, ~4 cm
  [1:18:0.2] GPS | India's own: NavIC
  [1:18:0.3] V2X | C-V2X = 4G/5G cellular; DSRC = Wi-Fi based
  [1:18:0.4] Hydrogen fuel cell | Emits only water
[1:18:1] - EVs: **PM E-DRIVE** scheme (**Ministry of Heavy Industries**) extended to **31 March 2028**.
[1:18:2] - **Hydrogels:** up to 99% water; **contact lenses, wound dressings, diapers, drug delivery**. **MEMS accelerometer** – triggers **airbags**, **laptop free-fall detection**, step counting. **Flexible batteries** – wearables and flexible displays.
[1:18:3] TABLE head: Platform | Use
  [1:18:3.0] IHIP of IDSP | Integrated Health Information Platform – GIS real-time disease surveillance

  [1:18:5.0] Communication, flexible work | Decreased privacy

  [1:18:5.2] Digital payments | Cyber fraud, digital divide
[1:18:6] - **Aug 2026:** Supreme Court sought the Centre's reply on a plea on "high-risk" AI – **biometric surveillance** and **risk-scoring** in food, health, pension and scholarship delivery.
[1:18:7] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:1 (sub 5, 7-9)
[1:18:8] - **Accelerometer** also detects **tilt** – phone screen rotation between portrait and landscape.
[1:18:9] - **Environment:** **reusable** glass/metal cups are best; paper (plastic-lined) and plastic cups are single-use.
[1:18:10] - **Fuel efficiency of cars:** **streamlined body** (less drag), **multipoint fuel injection**, radial tyres (less rolling resistance); **catalytic converter** = emission control only.
[1:18:11] - **Kohlberg's moral stages:** 1 punishment-avoidance → 2 self-interest → 3 good boy/girl → 4 **law and order** → 5 **social contract** → 6 **universal ethical principle** (highest).
[1:18:12] - **Black box** (flight recorder, actually orange) – fitted in the **tail** of aircraft.
## SUB 19 | Off-syllabus Items Filed Here – Engineering, Statistics, Psychology, Language
[1:19:0] # Engineering and maths (off-syllabus)
[1:19:1] - **DG sets:** **10% overload** permitted for 1 hour in every 12 hours.
[1:19:2] - **Exterior angles** of a convex polygon sum to **360°**; **π** = circumference ÷ diameter, **irrational**.
[1:19:3] - **Wood science:** wood neither gaining nor losing moisture is at its **equilibrium moisture content**; water within the cell walls = **bound (hygroscopic) water**, in the cavities = free water.
[1:19:4] - **Fluorescent tube:** the **choke** strikes the arc and stabilises it; the **capacitor** across the supply **improves the power factor**; **transformer** max efficiency at constant load current – **unity power factor**; a **synchronous motor's damper winding** gives **no torque** at synchronous speed.
[1:19:5] - **Statistics:** ranked data = **ordinal scale**; budget "rupee goes to" = **pie chart**; **regression** – **Francis Galton** (correlation – Karl Pearson).
[1:19:6] - **Surveying:** **ranging rod** – marks station positions, ranges lines; **dumpy level** – simpler, fewer and longer-lasting adjustments (quick testing of adjustments is the **wye level's** advantage).
[1:19:7] - **Power engineering:** earth resistance at large power stations ≤ **0.5 Ω**; DG set rated power factor **0.8** lagging; transformer no-load current **lags ~75°**; salient-pole synchronous motor at no load **keeps running** at synchronous speed with field off (reluctance torque); a chain that is **too long** → **positive** correction.
[1:19:8] - **Dry rot** of timber – the **dry rot fungus** (*Serpula lacrymans*).
[1:19:9] - **Prismatic compass** measures bearings – **not gradients** (clinometer, Ceylon ghat tracer do).
[1:19:10] # Psychology and education (off-syllabus)
[1:19:11] - **Pavlov's classical conditioning:** independent variable = **number of CS–US pairings** (bell–food).
[1:19:12] - **Humanistic realism** (16th-century): Erasmus, Rabelais, Milton – **not Aristotle**; **Sri Aurobindo** – world evolving to greater perfection that is God; **Wallas's creativity stages:** preparation, incubation, **illumination** ("Eureka"), verification.
[1:19:13] - **IQ** = mental age ÷ chronological age × 100 (MA 6, CA 10 → **60**).
[1:19:14] - **Latent learning** – **Edward Tolman** (rats in mazes, cognitive maps).
[1:19:15] - **Kallikak family** – **Goddard**; **Kurt Lewin** = field theory, **not a behaviourist** (Pavlov, Watson, Skinner are).
[1:19:16] # Everyday technology
[1:19:17] - **Car child lock** – rear door **cannot be opened from inside**.
[1:19:18] - A **dripping tap** wastes ~**1,200 litres** a year.
[1:19:19] - **Photocopier drum** – photoconductive **selenium**; **holography** – printed key "navigators" (widely used in museums and security); **John Logie Baird** – television (1926).
[1:19:20] # Language and reasoning (off-syllabus)
[1:19:21] - **Animal movement words:** owl **flits**, horse **gallops**, eagle **swoops**, bear **lumbers**; "loiter aimlessly" = **hang out**.
[1:19:22] ~~ Sources: printed keys of the APPSC papers concerned (AEE, Forest, Agriculture and education papers); standard texts – electrical machines (B.L. Theraja), surveying (Punmia), educational psychology (S.K. Mangal); general knowledge, not checked
