  x: Option C is incorrect: among fruits, mango is the richest vitamin A source; carrot is a vegetable source. The vitamin C sources are right.
[nbiology-25-4] sub4
  Which of the following is INCORRECTLY matched?
  *Umetaro Suzuki – Associated vitamin C with citrus curing scurvy | Frederick Gowland Hopkins – Accessory food factors | Casimir Funk – Coined 'vitamine' | James Lind – Citrus fruits curing scurvy
  x: Suzuki extracted a water-soluble micronutrient mixture from rice bran; James Lind linked citrus with curing scurvy.
[nbiology-6-8] sub4
  Which of the following is INCORRECTLY matched?
  Wheat germ oil – Source of vitamin E | Carbohydrate + fat + protein – Maximum energy-yielding diet group | ATP → ADP – Biotransformation providing maximum energy | *Rice – Source of vitamin E
  x: Rice is noted for the highest percentage of carbohydrate; vitamin E comes from wheat germ oil.
[nbiology-25-1] sub4
  Who was associated with the discovery of Vitamin A?
  James Lind and Umetaro Suzuki | Frederick Gowland Hopkins and Casimir Funk | *Elmer Verner McCollum and Marguerite Davis | Casimir Funk and Marguerite Davis
  x: Elmer McCollum and Marguerite Davis discovered vitamin A in 1913. Lind linked citrus with scurvy and Funk coined "vitamine".
[f8b018f9bcdb5] sub4
  Which condition is caused by deficiency of vitamin B5 (pantothenic acid)?
  Pellagra | *Burning feet | Beri-beri | Scurvy
  x: B5 deficiency causes burning feet; pellagra is due to B3 (niacin) and beri-beri to B1.
[f2fbe875c1443] sub4
  Who coined the term 'vitamine'?
  James Lind | Hopkins | McCollum | *Casimir Funk
  x: Casimir Funk coined 'vitamine'; James Lind showed citrus cures scurvy.
[nbiology-25-6] sub4
  Elmer Verner McCollum, credited with the discovery of vitamin A, also played a key role in the discovery of which other vitamin?
  Vitamin C | *Vitamin D | Vitamin K | Vitamin E
  x: McCollum, who co-discovered vitamin A in 1913, identified vitamin D in 1922 as the anti-rickets factor in cod-liver oil. Vitamin C was isolated by Szent-Györgyi, and vitamin E was found by Evans and Bishop.
[gffcc117a3a66] sub4
  Which vitamins are made by bacteria in the human gut?
  Vitamin C and A | *Vitamin B12 and K | Vitamin D and E | Vitamin B1 and C
  x: Gut bacteria make vitamin B12 and vitamin K; vitamin D is made in the skin in sunlight.
[g75f82627dde0] sub4
  Deficiency of pyridoxine (vitamin B6) leads to which of the following?
  Scurvy and bleeding gums | *Anaemia and dermatitis | Night blindness | Rickets
  x: Vitamin B6 is needed for amino-acid metabolism and antibody production; its lack causes anaemia and dermatitis.
[g8fa5f5fa706c] sub4
  Deficiency of which vitamin causes sterility?
  Vitamin A | Vitamin D | *Vitamin E | Vitamin K
  x: Vitamin E (tocopherol) deficiency is linked with sterility; vitamin A causes night blindness and vitamin D rickets.
[nchemistry-15-13] sub7
  Which of the following acts as an antibacterial preservative?
  Aspartame | Fructose | Stevia | *Formic acid
  x: Formic acid acts as an antibacterial preservative. Aspartame and stevia are sweeteners and fructose is a natural sugar, so none of them is an antibacterial preservative.
[g1dda1b41f761] sub7
  Which acid is present in cola drinks?
  Citric acid | *Phosphoric acid | Formic acid | Acetic acid
  x: Colas contain phosphoric acid and caffeine; citric acid is in lemon and formic acid in ant bites.
[nbiology-27-2] sub9
  Which is the major protein of milk?
  Gluten | *Casein | α-Keratin | Collagen
  x: Casein is the major milk protein; gluten is in wheat, collagen in connective tissue and keratin in hair and nails.
[nbiology-27-3] sub9
  Approximately how much energy does one gram of protein provide?
  2 kcal | 9 kcal | *4 kcal | 7 kcal
  x: One gram of protein gives about 4 kcal of energy; fat gives about 9.3 kcal per gram.
[nbiology-27-5] sub9
  Deficiency of which nutrient causes kwashiorkor and marasmus?
  Iron | Vitamin A | Iodine | *Protein
  x: Kwashiorkor and marasmus are protein-deficiency diseases.
[nbiology-27-9] sub9
  A nursing mother requires at least how much protein per day?
  *65 g | 55 g | 45 g | 85 g
  x: A nursing mother needs at least about 65 g of protein a day; other women need about 45–46 g.
[g6c8a28d57f26] sub9
  How many amino acids are commonly found in proteins, and how many of them are essential?
  *20 and 10 | 22 and 8 | 20 and 5 | 12 and 6
  x: Proteins are built from 20 amino acids, of which 10 are essential and must come from food.
[ga19dc4831dcb] sub9
  Which protein is found in wheat?
  Casein | *Gluten | Keratin | Fibroin
  x: Gluten is the wheat protein; casein is in milk, keratin in hair and nails, fibroin in silk.
[g346bd7b27e0e] sub9
  Which grain has the highest protein content?
  Rice | Wheat | *Soybean | Maize
  x: Soybean has the highest protein content; rice is mainly a source of carbohydrate.
[nbiology-25-5] sub10
  What is the main source of energy in the body?
  Minerals | *Carbohydrates | Vitamins | Water
  x: Carbohydrates, not minerals, are the main energy source; amino acids build proteins.
[nbiology-6-6] sub10
  Which oilseed has the maximum α-linolenic acid?
  Groundnut | Mustard | *Linseed | Sunflower
  x: Linseed oil has the maximum α-linolenic acid among oilseeds.
[f4fe05231a873] sub10
  Fat provides about how much energy per gram?
  4 kcal | *9.3 kcal | 6 kcal | 2 kcal
  x: Fat gives about 9.3 kcal per gram, against about 4 kcal for protein.
[n93fd3f6a7b2] sub13
  Which of the following food–adulterant pairs is NOT correctly matched?
  Chilli powder — Brick powder or Sudan dyes | Milk — Water, detergent, urea or starch | Mustard oil — Argemone oil | *Turmeric — Argemone oil
  x: Turmeric is adulterated with metanil yellow or lead chromate; argemone oil is the adulterant of mustard oil.
[n6f71c9b3e34] sub13
  Adulteration of mustard oil with which substance causes epidemic dropsy?
  Metanil yellow | *Argemone oil | Sudan dyes | Brick powder
  x: Argemone oil is a common adulterant of mustard oil and is associated with epidemic dropsy.
[ndc01dbb90c7] sub13
  Rhodamine B is associated with which of the following products and hazards?
  *Cotton candy colouring and carcinogenicity | Toothpaste and antimicrobial resistance | Thermal receipts and endocrine disruption | PVC toys and kidney damage
  x: Rhodamine B is an industrial textile dye illegally used to colour cotton candy and is carcinogenic. Its sale was banned in Tamil Nadu.
[n546158c0cca] sub13
  Which one of the following pairs is NOT correctly matched?
  Triclosan — Antibacterial soaps and toothpaste | BPA — Polycarbonate plastics and thermal receipts | Phthalates — PVC, toys and cosmetics | *Lead — MRI contrast agent
  x: Lead is associated with old paints, pipes, some cosmetics and lead-chromate-adulterated spices, not MRI contrast agents. BPA, triclosan and phthalates are correctly matched with their listed sources.
[f533908890859] sub13
  In which of the following is the harmful chemical aristolochic acid found?
  Polycarbonate plastics | *Some herbal medicines | Antibacterial soaps | Thermal receipts
  x: Aristolochic acid comes from Aristolochia plants used in some herbal medicines.
[f505334affba9] sub13
  In which of the following is Bisphenol A (BPA) commonly found?
  *Polycarbonate plastics and food-can linings | Antibacterial toothpaste | Herbal medicines | Cotton candy colouring
  x: BPA is found in polycarbonate plastics, food-can linings and thermal receipts.
[f81f9710ef098] sub13
  For what purpose are phthalates mainly used?
  Food preservatives | Antibacterial agents | Textile dyes | *Plasticisers in PVC
  x: Phthalates are PVC plasticisers found in toys and cosmetics.
[f086d7a5b5d7a] sub13
  With which of the following is turmeric commonly adulterated?
  Argemone oil | Urea | *Metanil yellow | Brick powder
  x: Turmeric is adulterated with metanil yellow and lead chromate; argemone oil goes into mustard oil and brick powder into chilli.
[nchemistry-15-2] sub13
  Which of the following statements is incorrect?
  Sodium benzoate is used in soft drinks, acidic foods and fruit juice. | Propionic acid or sodium propionate is used in stored grains, cheese and milk. | *Boric acid is a commonly preferred modern food preservative. | Sodium metabisulphite is used in jams, sauces and pickles.
  x: Option C is incorrect: boric acid is an old preservative, no longer preferred because it is toxic. Sodium benzoate, propionates and metabisulphite are current preservatives.
[nchemistry-15-9] sub13
  Which food preservative is INCORRECTLY matched with its use?
  Sodium benzoate – Soft drinks and fruit juice | Potassium sorbate – Cheese and pickles | Sodium propionate – Stored grains, cheese and milk | *Sulphur dioxide – Stored grains and milk
  x: Sulphur dioxide preserves dried fruits and is used in winemaking; propionates preserve grains and cheese.
[nchemistry-15-16] sub13
  Assertion (A): Monosodium glutamate is a flavour enhancer.
Reason (R): Monosodium glutamate provides umami flavour.
  *Both A and R are true, and R is the correct explanation of A | Both A and R are true, but R is NOT the correct explanation of A | A is true, but R is false | A is false, but R is true
  x: Both are true and R explains A: MSG gives the savoury umami taste, which is why it enhances flavour.
[n1df40f05f89] sub13
  Which radiation is used in food irradiation to destroy harmful organisms without much loss of nutrition?
  Infrared radiation | Microwave radiation | *Ionising radiation | Strong magnetic fields
  x: Food irradiation uses ionising radiation to destroy harmful organisms without altering nutrition substantially.
[n3e662acc975] sub13
  How many food irradiators has the Department of Atomic Energy installed in PPP mode?
  20 | 9 | 16 | *25
  x: DAE has installed 25 food irradiators in PPP mode; gamma rays extend potato and onion shelf life to eight months.
[n25fcedf53a1] sub13
  FSSAI marks fortified food with which logo?
  Agmark | *+F | Jaivik Bharat | Green dot
  x: FSSAI uses the '+F' logo for fortified foods, such as iodised salt.
[ne06deb97e7e] sub13
  The food-safety reforms of July 2026 proposed ending dual certification under which two systems?
  BIS and FSSAI | ISI and Hallmark | *BIS and AGMARK | AGMARK and FPO
  x: The July 2026 reforms included ending dual BIS/AGMARK certification, alongside Food Safety on Wheels, blockchain tracing and One Nation, One Regulator.
[nbfe0d80649e] sub13
  What does food adulteration refer to?
  Maintaining food at a controlled low temperature during transport | Adding micronutrients to improve nutritional value | Using ionising radiation to destroy harmful organisms | *Intentionally adding inferior or harmful substances to food
  x: Adulteration involves the intentional addition of inferior or harmful substances. It can cause acute poisoning, allergies and nutritional deficiency.
[f2e34069a63d4] sub13
  Which preservative is used for dried fruits and in winemaking?
  *Sulphur dioxide | Propionates | Sodium benzoate | Sorbic acid
  x: SO2 preserves dried fruits and is used in winemaking; propionates are used for stored grains and cheese.
[fe313c5818bf4] sub13
  Which preservative is no longer used because it is toxic?
  Propionates | Potassium sorbate | *Boric acid | Sodium metabisulphite
  x: Boric acid is an old preservative no longer used because it is toxic.
[g118aa4b91f10] sub13
  Which of the following is a natural sugar and not an artificial sweetener?
  Aspartame | Saccharin | *Fructose | Sucralose
  x: Fructose is the natural sugar of fruits and honey; aspartame, saccharin and sucralose are artificial sweeteners.
[g0e61efae0bc2] sub13
  Which ministry runs the Cold Chain scheme under the PM Kisan Sampada Yojana?
  Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare | *Ministry of Food Processing Industries | Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution | Department of Atomic Energy
  x: PM Kisan Sampada Yojana, including its Cold Chain scheme with irradiation units, belongs to the Ministry of Food Processing Industries.
[g9f7b07592502] sub16
  Why does parboiling raise the nutritive value of rice?
  Starch is converted into protein | *B vitamins move from the bran into the grain | Vitamin C is added by steaming | The fat content of the grain rises
  x: In parboiling (soaking and steaming) B vitamins move from the bran into the grain, so they are not lost on polishing.
[ndaa5a5ef0a2] sub18
  'Anna Chakra', seen in the news, is associated with which of the following?
  *Optimising Public Distribution System supply routes to fair price shops | Irradiating potatoes and onions to extend shelf life | Certifying fortified food with the '+F' logo | Blockchain batch tracing of packaged food
  x: Anna Chakra optimises PDS routes to about 4.37 lakh fair price shops, saving about Rs 250 crore a year. Irradiation of potatoes and onions is a DAE gamma-ray application; the '+F' logo is FSSAI's fortification mark; blockchain batch tracing is one of the July 2026 food-safety reforms.
[nfd1d16bf0e5] sub18
  Which technologies did Aarogya Setu use for COVID-19 contact tracing?
  Wi-Fi signals only | NFC and RFID | *Bluetooth and GPS | Infrared sensors
  x: Aarogya Setu used Bluetooth and GPS for COVID-19 contact tracing, not Wi-Fi alone.
[n290b184828a] sub18
  GIS-enabled real-time disease surveillance is carried out through which platform of the Integrated Disease Surveillance Programme?
  National Medical Commission Platform | National One Health Mission | Health Facility Mapping System | *Integrated Health Information Platform
  x: The Integrated Health Information Platform, or IHIP, of IDSP supports GIS-enabled real-time disease surveillance.
[n2f2f10fcc5c] sub18
  The National One Health Mission brings together which sectors to address zoonoses and antimicrobial resistance?
  Health, Agriculture, Finance and Railways | *Health, Animal Husbandry, Environment and Science and Technology | Environment, Education, Defence and Commerce | Animal Husbandry, Tourism, Housing and Heavy Industries
  x: The mission joins the Health, Animal Husbandry, Environment and Science and Technology ministries to address zoonoses and antimicrobial resistance.
[n810c5152ff6] sub18
  Which sensor detects sudden deceleration to trigger a car's airbags?
  Proximity sensor | Magnetometer | *Accelerometer | Ambient light sensor
  x: An accelerometer senses sudden deceleration to trigger airbags; it also enables step counting and screen rotation.
[n7674c9a4d21] sub18
  What are flexible batteries using graphene or carbon-fibre electrodes meant to power?
  only railway locomotives | only household refrigerators | *wearables and flexible displays | large hydroelectric turbines
  x: Graphene- or carbon-fibre-electrode flexible batteries can power wearables and flexible displays.
[n9f755ffee7f] sub18
  Hydrogels find application in which of the following groups?
  *Contact lenses, wound dressings, diapers and drug delivery | Solar cells, transformers, magnets and satellites | Brake fluids, cement, glass and steel | Tyres, batteries, optical fibres and semiconductors only
  x: Hydrogels are water-absorbing polymers used in contact lenses, wound dressings, diapers and drug delivery.
[f6783feb1a6e3] sub18
  Besides triggering airbags, for what are MEMS accelerometers used?
  Drug delivery | Desalination | Wound dressing | *Laptop free-fall detection
  x: MEMS accelerometers trigger airbags, detect laptop free fall and count steps.
[n2b34c9d4653] sub18
  Which of the following correctly distinguishes FASTag from a metro card?
  FASTag uses passive NFC, whereas a metro card uses active RFID powered by a battery. | *FASTag uses passive RFID, whereas a metro card uses passive NFC at 13.56 MHz and about 4 cm range. | FASTag uses active GPS, whereas a metro card uses passive Bluetooth. | Both use only satellite communication and require an internal battery.
  x: FASTag is a passive RFID system powered by the reader's field. Metro cards use passive NFC, generally at 13.56 MHz and about 4 cm range.
[n20f2d1249ef] sub18
  Which vehicle communication technology is Wi-Fi-based?
  *DSRC | C-V2X | LTE-M | NB-IoT
  x: DSRC is Wi-Fi-based, while C-V2X uses 4G and 5G networks.
[n6a2e603fa21] sub18
  What is the only emission from a hydrogen fuel-cell vehicle?
  Carbon dioxide | *Water | Methane | Carbon monoxide
  x: A fuel cell combines hydrogen and oxygen to produce electricity, emitting only water.
[n7d11bc3c2ef] sub18
  What is the name of India's own satellite-based navigation system?
  DSRC | IHIP | C-V2X | *NavIC
  x: NavIC is India's own satellite-based navigation system and supports location-related services.
[fc8a75994b94f] sub18
  On which technology is C-V2X vehicle communication based?
  Wi-Fi | *4G/5G cellular networks | NFC | Bluetooth
  x: C-V2X uses 4G/5G; DSRC is the Wi-Fi-based technology.
[f8e43c421b573] sub18
  Which ministry runs the PM E-DRIVE scheme for electric vehicles?
  *Ministry of Heavy Industries | Ministry of Road Transport and Highways | Ministry of Power | NITI Aayog
  x: PM E-DRIVE is a scheme of the Ministry of Heavy Industries.
[fe7ecf8ac1d51] sub18
  Up to which date has the PM E-DRIVE scheme been extended?
  31 March 2026 | 31 March 2027 | *31 March 2028 | 31 March 2030
  x: PM E-DRIVE has been extended to 31 March 2028.
[n66bf1a9d899] sub18
  Which of the following is a negative consequence of digital technology?
  Smarter health tracking | Digital payments | E-governance | *Cyber fraud
  x: Cyber fraud (like screen-time effects) is a negative consequence; health tracking and digital payments are benefits.
[n6a9175a63c3] sub18
  The Supreme Court plea on the absence of a statutory regime for high-risk AI involved which technologies?
  MRI and CT scanning used only for fracture diagnosis | *Biometric surveillance and risk-scoring used in food, health, pension and scholarship delivery | Gamma irradiation used only for potatoes and onions | Blockchain used exclusively for PDS route optimisation
  x: In August 2026, the Supreme Court sought the Centre's reply on a plea concerning high-risk AI, including biometric surveillance and risk-scoring in food, health, pension and scholarship delivery.
[n442ea4c1427] sub18
  Which one of the following is NOT a positive impact of technology on daily life?
  Improved communication | Flexible working | *Decreased privacy | Smarter health tracking through wearables
  x: Decreased privacy is a negative impact of technology (APPSC-GS 2022). Improved communication, flexible working and smarter health tracking through wearables are positive impacts.
[fb0fbe25ea862] sub18
  Consider the following statements about the impacts of technology:
1. Flexible work is a positive impact.
2. Eye strain from screen time is a negative impact.
3. Decreased privacy is a positive impact.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  *1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | 1 and 3 only | 1, 2 and 3
  x: Flexible work is positive and screen-time eye strain negative; decreased privacy is a negative impact.

=== NOTES 5:2 | Science, Technology and Innovation Policy - 1958 to Stip 2020
## SUB 0 | Science Days, Themes and Scientific Social Responsibility
[2:0:0] # National Science Day – 28 February
[2:0:1] - Marks C.V. Raman's discovery of the **Raman Effect** (28 Feb 1928); declared in 1986.
[2:0:2] TABLE head: Year | Theme
  [2:0:2.0] 2021 | Future of STI: Impact on Education, Skills and Work
  [2:0:2.1] 2022 | Integrated Approach in S&T for Sustainable Future (not "Women in Science")
  [2:0:2.2] 2023 | Global Science for Global Wellbeing
  [2:0:2.3] 2026 | Women in Science: Catalysing Viksit Bharat
[2:0:3] - **⚠ Trap:** NSD 2022 was held on **28 Feb 2022** but its theme was **not** "Women in Science" → only statement A true (TSPSC AEE 2023). "Radiation and Radioisotopes in Healthcare" is a distractor.
[2:0:4] # National Technology Day – 11 May
[2:0:5] - Marks **Pokhran-II** (11 May 1998); declared 1999.
[2:0:6] - **Scientific Social Responsibility (SSR) Guidelines 2022:** released by **DST, Ministry of Science and Technology**, on National Technology Day, **11 May 2022** (with the SRIMAN guidelines); scientists' voluntary engagement with society through anchor institutions.
[2:0:7] ~~ Sources: Praveen CA360 Feb 2026 one-liners item 201; Praveen CA360 Feb 2026 magazine p.13; DST SSR Guidelines 2022 (dst.gov.in, search result); app notes 5:2 (sub 6)
## SUB 1 | Innovation and Research Concepts
[2:1:0] - **Adopters of an innovation (Rogers' diffusion of innovations):** **Innovators → Early Adopters → Early Majority → Late Majority → Laggards** (APPSC G2).
[2:1:1] - **Validity of a study:** it measures what it claims to measure; **external validity** = the results can be **generalised** beyond the sample (APPSC G2 answer: "results can be generalised").
[2:1:2] - **Decade of Innovation: 2010-2020**; vehicle = **National Innovation Council** under **Sam Pitroda**; inclusive innovation.
[2:1:3] ~~ Sources: general knowledge, not checked (Rogers, validity); app notes 5:2 (sub 4)
## SUB 2 | India's S&T Policies – 1958 to STIP 2020
[2:2:0] # The five policies
[2:2:1] TABLE head: Policy | Year | PM | Core thrust
  [2:2:1.0] Scientific Policy Resolution (SPR) – first | 1958 | Nehru | "Foster, promote and sustain" science – pure, applied, educational; state-led institution building (national labs, IITs)
  [2:2:1.1] Technology Policy Statement (TPS) | 1983 | Indira Gandhi | Technological competence and self-reliance, less import dependence
  [2:2:1.2] Science and Technology Policy (STP) | 2003 | Vajpayee | Science and technology together; innovation, PPP, IPR, more R&D investment
  [2:2:1.3] STI Policy | 2013 | Manmohan Singh | First to add Innovation; "STI for the People"
  [2:2:1.4] Draft STIP 2020 – fifth | 2020 | Modi | Decentralised, bottom-up, inclusive design; Atmanirbhar Bharat, open science, equity
[2:2:2] - **Order to remember:** 1958 – 1983 – 2003 – 2013 – 2020 = Nehru – Indira – Vajpayee – Manmohan – Modi.
[2:2:3] - **⚠ Trap:** first Science Policy Resolution = **1958**, Technology (Policy) Statement = **1983** – "1958 and 1983 respectively" (TREI-RB 2023); not the other way round, not both in one year.
[2:2:4] - **SPR 1958** came during the **Second Five Year Plan** (1956-61).
[2:2:5] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 1 (pmf-science-tech-01) p.41-43 [book p.24-26]; NextGen S&T Group 2 Part 1 p.3-6; app notes 5:2 (sub 0)
## SUB 3 | Objectives of Each Policy
[2:3:0] # STP 2003
[2:3:1] - **Launched:** **3 January 2003** (Indian Science Congress); research, innovation, technology transfer, more R&D investment.
[2:3:2] - **Objectives include all four** asked by UPPSC 2008: **optimal utilisation of existing physical and knowledge resources**, **development of innovative technologies**, **systems and technologies for mitigation and management of natural hazards**, and **management of intellectual property** → "All the four".
[2:3:3] - **GERD target:** **2% of GDP by the end of the Tenth Plan**.
[2:3:4] # STI Policy 2013
[2:3:5] - India among the **top five** scientific powers by 2020; publications share **3.5% → over 7%**; about **10 high-impact sectors** for direct STI intervention.
[2:3:6] - **"Small idea-small money"**, **Risky Idea Fund**, **Fund for Innovations for Social Inclusion**; GERD to **2%** with private spending matching public.
[2:3:7] # Draft STIP 2020
[2:3:8] - Vision: among the **top three** scientific superpowers in the coming decade; **double FTE researchers, GERD and private GERD share every 5 years**.
[2:3:9] - Proposals: **One Nation, One Subscription**, **STI Development Bank** (corpus for long-term strategic areas), **RIEF**, National STI Observatory, open science (INDSTA archive), National Research Foundation with private/CSR (hybrid) funding.
[2:3:10] - **⚠ Trap:** **Atal Innovation Mission** is NOT a STIP 2020 outcome – set up by **NITI Aayog in 2016**. Top five by 2020 = STI 2013 (not STP 2003).
[2:3:11] - **ONOS** approved Nov 2024, about **Rs 6,000 crore** (not Rs 60,000 crore).
[2:3:12] ~~ Sources: NextGen S&T Group 2 Part 1 p.3-6; PMF S&T Part 1 p.42-43; app notes 5:2 (sub 1)
## SUB 4 | DST Schemes – Women and Young Talent
[2:4:0] TABLE head: Scheme | Purpose / target
  [2:4:0.0] Vigyan Jyoti | Meritorious girls of Classes 9-12 into STEM – addresses women's under-representation (APPSC Lecturers 2023)
  [2:4:0.1] WISE-KIRAN | Women in S&T R&D; Women Scientists Scheme for career-break women
  [2:4:0.2] GATI | Gender Advancement for Transforming Institutions
  [2:4:0.3] INSPIRE | Innovation in Science Pursuit for Inspired Research – school awards to PhD fellowships and faculty posts
  [2:4:0.4] SATHI | Sophisticated Analytical and Technical Help Institutes – shared high-end analytical instruments (2020)
  [2:4:0.5] SAIF | Regional sophisticated instrument facilities
  [2:4:0.6] FIST | Upgrades S&T infrastructure in universities
  [2:4:0.7] PURSE | Promotion of University Research and Scientific Excellence – grants based on publications
[2:4:1] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 1 p.40 (DST, INSPIRE); app notes 5:2 (sub 5)
## SUB 5 | Andhra Pradesh – S&T Administration
[2:5:0] - **APCOST** = AP Council of Science and Technology, under AP's **Environment, Forests, Science and Technology (EFS&T) Department**.
[2:5:1] - **State symbols (2018):** notified by the **EFS&T Department**, four years after bifurcation – not General Administration.
[2:5:2] - **State Councils of S&T** (such as APCOST): frame state S&T policy, give research grants and popularise science.
[2:5:3] - **Jul 2026:** DST-STI Hub at **SPMVV, Tirupati** – sericulture, agriculture, livelihoods for SC/ST women (3 years).
[2:5:4] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:2 (sub 7)
## SUB 6 | GERD Targets and Advisory Bodies
[2:6:0] TABLE head: Policy | GERD target
  [2:6:0.0] SPR 1958, TPS 1983 | No numeric target
  [2:6:0.1] STP 2003 | 2% of GDP by end of Tenth Plan
  [2:6:0.2] STI 2013 | 2%, private matching public
  [2:6:0.3] STIP 2020 | Double GERD every 5 years
[2:6:1] - **Reality:** **0.84% of GDP in FY 2023-24**, a 15-year high (earlier ~0.64-0.65%); the 2% goal missed under every policy.
[2:6:2] - **24 May 2026** recommendations: GERD 2% in 4-5 years via private sector; **NISPG**; single fellowship **"Vigyan Nidhi"**.
[2:6:3] TABLE head: Body | Fact
  [2:6:3.0] PSA | Office 1999; first PSA A.P.J. Abdul Kalam
  [2:6:3.1] PM-STIAC | 2018; chaired by the PSA
  [2:6:3.2] DST / DSIR / DBT | 1971 / 1985 (parent of CSIR, 1942) / 1986
  [2:6:3.3] ANRF | ANRF Act 2023; PM = President; S&T and Education Ministers = Vice-Presidents; subsumed SERB
[2:6:4] - **ANRF:** **Rs 50,000 crore (2023-28)**, about Rs 36,000 crore from non-government sources.
[2:6:5] - **RDI Fund:** **Rs 1 lakh crore** for private R&D, approved **1 July 2025**, via ANRF; **DST nodal**.
[2:6:6] - **NITI Aayog:** nodal for the GII rank; publishes the **India Innovation Index**; hosts AIM.
[2:6:7] - **DRDO** (1958) was formed by merging the **TDE, DTDP and the Defence Science Organisation**.
[2:6:8] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 1 p.40-41; PMF Lens Aug 2026 (pmf-lens-2026-08-02) p.88; app notes 5:2 (sub 2, 3)

=== NOTES 5:3 | R&d Spending in India - Gerd, Innovation Indices, Patents Ecosystem
## SUB 0 | Innovation Rankings – GII, NRI, India Innovation Index
[3:0:0] # Global Innovation Index (GII)
[3:0:1] - Published by **WIPO** (est. **1967**, **Geneva**, UN specialised agency; 1883/1886 Paris/Berne conventions, 1893 = predecessor bureau BIRPI).
[3:0:2] - **7 pillars:** 5 input (institutions, human capital and research, infrastructure, market sophistication, business sophistication) + 2 output (knowledge-technology, creative).
[3:0:3] TABLE head: Year | India's rank
  [3:0:3.0] 2015 | 81
  [3:0:3.1] 2016 | 66
  [3:0:3.2] 2017 | 60
  [3:0:3.3] 2019 | 52
  [3:0:3.4] 2022 | 40
  [3:0:3.5] 2023 | 40 (retained)
  [3:0:3.6] 2024 | 39
  [3:0:3.7] 2025 | 38 of 139
[3:0:4] - **DST report 2022:** India **3rd** in scientific publications (NSF database), in PhDs awarded and in number of start-ups – but **40th** in the GII (TREI-RB 2023).
[3:0:5] # Other indices (APPSC G1 2024 statements – all four correct)
[3:0:6] - **WIPO Report 2022:** India **7th** in **resident** patent filing activity (later **6th** in both resident and non-resident filing, WIPO 2023).
[3:0:7] - **Network Readiness Index 2023:** India **60th**, up from **79th (2019)**; NRI 2024 = **49th**.
[3:0:8] - **International IP Index 2024** (US Chamber, 12th edition): India **42nd of 55**.
[3:0:9] # India Innovation Index (NITI Aayog)
[3:0:10] - Publisher **NITI Aayog** (with Institute for Competitiveness); aim – rank States/UTs to lift the GII rank.
[3:0:11] - **2019 (first edition): Karnataka 1st** among major states (APPSC DAO).
[3:0:12] TABLE head: Category (2021, 3rd edition, released 21 July 2022) | Leader
  [3:0:12.0] Major States | Karnataka
  [3:0:12.1] North-East and Hill States | Manipur
  [3:0:12.2] UTs and City States | Chandigarh
[3:0:13] - **AP:** **9th** among Major States (2021, score 13.32); Telangana 2nd.
[3:0:14] - **State R&D policy:** **Karnataka** released India's first State Research, Development and Innovation Policy (Bengaluru Tech Summit, Nov 2022).
[3:0:15] ~~ Sources: DST Year End Review 2023 (dst.gov.in/node/7484, as quoted in search results); PMF S&T Part 8 (pmf-science-tech-08) p.78 (WIPO 1967, Geneva); NextGen Indian Economy Part 1 p.40; app notes 5:3 (sub 3, 4)
[3:0:16] - **Reuters World's Most Innovative Universities 2018:** **Stanford University** 1st (ahead of MIT and Harvard).
## SUB 1 | Patents, Copyright and IPR Law
[3:1:0] TABLE head: Act / policy | Provision
  [3:1:0.0] Patents Act, 1970 (amended 2005 for TRIPS) | Protection 20 years from filing; novel, inventive, industrially applicable
  [3:1:0.1] Copyright Act, 1957 | Published book: lifetime of the author + 60 years
  [3:1:0.2] Trade Marks Act, 1999 | 10 years, renewable
  [3:1:0.3] National IPR Policy, 2016 | "Creative India; Innovative India"
  [3:1:0.4] SIPP 2016 | Free start-up IP filing facilitation
[3:1:1] - **Not patentable in India:** **plants and animals** (except micro-organisms), **plant varieties** and **essentially biological processes** for producing plants or seeds (Sec. 3(j)); plant varieties are protected instead by the **PPV&FR Act, 2001**.
[3:1:2] - **IPAB** (Intellectual Property Appellate Board): set up **15 Sept 2003**; heard patent appeals from 2 April 2007; **abolished in April 2021** (Tribunals Reforms Ordinance/Act 2021) – appeals now go to the **High Courts**.
[3:1:3] - **CSIR** = the Indian institution with the most patents – it has filed more patents than all individual Indian patentees put together (APPSC G2 2003).
[3:1:4] - **Resident vs non-resident filings:** in **January-March 2022**, Indian applicants filed **10,706** applications against **9,090** by foreign applicants – first time in 11 years.
[3:1:5] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 8 (pmf-science-tech-08) p.69-74 (patents, copyright, what cannot be patented); NextGen Indian Economy Part 1 p.40 (IPAB set-up); IPAB abolition 2021: general knowledge, not checked; app notes 5:3 (sub 5)
## SUB 2 | Researchers, Publications and Research Institutions
[3:2:0] TABLE head: Indicator | India
  [3:2:0.0] Researchers | 262 FTE per million
  [3:2:0.1] Scientific publications | 3rd globally (nanoscience also 3rd, after China, USA)
  [3:2:0.2] Patent filing | 6th (WIPO 2023)
  [3:2:0.3] PhDs awarded (2021-22) | 32,588 (most in Science 7,408, then Engineering 6,270)
  [3:2:0.4] PhD enrolment (2021-22) | 2.12 lakh (+81.2% from 1.17 lakh in 2014-15)
[3:2:1] - **SCImago Institutions Ranking 2017:** **CSIR 9th** among 1,207 government (public research) institutions of the world; 75th overall (12th in 2016).
[3:2:2] - **ISSN** = **International Standard Serial Number** – an **8-digit** code for serial publications (journals, magazines); books carry the ISBN.
[3:2:3] - **AP:** **IISER Tirupati** 20th among Indian institutions in Nature Index 2026; **IIITDM Kurnool** = Institute of National Importance.
[3:2:4] ~~ Sources: AffairsCloud, "CSIR ranked 9th Public Research Institution of the World" (2 Sept 2017); general knowledge, not checked (ISSN); app notes 5:3 (sub 2)
## SUB 3 | GERD and Who Spends on R&D
[3:3:0] TABLE head: Country | GERD (% of GDP)
  [3:3:0.0] India | 0.84% (FY 2023-24) – 15-year high, ending a decade-long fall
  [3:3:0.1] USA | about 3.5%
  [3:3:0.2] China | about 2.4%
  [3:3:0.3] Israel | about 6.3% (2023) – world's highest (PMF Lens gives ~5%)
  [3:3:0.4] South Korea | about 5% (2023) – 2nd
[3:3:1] - **Earlier:** **0.64-0.65%** (2020-21). National target: **2% of GDP**.
[3:3:2] - **Total R&D funding:** Rs 1.33 lakh crore (FY20) → **Rs 2.45 lakh crore** (FY24).
[3:3:3] - **FY 2023-24:** private industry share **51.8%** – passed public funding **for the first time**; 2020-21 private only **~36%** (USA ~70%, South Korea ~76%).
[3:3:4] - **Agency shares of central R&D (2020-21):** **DOS 18.4%**, ICAR 12.4%, DAE 11.4%, CSIR 8.2%, DST 6.8%; **DRDO** long the largest (31.6% in 2017-18).
[3:3:5] - **Why private R&D lags:** risk aversion, weak lab-industry links, **shortage of deep-tech capital**.
[3:3:6] ~~ Sources: PMF Lens Aug 2026 (pmf-lens-2026-08-02) p.88; PMF S&T Part 1 p.43-44; app notes 5:3 (sub 0, 1)
## SUB 4 | Start-ups and R&D Funding Reforms
[3:4:0] - **Startup India – 16 January 2016:** Fund of Funds Rs 10,000 crore, **3-year tax exemption**, exit in **90 days** (not 30), self-certification.
[3:4:1] - India = **3rd-largest start-up hub**; **1.59 lakh** DPIIT-recognised start-ups (Sep 2025); unicorns: India **4th with 61** (Hurun Global Unicorn Index, Jun 2026).
[3:4:2] - **Support:** **AIM** (Atal Tinkering Labs, incubators); **iDEX** with India-Korea **KIND-X**; **MANAGE, Hyderabad** – agri-start-up incubation.
[3:4:3] - **ANRF Act 2023:** funds **basic and applied** research; ~80% of funds went to IITs (Aug 2026 critique).
[3:4:4] - **RDI Scheme – 1 July 2025:** **Rs 1 lakh crore**; custodian **ANRF**; private R&D in AI, quantum, biotech, energy.
[3:4:5] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:3 (sub 6, 7)

## SUB 3 | Polar and Earth-Science Research
[4:3:0] - **Antarctica:** **Dakshin Gangotri** (1983-84, first station, now a supply base) → **Maitri** (1989, second) → **Bharati** (2012, **third**).
[4:3:1] - **Arctic:** India's first expedition **Aug 2007** led by **Dr. Rasik Ravindra** (NCAOR, Goa); station **Himadri** (2008), **Ny-Alesund, Svalbard**.
[4:3:2] - **INCOIS, Hyderabad** (1999, MoES) houses the **Indian Tsunami Early Warning Centre** (2007).
[4:3:3] - **NCESS** (National Centre for Earth Science Studies): **Thiruvananthapuram**; began as Kerala's Centre for Earth Science Studies (**1978**); under **MoES** since **2014**.
[4:3:4] - **Soil piping study:** **Kerala**, through CESS with **NDMA** funding.

## SUB 4 | Departments and Councils – CSIR, DST, DBT, DSIR, SERB
[4:4:0] TABLE head: Body | Year | Status / fact
  [4:4:0.0] CSIR | September 1942 | Autonomous body registered as a society (Societies Registration Act 1860), not statutory
  [4:4:0.1] DST | 1971 | Nodal for new S&T areas, R&D infrastructure, missions, policy
  [4:4:0.2] National Biotechnology Board | 1982 | Upgraded to DBT in 1986
  [4:4:0.3] DSIR | January 1985 | One of 3 departments of MoST; controls CSIR + 2 PSUs
  [4:4:0.4] SERB | 2008 | Science and Engineering Research Board – statutory board (SERB Act 2008)
  [4:4:0.5] BIRAC | 2012 | DBT's not-for-profit Section 8 company for biotech start-ups
  [4:4:0.6] BRIC | 2023 | Integrates 14 DBT institutions
[4:4:1] # CSIR governance
[4:4:2] - **President: Prime Minister** (ex officio); **Vice-President: Union S&T Minister**; **DG CSIR = Secretary, DSIR**.
[4:4:3] - Network: **37 national laboratories**, 39 outreach centres, 1 innovation complex, 3 units (PMF: "35+ labs").
[4:4:4] - **BRIC** chair = Union S&T Minister (DBT Secretary = ex officio DG).

[4:4:7] - **APPSC G2 2025:** CIAB is a **DBT** institute; CFTRI, CGCRI, CCMB are CSIR labs.
[4:4:8] # DST
[4:4:9] - Autonomous bodies: **Bose Institute** (Kolkata, 1917, J.C. Bose; nodal for **FAIR**), JNCASR, Raman Research Institute, **ARIES** Nainital, Wadia Institute, **BSIP** Lucknow, SCTIMST; FAIR funded 50:50 by DST and DAE.
[4:4:10] - Schemes: INSPIRE, Vigyan Jyoti, STI Hubs for SC/ST, **NM-ICPS (2018, Rs 3,660 crore)**, Nano Mission; DST nodal for the **RDI Scheme**.
[4:4:11] # DSIR and SERB
[4:4:12] - DSIR PSUs: **NRDC** and **CEL**; recognises **2,000+ in-house R&D centres** and 900+ SIROs → **income tax and customs duty exemptions**; Umbrella scheme **PRISM, PACE, BIRD-crf, A2K+**.
[4:4:13] TABLE head: SERB scheme | Purpose
  [4:4:13.0] SERB-POWER | Women researchers
  [4:4:13.1] Ramanujan Fellowship | Repatriate scientists
  [4:4:13.2] Ramalingaswami Re-entry | Life sciences (not engineering)
  [4:4:13.3] SERB Overseas Fellowship | Diaspora
  [4:4:13.4] PM Research Fellowship | Rs 70,000-80,000/month (Aug 2026)
[4:4:14] - **ANRF Act 2023** repealed the SERB Act; SERB subsumed into **ANRF**; its schemes continue.
[4:4:15] - 2026: CSIR, DAE, DoS, ICMR, ICAR allowed to bypass GeM for scientific equipment.
[4:4:16] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 1 (pmf-science-tech-01) p.40-41; app notes 5:4 (sub 0, 1, 4, 5, 6, 7)
[4:4:17] - **CSIR Director General:** **Dr N. Kalaiselvi** (Aug 2022) = the **first woman** DG of CSIR.

## SUB 1 | Gravitational Waves, LIGO-India and Mega-Science Projects
[5:1:0] # Gravitational waves
[5:1:1] - Predicted by **Einstein (1916)**; ripples in space-time from **merging black holes**, neutron-star collisions.
[5:1:2] - **LIGO** = **Laser Interferometer Gravitational-Wave Observatory** (USA); first detection **14 September 2015** (announced 11 Feb 2016); **Nobel 2017**: Weiss, Barish, Thorne.
[5:1:3] - **Second detection** announced **June 2016**: two **black holes** merging ~**1.4 billion light-years** away (not white dwarfs).
[5:1:4] - **eLISA** (evolved Laser Interferometer Space Antenna) = space mission to **detect gravitational waves** (not neutrinos).
[5:1:5] - Black-hole merger observations (UPSC 2019) = **gravitational waves detected**, not Higgs bosons.
[5:1:6] # LIGO-India
[5:1:7] - At **Aundh, Hingoli**, Maharashtra; **DAE + DST** with US **NSF**.
[5:1:8] - Lead institutions: **IPR Gandhinagar, IUCAA Pune, RRCAT Indore** (not IIST or "Kepler's Observatory").
[5:1:9] - Consortium = **IndIGO** (Indian Initiative in Gravitational-wave Observations) – the APPSC G2 2017 key said "IndLIGO", which is not the name.
[5:1:10] # Other projects and telescopes
[5:1:11] TABLE head: Project | Site | Indian lead
  [5:1:11.0] ITER | Cadarache, France | ITER-India, IPR Gandhinagar (leads both LIGO-India and ITER-India)
  [5:1:11.1] Thirty Meter Telescope | Mauna Kea, Hawaii | DST + DAE; IIA Bengaluru
  [5:1:11.2] Square Kilometre Array | South Africa, Australia | NCRA-TIFR, Pune
  [5:1:11.3] INO (neutrinos) | Bodi West Hills, Theni, Tamil Nadu | TIFR
  [5:1:11.4] MACE | Hanle, Ladakh | BARC with ECIL; highest imaging Cherenkov telescope (opened 4 Oct 2024)
  [5:1:11.5] FAIR | Darmstadt, Germany | Bose Institute; DST:DAE 50:50
[5:1:12] - **Devasthal Optical Telescope** (3.6 m, ARIES, Nainital, 2016) = **India's largest optical telescope**.
[5:1:13] - **Sampurnanand Telescope** (104 cm) at **ARIES, Nainital** (Uttarakhand – **not Ladakh**): **50 years** (1972-2022); comets, star-forming regions, clusters.
[5:1:14] - **IIA Bengaluru**: VELC payload of Aditya-L1, Himalayan Chandra Telescope (2 m, Hanle), Kodaikanal Solar Observatory.
[5:1:15] ~~ Sources: PMF Science and Technology Part 8 p.127-128; app notes 5:5 (sub 7); general knowledge for the 2016 detection and DOT size (not checked)
## SUB 2 | ICAR – Origin, Structure, Heads and News
[5:2:0] - **ICAR** = **Indian Council of Agricultural Research**; set up **16 July 1929** (ICAR Day = **16 July**) as a **society** (Societies Registration Act 1860) on the **Royal Commission on Agriculture** report; HQ New Delhi.
[5:2:1] - Under **DARE** (Dec 1973), Ministry of Agriculture; **113 institutes** (72 research institutes).
[5:2:2] - **Director General** = Secretary DARE: **Dr S. Ayyappan** (2010-16, the answer in 2015) → **Dr Mangi Lal Jat** (as of 2026).
[5:2:3] - **KVKs**: district level, **100% centrally funded**, under **11 ATARIs**; first KVK **Puducherry, 1974** (Mohan Singh Mehta committee, 1973). Sep 2026: skill centres in all KVKs by Dec 2028.
[5:2:4] # In the news
[5:2:5] - **13 Dec 2019**: ICAR–**NABARD** MoU (sustainable agriculture, climate-resilient farming).
[5:2:6] - **Sardar Patel Outstanding ICAR Institution Award 2021** (large institute): **ICAR-NAARM, Hyderabad**.
[5:2:7] - **562** high-yielding field-crop varieties released 2019-2021 (APPSC AE 2022 key).
[5:2:8] - **Lumpi-ProVacInd**: lumpy skin disease vaccine developed by **two ICAR institutes**.
[5:2:9] ~~ Sources: Praveen CA360 Jan 2026 p.36; Daily CA 2 Nov 2026 p.3; app notes 5:5 (subs 0, 4); gktoday.in (ICAR-NABARD MoU); adda247 (NAARM award, search result)
## SUB 3 | ICFRE and Forest Institutes
[5:3:0] - **ICFRE** (1986) HQ **Dehradun** (MoEFCC); **FRI** (1906, roots in the Imperial Forest Research Institute) – **Dehradun** (not Shimla, Bhopal, Nainital).
[5:3:1] - **IIFM** (1982) – **Bhopal** (not under ICFRE).

  [5:3:2.0] IWST (wood science) | Bengaluru
  [5:3:2.1] IFGTB (genetics, tree breeding) | Coimbatore
  [5:3:2.2] IFB (forest biodiversity) | Hyderabad
  [5:3:2.3] TFRI (tropical) | Jabalpur
  [5:3:2.4] HFRI (Himalayan; ex-Temperate Forest Research Centre, 1998) | Shimla
  [5:3:2.5] CSFER / FRCER (eco-rehabilitation, 1992) | Prayagraj
  [5:3:2.6] AFRI / RFRI / IFP | Jodhpur / Jorhat / Ranchi

## SUB 4 | DAE, AEC and the Department of Space
[5:4:0] # Atomic energy
[5:4:1] - **Atomic Energy Commission: 1948** (Atomic Energy Act 1948), chaired by Secretary DAE; **DAE: 1954** by Presidential Order; both DAE and DoS are under the **Prime Minister**.
[5:4:2] - **AERB** (1983) = **nuclear and radiation safety regulator** (BARC = research, NPCIL = power plants, TIFR = research).
[5:4:3] TABLE head: Unit (all under DAE) | Function
  [5:4:3.0] BARC, Trombay | Began 1954 as AEET, renamed 1967 after Homi Bhabha
  [5:4:3.1] IGCAR, Kalpakkam | Fast breeder reactor R&D
  [5:4:3.2] NPCIL | PSU (1987); builds and runs nuclear plants (23 reactors, 7,480 MW)
  [5:4:3.3] TIFR (1945, Bombay) | Start of organised nuclear research; NCRA-TIFR runs GMRT near Pune
  [5:4:3.4] Heavy Water Board (Mumbai) | Heavy water – not AMD
  [5:4:3.5] IREL (Mumbai) | Beach-sand minerals, rare earths, zircon
  [5:4:3.6] AMD (Hyderabad) | Atomic minerals exploration
  [5:4:3.7] UCIL | Uranium mining
  [5:4:3.8] HBNI | DAE deemed university (NIRF 2025: 7th research, 20th overall)
[5:4:4] - Research reactors: **APSARA** Asia's first (1956), **Dhruva** largest, **KAMINI** only U-233-fuelled.
[5:4:5] - DAE products: Bhabhatron (cobalt therapy), Nisargruna (biogas), AKRUTI (rural incubator), TAG groundnut varieties.
[5:4:6] # Space and defence
[5:4:7] - **ISRO** 15 Aug 1969 (INCOSPAR 1962, Vikram Sarabhai); **Space Commission + DoS 1972**; **ISRO HQ and DoS secretariat: Antariksh Bhavan, Bengaluru**; Chairman V. Narayanan (2026).
[5:4:8] - Arms: Antrix (Sep 1992), **NSIL (2019)**, **IN-SPACe (2020, Ahmedabad)**; NRSC Hyderabad. LVM3-M6 (24 Dec 2025): BlueBird Block-2, heaviest payload from India.
[5:4:9] - **DRDO** (1958), DRDO Bhawan, New Delhi, under **DDR&D**; Chairman Samir V. Kamat.
[5:4:10] - Mandates: ISRO – space for development; DRDO – defence systems; DAE – nuclear power, radioisotopes; ICAR – agriculture; ICMR – biomedical research. ICMR under DHR, CSIR under DSIR.
[5:4:11] - Other HQs: Indian Bureau of Mines Nagpur; CIMFR Dhanbad; NATMO Kolkata; **MIDHANI Hyderabad**.
[5:4:12] ~~ Sources: PMF Science and Technology Part 4 p.61; PMF Science and Technology Part 5 p.9-12; Daily CA 21 Oct 2026 p.4; app notes 5:5 (subs 0-3)
## SUB 5 | ICMR and Health Research Institutes
[5:5:0] - **ICMR** = **Indian Council of Medical Research**, apex biomedical research body, under **DHR**.
[5:5:1] TABLE head: Institute | Location
  [5:5:1.0] NIV (virology; ex-Virus Research Centre, Rockefeller Foundation) | Pune
  [5:5:1.1] NIN (nutrition) | Hyderabad (Tarnaka)
  [5:5:1.2] NIE (epidemiology) | Chennai
  [5:5:1.3] NIRT (tuberculosis) | Chennai (not Pune)
[5:5:2] - **Vision 2030** pillars: research capacity, **traditional medicine**, alignment with National Health Policy (not privatisation). Jun 2026: institute-led restructuring; Jul 2026: draft National Health Research Policy.
[5:5:3] ~~ Sources: PMF Science and Technology Part 3 p.52; app notes 5:5 (sub 6)

=== NOTES 5:6 | Academies and Promotion Bodies - Insa, Iasc, Nasi, Vigyan Prasar, Nif
## SUB 0 | Indian Science Congress – Sessions, Venues and Themes (most asked)
[6:0:0] - **ISCA** founded **1914** by British chemists **J.L. Simonsen and P.S. MacMahon**; HQ **Kolkata** (not New Delhi).
[6:0:1] - **First session: January 1914, Kolkata** (Asiatic Society), presided by **Sir Ashutosh Mukherjee**.
[6:0:2] - **100th (centenary) session: Kolkata, 3-7 Jan 2013** – books say ISC "completed 100 years" in **2013** (strictly 100 years from Jan 1914 = 2014).
[6:0:3] TABLE head: Session | Year / venue | Theme / note
  [6:0:3.0] 88th | 3-7 Jan 2001, New Delhi | "Food, Nutrition and Environmental Security"
  [6:0:3.1] 96th | Jan 2009, Shillong | –
  [6:0:3.2] 97th | Jan 2010, Thiruvananthapuram | –
  [6:0:3.3] 98th | Jan 2011, Chennai (SRM University) | –
  [6:0:3.4] 99th | Jan 2012, Bhubaneswar (KIIT, NISER) | "S&T for Inclusive Innovation – Role of Women"; first Women's Science Congress held alongside
  [6:0:3.5] 103rd | Jan 2016, Mysuru | "Science and Technology for Indigenous Development in India"
  [6:0:3.6] 104th | Jan 2017, Tirupati (Sri Venkateswara University) | –
  [6:0:3.7] 105th | Mar 2018, Imphal (Manipur University) | –
  [6:0:3.8] 106th | Jan 2019, Jalandhar (LPU, Phagwara) | –
  [6:0:3.9] 107th | Jan 2020, Bengaluru | –
  [6:0:3.10] 108th | Jan 2023, Nagpur (RTM Nagpur University) | "S&T for Sustainable Development with Women Empowerment"
[6:0:4] - 109th: planned at **LPU** (Jan 2024); LPU withdrew amid a **DST-ISCA dispute**.
[6:0:5] ~~ Sources: Wikipedia, Indian Science Congress Association (sessions table, fetched); app notes 5:6 (subs 0-1)
[6:0:6] - **90th Science Congress (3-7 Jan 2003, Bangalore):** hosted jointly by **ISRO and Bangalore University**.
## SUB 1 | Science Days, IISF and Vigyanika
[6:1:0] TABLE head: Day | Date | Marks
  [6:1:0.0] National Science Day | 28 February | Raman Effect (C.V. Raman, 1928); declared 1986
  [6:1:0.1] National Technology Day | 11 May | Pokhran-II (1998); declared 1999
  [6:1:0.2] National Mathematics Day | 22 December | Ramanujan's birth; declared 2012
  [6:1:0.3] Air Force Day / Navy Day / Anti-Terrorism Day | 8 October / 4 December / 21 May | (match-question distractors)
[6:1:1] - Science Day themes: 2015 "Science for Nation Building"; **2016 "Scientific Issues for Development of the Nation"** (DST release: "Make in India: S&T driven Innovations") – **not** "Science for Sustainable Development"; 2026 "Women in Science: Catalyzing Viksit Bharat".
[6:1:2] - **IISF** (India International Science Festival): begun **2015**, New Delhi; MoST + MoES + **Vijnana Bharati**. 11th IISF: **Panchkula**, 6-9 Dec 2025.
[6:1:3] - **Vigyanika** = the **International Science Literature Festival** of IISF (CSIR-NIScPR, Vigyan Prasar, Vijnana Bharati).
[6:1:4] ~~ Sources: Praveen CA360 Feb 2026 p.143; Wikipedia, National Science Day (fetched); NISCAIR press release 1 Nov 2019 (Vigyanika); app notes 5:6 (sub 6)
[6:1:5] - **IISF 2021 (7th edition):** **Panaji, Goa**, 10-13 Dec 2021 (MoST + Vijnana Bharati).
## SUB 2 | Vigyan Prasar, Outreach Bodies and the National Innovation Foundation
[6:2:0] # Science communication
[6:2:1] - **Vigyan Prasar**: autonomous body of **DST**, **1989**, New Delhi (not HRD).
[6:2:2] - **DD Science** (one-hour slot on DD National) + **India Science** (Vigyan Prasar's internet/OTT channel, not NCSM) – launched together **15 January 2019**.
[6:2:3] - **Vigyan Dhara**: DST umbrella scheme merging 3 schemes – capacity building; R&D; innovation and technology deployment (Aug 2024).
[6:2:4] TABLE head: Channel | Run by
  [6:2:4.0] Gyan Darshan | 26 Jan 2000; MHRD with IGNOU and Prasar Bharati
  [6:2:4.1] Gyan Vani / Vyas | FM counterpart / UGC-CEC channel
  [6:2:4.2] SWAYAM Prabha | Free DTH, Ministry of Education, 2017, 32 channels
[6:2:5] # Outreach bodies
[6:2:6] TABLE head: Body | Under | Fact
  [6:2:6.0] NCSTC | DST | National Children's Science Congress; VIGYANOTSAV
  [6:2:6.1] NCSM | Ministry of Culture (not DST) | 1978, HQ Kolkata; nodal for SPoCS
  [6:2:6.2] Vigyan Rail | DST (not CSIR) | 2003-04
  [6:2:6.3] Science Express | DST + Vikram A. Sarabhai Community Science Centre | First flagged off 2007
  [6:2:6.4] Rashtriya Avishkar Abhiyan | Ministry of HRD/Education | 2015
  [6:2:6.5] ICMR-SHINE | ICMR | Classes 9-12
[6:2:7] - **ICSSR** (social science) is **not an S&T body**, unlike DST, CSIR, DAE.
[6:2:8] - India's **first dinosaurium**: **B.M. Birla Science Centre, Hyderabad**.
[6:2:9] # National Innovation Foundation
[6:2:10] - Grew from the **Honey Bee Network** (Prof. **Anil K. Gupta**, SRISTI, IIM Ahmedabad); **NIF-India, March 2000**: **autonomous body of DST**, **Gandhinagar**.
[6:2:11] - Mandate: scout, support, spread **grassroots innovations** and traditional knowledge – **not** advanced research with foreign institutes (UPSC 2015: "a only").
[6:2:12] - IGNITE, Biennial Grassroots Innovation Awards, **Festival of Innovation (FOIN)** at **Rashtrapati Bhavan**.
[6:2:13] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:6 (subs 2-5); general knowledge for the dinosaurium (not checked)
[6:2:14] - **Rashtriya Avishkar Abhiyan** (MHRD, science and maths curiosity in school children) was launched by **Dr A.P.J. Abdul Kalam** on **9 July 2015**.
## SUB 3 | Science Academies – NASI, IASc, INSA
[6:3:0] TABLE head: Body | Year | Founder / origin | HQ
  [6:3:0.0] NASI | 1930 | Meghnad Saha (as UP Academy of Sciences); oldest | Prayagraj
  [6:3:0.1] IASc | 1934 | C.V. Raman | Bengaluru
  [6:3:0.2] INSA | 1935 | As National Institute of Sciences of India; renamed 1970 | New Delhi
[6:3:1] - Order: NASI → IASc → INSA.
[6:3:2] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:6 (sub 0)

=== NOTES 5:7 | Programmes Promoting S&t and Innovation – Inspire, Fellowships, Atal Innovation Mission
## SUB 0 | Student–Scientist Connect and Research e-Platforms
[7:0:0] TABLE head: Programme | Partners | Objective
  [7:0:0.0] JIGYASA (Student-Scientist Connect) | CSIR + Kendriya Vidyalaya Sangathan (2017) | KV students into CSIR labs
  [7:0:0.1] Smart India Hackathon | Ministry of Education's Innovation Cell + AICTE | Students solve problems of ministries and industry
  [7:0:0.2] VIPNET | Vigyan Prasar | Network of science clubs
[7:0:1] - **Shodhganga:** the **national repository of doctoral theses and dissertations** of Indian universities, run by **INFLIBNET** (UGC) – not a Ganga research project.
[7:0:2] - **e-PG Pathshala:** MoE/UGC portal of PG e-content, hosted by the **INFLIBNET Centre, Gandhinagar** (Gujarat; the centre was earlier at Ahmedabad).
[7:0:3] > ⚠ Trap: JIGYASA = CSIR + **KVS**, not CBSE, Navodaya or Army schools; Vigyan Jyoti = Navodaya Vidyalaya Samiti.
[7:0:4] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:7 sub 5; inflibnet.ac.in (general knowledge, not checked)
[7:0:5] - **Accelerate Vigyan (AV):** scheme of **SERB (DST)** offering research internships and workshops (ABHYAAS, SAMMOHAN) to **students of universities, institutes and recognised colleges**.
## SUB 1 | Women in STEM – KIRAN, WIDUSHI, WEST
[7:1:0] TABLE head: Scheme | Body | Target group
  [7:1:0.0] WISE-KIRAN (Knowledge Involvement in Research Advancement through Nurturing; WISE = Women in Science and Engineering) | DST | Women scientists, esp. after a career break; WISE-PhD, WISE-PDF, WIDUSHI
  [7:1:0.1] WIDUSHI | DST (under WISE-KIRAN) | Senior women scientists, interdisciplinary S&T
  [7:1:0.2] Vigyan Jyoti | DST, with Navodaya Vidyalaya Samiti | Meritorious girls, Classes IX–XII
  [7:1:0.3] CURIE | DST | 22 women PG colleges – research facilities
  [7:1:0.4] GATI (Gender Advancement for Transforming Institutions) | DST | Institutions – gender-equity charter, modelled on UK's Athena SWAN
  [7:1:0.5] SERB-POWER | SERB (now ANRF) | Women researchers in academic/R&D institutions
  [7:1:0.6] BioCARe | DBT | Women biotech scientists after a career break
  [7:1:0.7] SEED | DST | 52 STI Hubs for SC/ST communities
[7:1:1] - **KIRAN** is the GoI initiative for innovation and research **especially among women** – not Jan Dhan, Digital India or Make in India (TSPSC 2023).
[7:1:2] - **WEST (Women in Engineering, Science & Technology):** launched **September 2022** by the **Office of the Principal Scientific Adviser** as a new initiative **of I-STEM**; helps women with STEM backgrounds, including after a career break.
[7:1:3] - **I-STEM** (Indian Science, Technology and Engineering facilities Map) = a **national** web portal for sharing R&D facilities (PM-STIAC mission) – **not international**.
[7:1:4] > ⚠ Trap: senior women + interdisciplinary = **WIDUSHI**; GATI targets institutions, not individuals; SERB-POWER is SERB, not DST.
[7:1:5] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:7 sub 2; pib.gov.in PRID=1857175 (WEST, 6 Sep 2022)
## SUB 2 | DST Facility and Start-up Programmes – SATHI, FIST, NIDHI
[7:2:0] TABLE head: Scheme | Full form | Purpose
  [7:2:0.0] SATHI | Sophisticated Analytical & Technical Help Institutes | Shared, professionally run instrument centres – tackle accessibility, maintenance, redundancy and duplication of costly equipment and foster collaboration across institutions and disciplines
  [7:2:0.1] FIST | Fund for Improvement of S&T Infrastructure | Upgrade S&T infrastructure in universities
  [7:2:0.2] PURSE | Promotion of University Research and Scientific Excellence | University-only research support (2009)
  [7:2:0.3] SAIF | Sophisticated Analytical Instrument Facilities | Access to costly analytical instruments
[7:2:1] - **SATHI statement-conclusion (FRO 2023):** both conclusions (access/duplication; collaboration) follow → **both i and ii**.
[7:2:2] - **NIDHI** (**National Initiative for Developing and Harnessing Innovations**, DST, 2016): covers the chain from scouting to scaling – **PRAYAS**, Entrepreneur-in-Residence, Seed Support System, Technology Business Incubators, Accelerators, Centres of Excellence; a NIDHI **research park** at **IIT Gandhinagar** (FRO 2022).
[7:2:3] TABLE head: Other start-up support | Body | Fact
  [7:2:3.0] TIDE 2.0 | MeitY, 2019 | ICT start-ups via incubators
  [7:2:3.1] Fund of Funds for Startups | SIDBI | ₹10,000 crore
  [7:2:3.2] Credit Guarantee Scheme for Startups | NCGTC | NOT a NIDHI component
[7:2:4] - **RDI Scheme** (Cabinet 1 July 2025): **₹1 lakh crore over six years** for private-sector research, development and innovation.
[7:2:5] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:7 sub 3-4; dst.gov.in SATHI (general knowledge, not checked)
[7:2:6] - **SATYAM** (Science and Technology of Yoga and Meditation, **DST**, 2015): funds research on **yoga and meditation**.
## SUB 3 | Andhra Pradesh Innovation Ecosystem
[7:3:0] - **Andhra Pradesh Innovation Society (APIS):** set up **2015**; nodal agency for start-ups under the **ITE&C Department**, headed by a CEO; began at **Sunrise Towers, Innovation Valley, Rushikonda, Visakhapatnam** (exam answer for "headquarters"); its office is now at **Mangalagiri** (Amaravati region).
[7:3:1] - AP was the **first State** with an **Innovation and Startup Policy (2014-2020)**; AP has **590 ATLs** (2024-25).
[7:3:2] - **Ratan Tata Innovation Hub (RTIH):** set up by APIS as a Section-8 company; inaugurated **20 Aug 2025**; central hub at **Mangalagiri** with spokes at Visakhapatnam, Rajahmundry, Vijayawada-Guntur, Tirupati, Anantapur.
[7:3:3] - **IIDT (International Institute of Digital Technologies):** **Tirupati**.
[7:3:4] > ⚠ Outdated angle: "APIS HQ = Visakhapatnam" is the old answer; current office Mangalagiri.
[7:3:5] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:7 sub 8; web search (startupgrantsindia.com, thehansindia.com 2017: APIS est. 2015, Rushikonda/Mangalagiri)
## SUB 4 | INSPIRE – DST Flagship (2008)
[7:4:0] # Basics
[7:4:1] - **INSPIRE** = **Innovation in Science Pursuit for Inspired Research**; flagship scheme of the **Department of Science and Technology (DST)**, launched **2008**, to draw young talent into science.
[7:4:2] - **Not** a Ministry of Education scheme and **not** a residential programme for Classes VI–X (the residential part is the INSPIRE science camp; MANAK is an award, not a course).
[7:4:3] # Components
[7:4:4] TABLE head: Component | Full form | Target
  [7:4:4.0] SEATS | Scheme for Early Attraction of Talents for Science | School students (MANAK awards, INSPIRE camps)
  [7:4:4.1] MANAK | Million Minds Augmenting National Aspiration and Knowledge | Classes VI–X, aged 10–15; ₹10,000 each
  [7:4:4.2] SHE | Scholarship for Higher Education | Top-ranked UG/PG students in natural and basic sciences
  [7:4:4.3] AORC | Assured Opportunity for Research Careers – INSPIRE Fellowship (PhD) + INSPIRE Faculty | Doctoral and post-doctoral researchers
[7:4:5] - **2025 data:** MANAK **8.54 lakh** ideas, **46,926** students given ₹10,000; INSPIRE Fellowship **653** doctoral fellowships, **65% women**.
[7:4:6] > ⚠ Trap: SHE = **Scholarship for Higher Education**, NOT "Support for Her Education" – it is not a women's scheme (APPSC FRO 2022). Statement angle: "separate components SEATS, SHE, AORC" ✓ and "DST flagship" ✓; "residential for VI–X" ✗ and "launched by Ministry of Education" ✗ → **B & C only** (TREI-RB 2023).
[7:4:7] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 1 p.40 (DST key initiatives: INSPIRE); app notes 5:7 sub 0; online-inspire.gov.in (general knowledge, not checked: camps)
## SUB 5 | Atal Innovation Mission (AIM)
[7:5:0] - **AIM** = flagship of **NITI Aayog**, set up **2016** (announced Budget 2015) to promote innovation and entrepreneurship across the country – not DST, Labour or Skill Development (UPSC 2019; APPSC Polytechnic Lecturers 2018).
[7:5:1] TABLE head: Component | Focus
  [7:5:1.0] Atal Tinkering Labs (ATLs) | Schools, grades 6–12: IoT, 3D printing, robotics; 10,000 ATLs in 700+ districts
  [7:5:1.1] Atal Incubation Centres (AICs) | Start-ups in universities/institutions
  [7:5:1.2] Atal Community Innovation Centres (ACICs) | Rural and under-served areas
  [7:5:1.3] ARISE | MSMEs
  [7:5:1.4] Atal New India Challenges | Product innovation for national challenges
[7:5:2] - **AIM 2.0 (2024):** **₹2,750 crore** till **March 2028**; **LIPI** – vernacular innovation centres in the 22 scheduled languages.
[7:5:3] - **Budget 2026-27:** ATL funding **₹500 crore → ₹3,200 crore**. **Jun 2026:** **Tripura** first State Innovation Mission under AIM 2.0; **India-France ATL Bridge** launched at **Nice**.
[7:5:4] > ⚠ Trap: "Sardar Patel / Mahatma Innovation Mission" do not exist; Kaushal Navin Lakshay is a distractor.
[7:5:5] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 1 p.41 (NITI Aayog – AIM); app notes 5:7 sub 7
## SUB 6 | Fellowships and Brain-gain Schemes
[7:6:0] TABLE head: Scheme | Awarding body | Target
  [7:6:0.0] VAJRA (Visiting Advanced Joint Research Faculty) | SERB (now ANRF) | Overseas scientists incl. NRIs/OCIs, short spells in India
  [7:6:0.1] Ramanujan Fellowship | SERB, now ANRF | Indian scientists abroad who return
  [7:6:0.2] Ramalingaswami Re-entry | DBT (not DST) | Life-science/biotech returnees only
  [7:6:0.3] J.C. Bose Fellowship (now JC Bose Grant) | SERB/ANRF | Active senior scientists
  [7:6:0.4] Swarnajayanti | DST, 1997 (50 years of Independence) | Young scientists with a proven record
  [7:6:0.5] PMRF | Ministry of Education (2018) | PhD scholars in IITs, IISc, IISERs
[7:6:1] - **Brain drain → brain gain:** **VAJRA, Ramanujan Fellowship and INSPIRE** (Group 1); the ASEAN-India Research Training Fellowship is for ASEAN researchers coming to India – not a brain-gain scheme.
[7:6:2] - ANRF schemes: PMECRG (PM Early Career Research Grant), Prime Minister Professorship, National Science Chair, N-PDF.
[7:6:3] > ⚠ Trap: PMRF is Ministry of Education and not a career-break scheme; Ramalingaswami and BioCARe are the only DBT schemes here.
[7:6:4] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:7 sub 1; PMF S&T Part 1 p.40 (SERB integrated into ANRF)

=== NOTES 5:8 | National S&t Missions – Quantum, Supercomputing, Deep Ocean, BIO-E3, Anrf
## SUB 0 | Indian Supercomputers and Computing Firsts
[8:0:0] - India's supercomputer family is **PARAM**, designed and assembled by **C-DAC, Pune** (C-DAC set up 1988) – not Shubham, SX-2, Tejas or EKA.
[8:0:1] - **PARAM 8000 (1991)** = India's **first** (first indigenously developed) supercomputer; C-DAC under **Vijay Bhatkar**, the **father of Indian supercomputing**.
[8:0:2] TABLE head: Machine | Developer / site
  [8:0:2.0] PARAM 8000 | C-DAC (1991)
  [8:0:2.1] Anupam | BARC – from 1991, parallel processing
  [8:0:2.2] Flosolver | NAL Bengaluru
  [8:0:2.3] PARAM Padma | C-DAC (2003) – first Indian system on the TOP500 list (June 2003, rank 171)
  [8:0:2.4] SAGA-220 | ISRO (2011) – about 220 TFLOPS
  [8:0:2.5] SahasraT (Cray XC40) | SERC, IISc – India's fastest in 2015-16 (ahead of Aaditya, IITM Pune)
  [8:0:2.6] PARAM Siddhi-AI | C-DAC (2020) – ranked 62nd/63rd on TOP500 (Nov 2020), India's highest then
  [8:0:2.7] Pratyush / Mihir (weather) | IITM Pune / NCMRWF Noida
[8:0:3] - **Computing firsts:** first analog computer in India designed by **Samarendra Kumar Mitra** (ISI Kolkata, 1953); **TIFRAC** (1960) = first indigenous digital computer; exam keys name **"Siddharth"** as "the first computer made in India".
[8:0:4] > ⚠ Trap: PARAM 8000 is the first; PARAM Shivay is only the first NSM machine; PARAM Padma, not Siddhi-AI, was the first in the TOP500.
[8:0:5] ~~ Sources: Praveen CA360 Aug 2026 p.59 ("the first, PARAM 8000, was launched in 1991"); app notes 5:8 sub 0; web: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supercomputing_in_India (PARAM Padma TOP500 2003; SahasraT), en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TIFRAC
## SUB 1 | National Supercomputing Mission (NSM, 2015) – Systems and Hosts
[8:1:0] - Run jointly by **DST and MeitY** (2015); implemented by **C-DAC Pune + IISc Bengaluru**; machines linked as the **National Supercomputing Grid** over the **National Knowledge Network**; **40 systems** planned.
[8:1:1] - Indigenous stack: **Rudra** server board + **Trinetra** interconnect + open-source (AlmaLinux) software.
[8:1:2] TABLE head: System | Host
  [8:1:2.0] PARAM Shivay (2019) | IIT (BHU) Varanasi – first NSM machine
  [8:1:2.1] PARAM Brahma | IISER Pune
  [8:1:2.2] PARAM Ganga | IIT Roorkee (March 2022; built by C-DAC)
  [8:1:2.3] PARAM Porul | NIT Tiruchirappalli (May 2022)
  [8:1:2.4] PARAM Pravega | IISc Bengaluru
  [8:1:2.5] PARAM Ananta | IIT Gandhinagar
  [8:1:2.6] PARAM Kamrupa | IIT Guwahati – opened by President Droupadi Murmu, Oct 2022
  [8:1:2.7] PARAM Shakti | IIT Kharagpur; 3.1 PF Rudra-based system at IIT Madras (Jan 2026)
  [8:1:2.8] PARAM Rudra | GMRT Pune, IUAC Delhi, S.N. Bose Centre Kolkata
  [8:1:2.9] PARAM Pragya | IIT Delhi (Sonipat), AI system, Aug 2026
  [8:1:2.10] AIRAWAT (AI supercomputer) | C-DAC Pune, under the national AI programme – 75th in TOP500 at ISC 2023 (Germany, May 2023)
[8:1:3] > ⚠ Pairs trap (AEE): PARAM Shivay ≠ IIT Guwahati; Pravega ≠ IIT Kanpur; Ananta ≠ IIT Mandi → correct pair **AIRAWAT – C-DAC Pune**.
[8:1:4] ~~ Sources: PMF Lens Jan 2026 (2) p.6 (PARAM Shakti, NSM 2015 by MeitY and DST); Praveen CA360 Jan 2026 p.75; Rocket CA Feb 2026 p.102-103 (AIRAWAT, PARAM Pragya); app notes 5:8 sub 1
[8:1:5] - **NSM status (31 Dec 2024):** **33 supercomputers, 32 Petaflops** at IISc, IITs, C-DAC and Tier-II/III institutions; used by **10,000+ researchers** incl. 1,700+ PhD scholars from 200+ institutions; PARAM Rudra (Sept 2024) extends access to Tier-II/III cities.
## SUB 2 | National Quantum Mission (NQM)
[8:2:0] - **Qubit** = basic unit of **quantum information**; by **superposition** it can be 0 and 1 together (bit/byte are classical).
[8:2:1] TABLE head: Item | Fact
  [8:2:1.0] Approved | Union Cabinet, 19 April 2023
  [8:2:1.1] Outlay | ₹6,003.65 crore
  [8:2:1.2] Period | 2023-24 to 2030-31 (8 years)
  [8:2:1.3] Nodal | DST
[8:2:2] - Targets: computers of **50–1000 physical qubits**; satellite QKD over **2000 km**; magnetometers and atomic clocks.
[8:2:3] TABLE head: T-Hub | Host
  [8:2:3.0] Quantum Computing | IISc Bengaluru
  [8:2:3.1] Quantum Communication | IIT Madras (with C-DOT)
  [8:2:3.2] Quantum Sensing and Metrology | IIT Bombay
  [8:2:3.3] Quantum Materials and Devices | IIT Delhi
[8:2:4] - Start-ups backed: **QNu Labs** (QKD), QpiAI. **14 April = World Quantum Day**.
[8:2:5] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 7 p.75-76 (NQM); daily CA 3 Nov 2026 p.6 and Praveen CA360 Aug 2026 p.32 (₹6,003.65 crore, 2023-24 to 2030-31); app notes 5:8 sub 2
## SUB 3 | Deep Ocean Mission
[8:3:0] - Nodal **Ministry of Earth Sciences**; Cabinet **June 2021**, **₹4,077 crore** for five years; six components.
[8:3:1] - **Samudrayaan** = sub-project (India's first crewed deep-ocean mission); **Matsya 6000** = crewed submersible built by **NIOT Chennai**, **3 people to 6,000 m**; **Varaha** = deep-sea mining machine trialled at 5,270 m.
[8:3:2] - **Components:** deep-sea mining and manned submersible; ocean climate change advisory services; deep-sea biodiversity; deep ocean survey; **energy and freshwater from the ocean** (OTEC-powered desalination); **Advanced Marine Station for Ocean Biology**.
[8:3:3] ~~ Sources: app notes 5:8 sub 3
[8:3:4] - **ORV Sagar Kanya** = India's **oceanographic research vessel**, bought from West Germany (commissioned 1983).
[8:3:5] - **DSV Limiting Factor** (crewed submersible of the Five Deeps Expedition, able to reach any ocean depth) was carried by the support ship **DSSV Pressure Drop**.
## SUB 4 | BIO-E3, ANRF and Other Missions
[8:4:0] - **Bio-E3 = Biotechnology for Economy, Environment and Employment**; Cabinet **24 Aug 2024**; steered by **DBT**; six sectors – bio-based chemicals and enzymes, **smart proteins**, precision biotherapeutics, **climate-resilient agriculture**, carbon capture, **marine and space research**; infrastructure = **biomanufacturing hubs, Bio-AI hubs, biofoundries** (not Technology Innovation Hubs); bioeconomy **over US$165 billion** (2024); Budget 2026-27 **Biopharma SHAKTI** ₹10,000 crore over 5 years.
[8:4:1] - **ANRF:** created by the **ANRF Act, 2023**; **subsumed SERB**; **Prime Minister** = ex officio President of its Governing Board; custodian of the **₹1 lakh crore RDI Fund** via a **two-tier Special Purpose Fund**.
[8:4:2] TABLE head: Mission | Ministry | Year | Outlay / fact
  [8:4:2.0] IndiaAI Mission | MeitY | 2024 | ₹10,371.92 crore; 10,000+ GPUs
  [8:4:2.1] Semicon India | MeitY | – | ₹76,000 crore
  [8:4:2.2] NM-ICPS | DST | Cabinet 6 Dec 2018 | ₹3,660 crore; 25 Technology Innovation Hubs (Section-8 cos.); IISER Tirupati TIH = Positioning and Precision Technologies
  [8:4:2.3] NQM | DST | 2023 | ₹6,003.65 crore
  [8:4:2.4] National Geospatial Mission | DST (Survey of India) | Budget 2025-26 | –
[8:4:3] > ⚠ Trap: NQM is DST, not MeitY; Bio-E3's third E is Employment, not Energy.
[8:4:4] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 1 p.40 (DBT – BioE3; SERB integrated into ANRF Act 2023); app notes 5:8 sub 4-6, 5:7 sub 6
## SUB 5 | Andhra Pradesh Quantum Push – Amaravati Quantum Valley
[8:5:0] - **Andhra Pradesh** = **first State** with its own **quantum computing policy**; foundation stone at **Uddandarayunipalem**.
[8:5:1] - Incentive: **100% exemption from stamp duty and registration fees** for tech firms; plan to train 4.5 million people in AI and quantum.
[8:5:2] - **14 April 2026** (World Quantum Day): India's first **Quantum Reference Facility** test beds – **1S at SRM University-AP**, **1Q at Medha Towers, Gannavaram**.
[8:5:3] - MoUs (Feb 2026): **IBM to train 1 lakh youth**; **NIELIT Quantum and AI campus** classes from Sept 2026 at **Acharya Nagarjuna University, Guntur**.
[8:5:4] - PMF: AP announced India's first **133-qubit** quantum computer at Amaravati (April 2026).
[8:5:5] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 7 p.76; app notes 5:8 sub 7

=== NOTES 5:9 | Prominent Indian Scientists – Raman, J.c. Bose, S.n. Bose, Ramanujan, Saha, Bhabha
## SUB 0 | World Scientists – Discoveries, Laws and Titles
[9:0:0] TABLE head: Discovery / law | Scientist
  [9:0:0.0] Radioactivity (1896) | Henri Becquerel (France); Nobel Physics 1903 shared with Pierre and Marie Curie for radioactivity
  [9:0:0.1] Electron (1897) | J.J. Thomson (Cavendish Laboratory)
  [9:0:0.2] Atomic nucleus (1911), proton (1919) | Ernest Rutherford – Father of Nuclear Physics
  [9:0:0.3] Neutron (1932) | James Chadwick
  [9:0:0.4] Positron (1932) | Carl D. Anderson
  [9:0:0.5] Model of atomic structure | Rutherford and Bohr (Bohr explained the hydrogen spectrum; Danish)
  [9:0:0.6] Photoelectric effect | Discovered by Hertz (1887); explained by Einstein (Nobel 1921)
  [9:0:0.7] Theory of relativity | Einstein – special 1905 (time as the fourth dimension), general 1915; played the violin; element einsteinium named after him; moved Germany → Switzerland → USA, at IAS Princeton
  [9:0:0.8] Universal law of gravitation (1687), third law (action = reaction) | Isaac Newton
  [9:0:0.9] First measurement of G | Henry Cavendish – "man who weighed the Earth"
  [9:0:0.10] First two laws of thermodynamics; absolute zero –273.15 °C | William Thomson (Lord Kelvin) – Kelvin scale
  [9:0:0.11] Right-hand thumb rule (field round a current) | Maxwell (Fleming gave the left-/right-hand rules for motors and generators)
  [9:0:0.12] Quantum theory | Max Planck – Father of Quantum Theory
  [9:0:0.13] First nuclear reactor (Chicago Pile-1, 1942) | Enrico Fermi – Architect of the Nuclear Age; Italy → USA
  [9:0:0.14] First atomic bomb (Manhattan Project) | J. Robert Oppenheimer – Father of the atomic bomb
  [9:0:0.15] Father of Modern Chemistry; named oxygen (1778) and hydrogen | Antoine Lavoisier (Priestley and Scheele isolated oxygen first)
  [9:0:0.16] Father of Electricity / unified electromagnetism | Faraday / Maxwell
  [9:0:0.17] Father of Atomic Physics | Niels Bohr – Denmark, Copenhagen (not Germany)
  [9:0:0.18] Father of Experimental Physics / Classical Mechanics / Modern Physics | Galileo / Newton / Einstein
  [9:0:0.19] Smallest particle (of those asked) | Quark – constituent of protons and neutrons
[9:0:1] > ⚠ Trap: Rutherford found the nucleus and proton, Chadwick the neutron, Thomson the electron; Becquerel (not Curie) discovered radioactivity; Newton framed gravitation but Cavendish measured G.
[9:0:2] ~~ Sources: Rocket Physics p.2, p.6, p.9, p.17, p.21-22, p.91; Rocket Chemistry p.6, p.8 (Lavoisier, oxygen); app notes 5:9 sub 9; general knowledge, not checked (right-hand thumb rule, Kelvin)
## SUB 1 | C.V. Raman and National Science Day
[9:1:0] - Born **7 November 1888**; worked at **IACS, Kolkata**; **Raman Effect** discovered **28 February 1928** → **National Science Day, 28 February** (marks the discovery, not his birthday or a satellite launch).
[9:1:1] - **Raman Effect:** when light passes through a **transparent** material (any transparent medium – solid, liquid or gas), a small fraction is **scattered with a change in wavelength**.
[9:1:2] - **Raman spectroscopy** identifies molecular structure – the robust tool to **characterise graphene**.
[9:1:3] - **Sky is blue** because of **scattering** (Rayleigh scattering of shorter wavelengths) – not absorption, reflection or dispersion.
[9:1:4] - Discovered the Effect at **IACS, Kolkata**; first Indian director of **IISc**; first to study the harmonic nature of the **tabla and mridangam**. He did **not** found the Saha Institute.
[9:1:5] - **Honours:** knighted **1929**, **Nobel Physics 1930** (first Asian science Nobel), **Bharat Ratna 1954**; "Father of Indian Physics".
[9:1:6] - Founded the **Indian Academy of Sciences (1934)** and the **Raman Research Institute, Bengaluru (1948)** – now an autonomous institute under **DST**.
[9:1:7] > ⚠ Trap: 28 Feb = Science Day (Raman); 22 Dec = Mathematics Day (Ramanujan); 11 May = Technology Day.
[9:1:8] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 1 p.36 (Raman Effect, Raman spectroscopy); Rocket Physics p.6, p.9, p.27 (blue sky – Rayleigh scattering); app notes 5:9 sub 1
## SUB 2 | Homi J. Bhabha and the Nuclear Institutions
[9:2:0] - **Father of India's Nuclear / Atomic Energy Programme** (also "Father of Indian Nuclear Science"); designed the **three-stage** programme (thorium).
[9:2:1] - Founded **TIFR, Mumbai (1945)**; first chairman of the **Atomic Energy Commission (1948)**; set up **AEET Trombay (1954)**, renamed **BARC** in **1967** after his death (Mont Blanc air crash, **1966**); Apsara reactor 1956.
[9:2:2] - **Five-Year Plan angle:** exam keys place TIFR and the Atomic Energy Commission under the **Second Plan (1956-61)** (AEC reconstituted with full powers in 1958); strictly TIFR dates from 1945 and the AEC from 1948.
[9:2:3] - **Homi Bhabha Award** – for **nuclear energy**. **Bhabha Kavach** – BARC's lightweight bullet-proof jacket (2018) for the forces.
[9:2:4] - **Bhabha scattering** (electron-positron); **cascade theory** of cosmic-ray showers; **Adams Prize 1942**, **FRS 1941**, **Padma Bhushan 1954**.
[9:2:5] - Three-stage programme is **thorium-based** (not uranium-based); Bhabha was **President of the first UN Conference on the Peaceful Uses of Atomic Energy, Geneva, 1955**.
[9:2:6] - BARC was **not** founded as BARC in 1945: TIFR = 1945, AEET = 1954, BARC name = 1967.
[9:2:7] > ⚠ Trap: not Sarabhai (space), Raja Ramanna, Satish Dhawan or Bhatnagar.
[9:2:8] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 1 p.37; PMF S&T Part 4 p.61 (TIFR 1945, AEC 1948, AEET 1954; it says renamed 1966, Part 5 BARC entry says 1967); Rocket Physics p.9, p.22; web: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five-Year_Plans_of_India and study-guide summaries (Second Plan); app notes 5:9 sub 6
## SUB 3 | Inventions, Books and Eponyms
[9:3:0] TABLE head: Invention / book | Person
  [9:3:0.0] Telescope (improved, 1609) | Galileo Galilei (Lippershey 1608)
  [9:3:0.1] Electric light bulb | Thomas Alva Edison
  [9:3:0.2] Telephone | Alexander Graham Bell
  [9:3:0.3] Cathode ray tube | Karl Ferdinand Braun
  [9:3:0.4] Ball-point pen | László (Lazo) Bíró
  [9:3:0.5] 'On the Origin of Species' (1859) | Charles Darwin
  [9:3:0.6] 'Liber Abaci' (1202) | Leonardo Fibonacci
  [9:3:0.7] Vitamin (named) / EEG / Jarvik-7 artificial heart | Casimir Funk / Hans Berger / Robert Jarvik
  [9:3:0.8] Hybridoma (monoclonal antibodies) / Biorock reef | Köhler and Milstein / Wolf Hilbertz
[9:3:1] - **Match (Group 2):** Galileo – telescope, Edison – bulb, Bell – telephone, Braun – CRT → **a-III, b-IV, c-I, d-II**.
[9:3:2] - **First human clone claim** – baby girl **'Eve'** (26 Dec 2002), by the company **Clonaid** (claim never verified). **World's first molecular ("atomic") robot** – **University of Manchester** (2017).
[9:3:3] ~~ Sources: Rocket Physics p.2, p.17; PMF General Science Part 4 p.39 (Origin of Species); app notes 5:9 sub 9; general knowledge, not checked (Biro, Fibonacci, Clonaid, Manchester robot)
## SUB 4 | Ramanujan, S.N. Bose and Meghnad Saha
[9:4:0] - **Srinivasa Ramanujan** (1887–1920): born 22 Dec 1887, Erode; worked with **G.H. Hardy** at Cambridge; first Indian **Fellow of Trinity College, Cambridge** (1918) and FRS; **1729** = Hardy-Ramanujan number; **22 Dec = National Mathematics Day**; biopic **"The Man Who Knew Infinity"** (2015, from Robert Kanigel's book).
[9:4:1] - **Ramanujan's work:** number theory, partitions, infinite series, **mock theta functions**; **2012 = National Mathematical Year** (125th birth anniversary).
[9:4:2] - **S.N. Bose:** paper **"Planck's Law and the Hypothesis of Light Quanta" (1924)**, sent to **Einstein**, who **translated it into German** → **Bose-Einstein statistics** (quantum statistics); particles called **bosons** – name given by **Paul Dirac**; **Padma Vibhushan 1954**, **FRS 1958**; Tagore dedicated *Visva-Parichay* to him.
[9:4:3] - **Meghnad Saha:** **thermal ionisation** theory (1920-21) → **Saha ionisation equation** for stellar spectra; Damodar Valley plan; founded **NASI (1930)**; **Saha Institute of Nuclear Physics** is in **Kolkata**; elected to the **Lok Sabha 1952**; devised an instrument to measure the **weight and pressure of solar rays**; FRS 1927.
[9:4:4] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 1 p.35-36; app notes 5:9 sub 3-5
## SUB 5 | Other Indian Scientists, Women Firsts and Institutions
[9:5:0] TABLE head: Scientist | Known for
  [9:5:0.0] Kamala Sohonie | First Indian woman PhD in a science discipline (Cambridge, 1939)
  [9:5:0.1] Anandibai Joshi | First Indian woman doctor of Western medicine (1886)
  [9:5:0.2] Janaki Ammal | Plant breeder (sugarcane hybrids)
  [9:5:0.3] Asima Chatterjee | First woman D.Sc. from an Indian university (1944)
  [9:5:0.4] G.S. Khush | 'Super rice' – chief rice breeder at IRRI (Philippines)
  [9:5:0.5] Salim Ali | "Bird Man of India" (ornithologist)
  [9:5:0.6] Prafulla Chandra Ray / Birbal Sahni / M. Visvesvaraya | Chemistry / palaeobotany / engineering, Diwan of Mysore 1912-19
[9:5:1] TABLE head: Institution | Fact
  [9:5:1.0] IISc Bangalore | 1909, through the efforts of Jamsetji Tata
  [9:5:1.1] TIFR | Mumbai, 1945 (Bhabha)
  [9:5:1.2] IACS Kolkata | 1876, Mahendra Lal Sircar – Raman, J.C. Bose, S.N. Bose and Saha worked here
  [9:5:1.3] Raman Research Institute | Bengaluru, 1948
  [9:5:1.4] Saha Institute of Nuclear Physics | Kolkata
[9:5:2] - TIFR runs the **Radio Astronomy Centre, Ooty**. **1954:** Raman Bharat Ratna, Bhabha Padma Bhushan, S.N. Bose Padma Vibhushan.
[9:5:3] - **Women scientists:** Kamala Sohonie was also the **first woman admitted to IISc**; **Asima Chatterjee** developed the anti-epileptic **Ayush-56** and was the first woman General President of the Indian Science Congress (**1975**); **Anna Mani** = "Weather Woman of India" (met instruments, solar radiation, wind energy); **Janaki Ammal** – Padma Shri **1977**; **Bibha Chowdhuri** – star HD 86081 named "Bibha" (IAU, 2019).
[9:5:4] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 1 p.33 (IACS 1876, IISc 1909); app notes 5:9 sub 0, 7, 8; general knowledge, not checked (Salim Ali, Ramanujan Trinity fellowship)
## SUB 6 | J.C. Bose – Plants Have Life
[9:6:0] - **Jagadish Chandra Bose** showed that **plants have life** and respond to stimuli (light, heat, electricity); invented the **Crescograph**.
[9:6:1] - Pioneer of **radio / microwave** research (coherer, galena detector) → researched in **both physics and biology**.
[9:6:2] - Founded **Bose Institute, Kolkata (1917)**; **FRS 1920**; books *Response in the Living and Non-Living* (1902), *The Nervous Mechanism of Plants*; Bengali science fiction *Niruddesher Kahini*.
[9:6:3] > ⚠ Trap: J.C. **Bose** (plants, radio) ≠ **S.N. Bose** (bosons) ≠ J.C. Ghosh (chemist).
[9:6:4] ~~ Sources: PMF S&T Part 1 p.35; Rocket Biology p.16, p.20, p.22; app notes 5:9 sub 2

=== OWN MCQs of 5:9 (79)
[nphysics-9-15] sub0
  Who is known as the Father of Quantum Theory?
  Niels Bohr | Homi J. Bhabha | C.V. Raman | *Max Planck
  x: Max Planck is the Father of Quantum Theory; Bohr is the Father of Atomic Physics, Raman of Indian Physics and Bhabha of India's nuclear programme.
[nphysics-9-8] sub0
  The scientist Henri Becquerel was associated with which country?
  *France | Germany | Poland | Britain
  x: Becquerel was French; Marie Curie was associated with both Poland and France.

  University of Chicago | Columbia University | *Cavendish Laboratory, Cambridge | IAS, Princeton
  x: J.J. Thomson worked at the Cavendish Laboratory, Cambridge; Chicago and Columbia are linked with Fermi.

  Robert Oppenheimer | *Enrico Fermi | Ernest Rutherford | Michael Faraday
  x: Enrico Fermi is called the architect of the nuclear age; Michael Faraday is known as the father of electricity.

  x: Einstein worked at Princeton's Institute for Advanced Study; Rutherford was at the Cavendish Laboratory and Manchester, and Bohr at Copenhagen.

  Who explained the hydrogen spectrum?

  x: Bohr explained the hydrogen spectrum; Planck explained black-body radiation, Einstein the photoelectric effect and Maxwell unified electromagnetism.
